is suave as both
\mathbb{A}^{1}
and the map
\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
are suave and suaveness may be checked suave-locally on the source. We claim that it is also prim: Indeed, this would imply the claim by combining
[
31
, Lem.s 4.4.9.(ii), 4.5.11.(ii)]
. Now note that we have

\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}=\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\bigsqcup_{\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}

and hence
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is a retract of
\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
: The retraction is just given by the projection of one of the two summands onto the point. Thus, it suffices to show that
\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is prim by
[
48
, Prop. 6.17]
.

To prove this, observe that
\mathbb{P}^{1}
is even weakly cohomologically proper and hence we are reduced to showing that
*/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is prim. However, note that
f:*\rightarrow*/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is suave, hence
f_{!}1
is prim over the point by
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.16]
. As prim objects are stable under shifts and retracts, primness of
*/\mathbb{G}_{m}
would thus follow if we can show that
1
is a retract of
f_{!}1[1]
. In turn, using the calculations from the proof of
2.20
, this amounts to showing that
A
is a retract of
A\{x^{-1}\}^{\dagger}\oplus xA\{x\}^{\dagger}
as modules over
A\{x^{-1}\}^{\dagger}\oplus xA\{x\}^{\dagger}
, where
x^{i}
acts trivially on
A
for
i\neq 0
. However, this was already established in loc. cit., so we are done.
∎

In fact, we will typically use the Rees equivalence not for
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, but for the quotient
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
, where
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
is the overconvergent unit disk and
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is the overconvergent unit torus.

Corollary 2.25
.

Let
A
be any Gelfand ring. Then there is an equivalence between perfect complexes on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
over
A
and the full subcategory of
\mathcal{DF}(A)
consisting of objects
\Fil^{\bullet}M
satisfying the following two conditions:

(i)

Each
\Fil^{i}M
is a perfect complex over
A
.

(ii)

We have
\Fil^{i}M=0
for
i\gg 0
and
\Fil^{i}M\rightarrow\Fil^{i-1}M
is an isomorphism for
i\ll 0
.

Proof.

Combine the previous proposition with the proof of
2.21
, where we have shown that
\Perf(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong\Perf(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})
.
∎

To conclude the section, let us record another result that, while not directly related to the Rees equivalence, still falls into the realm of the kind of “GAGA” results for perfect complexes we have proved above.

Lemma 2.26
.

Let
A
be any Gelfand ring. Pullback along the canonical map

\GSpec A/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\rightarrow\GSpec A/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}

induces an equivalence on perfect complexes. In particular, a perfect complex on
\GSpec A/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
identifies with a perfect complex over
A
together with a single endomorphism.

Proof.

As in
[
46
, Prop. II.2.3]
, one shows that the pullback functor

\mathcal{D}(\GSpec A/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\GSpec A/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a})\cong\mathcal{D}(A[t])\;,

where the last equivalence is by Cartier duality, see
[
39
, Prop. 4.2.5.(1)]
, is fully faithful and identifies the image with the full subcategory spanned by those modules killed after tensoring with the idempotent
A[t]
-algebra
A[t]\{t^{-1}\}^{\dagger}
. Thus, using
2.3
, our task is to show that if
M
is a perfect complex over
A(*)
equipped with an endomorphism
t:M\rightarrow M
, then
M\otimes_{A[t]}A[t]\{t^{-1}\}^{\dagger}=0
.

For this, fix once and for all a complex of finite projective
A(*)
-modules representing
M
in
\mathcal{D}(A(*))
and choose integers
a\leq b
such that
M^{i}=0
whenever
i\not\in[a,b]
. Then the composite map
\sigma^{\leq b-1}M\rightarrow M\xrightarrow{t}M
, where
\sigma^{\leq\bullet}
denotes the stupid truncation, corresponds to a map
\sigma^{\leq b-1}M\rightarrow\tau^{\leq b-1}M
by adjunction and hence in particular induces a map
\sigma^{\leq b-1}M\rightarrow\sigma^{\leq b-1}M
. Moreover, the map
\sigma^{\leq b-1}M\rightarrow M\xrightarrow{t}M\rightarrow M^{b}[-b]
is nullhomotopic and hence
t
induces a map
M^{b}[-b]\rightarrow M^{b}[-b]
. Overall, we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{\leq b-1}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{b}[-b]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{\leq b-1}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{b}[-b]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

whose rows are fibre sequences and hence we may check the claim for
M^{b}[-b]
and
\sigma^{\leq b-1}M
in place of
M
. By induction, we are thus eventually reduced to the case where
M
is a finite projective
A(*)
-module.

As the assertion may also be checked after localising on
\Spec A(*)
, we may furthermore assume that
M\cong A(*)^{\oplus n}
is free. However, then the endomorphism
t
is given by an
n\times n
-matrix over
A(*)
. As
A
is bounded, each entry of this matrix has finite norm and hence the operator
t
itself has finite norm, which implies
M\otimes_{A[t]}A[t]\{t^{-1}\}^{\dagger}=0
, as desired.
∎

2.5.
p
-adic Lie groups as Gelfand stacks

Before we finally discuss de Rham and Hyodo–Kato stacks, let us quickly recall the different ways we can view
p
-adic Lie groups as Gelfand stacks.

The first such way in fact works for any group object
G
in (light) condensed anima: it simply takes
G
to the Gelfand stack
G^{\mathrm{Betti}}
, which will be a group stack by functoriality. Recall that, in the special case where
G
is a profinite group, e.g. a compact
p
-adic Lie group, the latter is just given by

G^{\mathrm{Betti}}=\GSpec C^{\mathrm{sm}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\;,

where
C^{\mathrm{sm}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denotes the algebra of locally constant
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-valued functions on
G
. Owing to the fact that representations of (locally) profinite groups where each orbit map factors through a finite (or discrete) quotient are usually called
smooth representations
, we introduce a slightly different notation for
G^{\mathrm{Betti}}
in this context.

Definition 2.27
.

Let
G
be a locally profinite group, e.g. a
p
-adic Lie group. Then we write
G^{\mathrm{sm}}
to denote the group stack
G^{\mathrm{Betti}}
.

In keeping with the notation, for any locally profinite group
G
, by
[
41
, Prop. 5.4.2]
, there is an equivalence

\mathcal{D}(*/G^{\mathrm{sm}})\cong\mathcal{D}(\Rep^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(G))

between the derived category of quasicoherent sheaves on the classifying stack
*/G^{\mathrm{sm}}
and the derived category of smooth
G
-representations on solid
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector spaces in the sense of
[
41
, §5]
.

What if we are instead interested in locally analytic representations? Following
[
41
]
, we should make the following definition.

Definition 2.28
.

Let
G
be a
p
-adic Lie group and
G_{0}\subseteq G
a compact open subgroup. We define the group stack
G^{\mathrm{la}}
as

G^{\mathrm{la}}\coloneqq\coprod_{g\in G/G_{0}}\GSpec C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\;,

where
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denotes the algebra of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-valued locally analytic functions on
G_{0}
.

Let us point out that one can show that the above definition is independent of the choice of
G_{0}
. Moreover, as before in the case of smooth representations, by
[
41
, Thm. 4.3.3]
, for any
p
-adic Lie group
G
, there is an equivalence

\mathcal{D}(*/G^{\mathrm{la}})\cong\mathcal{D}(\Rep^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(G))

between the derived categories of quasicoherent sheaves on
*/G^{\mathrm{la}}
and locally analytic
G
-representations on solid
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector spaces in the sense of
[
41
, §3]
.

Definition 2.29
.

Let
G
be a locally profinite group and
G_{0}\subseteq G
a compact open subgroup. The group stack
G^{\mathrm{cont}}
is defined as

G^{\mathrm{cont}}\coloneqq\coprod_{g\in G/G_{0}}\GSpec C^{\mathrm{cont}}(G_{0},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\;,

where
C^{\mathrm{cont}}(G_{0},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denotes the algebra of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-valued continuous functions on
G_{0}
.

Let us stress that, while one should think of quasicoherent sheaves on
*/G^{\mathrm{cont}}
as continuous
G
-representations on solid
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector spaces for a locally profinite group
G
, it is
not
true that
\mathcal{D}(*/G^{\mathrm{cont}})
embeds fully faithfully into the derived category
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}[G])
of modules over the solid group algebra
\mathbb{Q}_{p}[G]
, which is usually called the
Iwasawa algebra
of
G
.

Now letting
G
be a
p
-adic Lie group, the natural maps

C^{\mathrm{sm}}(G_{0},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{cont}}(G_{0},\mathbb{Q}_{p})

for a compact open subgroup
G_{0}
induce corresponding maps of group stacks

G^{\mathrm{cont}}\rightarrow G^{\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow G^{\mathrm{sm}}\;.

In particular, we obtain a chain of maps

*/G^{\mathrm{cont}}\xrightarrow{f}*/G^{\mathrm{la}}\xrightarrow{g}*/G^{\mathrm{sm}}

between the corresponding classifying stacks. In terms of representations, pushforward along
f
then corresponds to taking locally analytic vectors in the sense of
[
41
, §3]
while pushforward along
g
corresponds to taking Lie algebra cohomology, see
[
41
, Prop. 6.2.1]
, while the respective pullback functors just correspond to forgetful functors.

2.6.
De Rham stacks

Finally, we recall the definitions and some statements surrounding the analytic de Rham stack, which was first introduced in
[
39
]
and has recently been revisited in
[
1
]
in the setting of Gelfand stacks. For this, first recall that, in loc. cit., the authors have defined a category
\mathrm{arcStk}
of (qfd)
arc-stacks
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, which are hypersheaves on (separable qfd) perfectoid
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebras for the arc-topology introduced in
[
47
]
, and we will use the notation
\Spd\overline{A}
to refer to the arc-stack represented by a perfectoid
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra
\overline{A}
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, we can get an arc-stack denoted by
X^{\diamondsuit}
by restricting its functor of points to perfectoid rings. This yields a functor

(-)^{\diamondsuit}:\mathrm{GelfStk}\rightarrow\mathrm{arcStk}

called the
diamond
, which has a left-adjoint given by extending the assignment
\Spd\overline{A}\mapsto\GSpec\overline{A}
by colimits. While we will usually not make use of this left-adjoint directly, what we
will
use is its precomposition with
(-)^{\diamondsuit}
:

Definition 2.30
.

The postcomposition of
(-)^{\diamondsuit}:\mathrm{GelfStk}\rightarrow\mathrm{arcStk}
with its left-adjoint is called the
perfectoidisation
and denoted by

\widehat{(-)}:\mathrm{GelfStk}\rightarrow\mathrm{GelfStk}\;.

Note that, by definition, any Gelfand stack
X
receives a natural map from its perfectoidisation
\widehat{X}
. The diamond functor also has a right-adjoint.

Definition 2.31
.

The right-adjoint of the functor
(-)^{\diamondsuit}:\mathrm{GelfStk}\rightarrow\mathrm{arcStk}
is called the
analytic de Rham stack
and denoted

(-)^{\mathrm{dR}}:\mathrm{arcStk}\rightarrow\mathrm{GelfStk}\;.

We will also use the same name and notation to refer to the precomposition of this functor with
(-)^{\diamondsuit}:\mathrm{GelfStk}\rightarrow\mathrm{arcStk}
.

Again, note that, by definition, there is always a natural map
X\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{dR}}
for any Gelfand stack
X
.

Example 2.32
.

Recall from
[
1
, Ex. 4.1.9]
that there is a colimit-preserving functor

\underline{(-)}:\widehat{\Shv}(\mathrm{Pro}_{\mathbb{N}}(\mathrm{Fin}))\rightarrow\mathrm{arcStk}

given by sending a light profinite set
S
to the arc-stack
\underline{S}
whose functor of points is given by

\underline{S}(\overline{A})=\Hom_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathcal{M}(\overline{A}),S)\;,

where
\mathcal{M}(\overline{A})
denotes the Berkovich space of
\overline{A}
. By
[
1
, Ex. 5.6.8]
, the composition

(-)^{\mathrm{dR}}\circ\underline{(-)}:\widehat{\Shv}(\mathrm{Pro}_{\mathbb{N}}(\mathrm{Fin}))\rightarrow\mathrm{GelfStk}

identifies with the Betti stack functor from
2.5
.
∎

Example 2.33
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a Berkovich smooth group object in the category of
\dagger
-rigid spaces in the sense of
[
1
, Def. 4.3.6]
, see also
[
26
]
. Then
G\coloneqq\mathbb{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
naturally admits the structure of a
p
-adic Lie group and we can give a very simple alternative description of
G^{\mathrm{la}}
in terms of the analytic de Rham stack of
\mathbb{G}
. For this, first recall that there is a natural map
\underline{G}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}^{\diamondsuit}
and this induces a map
G^{\mathrm{sm}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{dR}}
upon taking de Rham stacks by the previous example. Now
G^{\mathrm{la}}
may be described by the cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\mathrm{la}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\mathrm{sm}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Indeed, any analytic function on
\mathbb{G}
yields a locally analytic function on
\mathbb{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, which defines the map
G^{\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}
. Moreover, by
[
39
, Lem. 6.2.2]
, the map
G^{\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow G^{\mathrm{sm}}
is surjective with kernel
(1\subseteq\mathbb{G})^{\dagger}
, the overconvergent neighbourhood of
1
inside
\mathbb{G}
, and the same is true for
\mathbb{G}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}^{\mathrm{dR}}
by
[
39
, Ex. 6.1.8.(2)]
, hence the diagram is indeed cartesian.
∎

To explicitly describe the functor of points of the de Rham stack of a given arc-stack, first recall from
[
1
, Prop. 4.6.4]
that any Gelfand ring admits a
!
-cover by a ring of the following form.

Definition 2.34
.

A Gelfand ring
A
is called
nilperfectoid
if its
\dagger
-reduction
\overline{A}
is perfectoid. It is moreover called
totally disconnected
if the Berkovich space
\mathcal{M}(A)=\mathcal{M}(\overline{A})
is a profinite set.

Now given any arc-stack
X
, it suffices to specify the functor of points of
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
on nilperfectoid rings
A
, and there it is given by the formula

X^{\mathrm{dR}}(A)=X(\overline{A})\;,

see
[
1
, Prop. 4.6.5]
. If
X
is a Gelfand stack, this shows that
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
is obtained by sheafifying the assignment
A\mapsto X(\overline{A})
for Gelfand rings
A
.

One of the main results of
[
1
]
is the fact that the functor

(-)^{\mathrm{dR}}:\mathrm{arcStk}\rightarrow\mathrm{GelfStk}

commutes with colimits, which is also what enables
2.32
above. Moreover, it calculates de Rham cohomology of partially proper rigid analytic spaces.

Proposition 2.35
.

For any smooth partially proper rigid space
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, there is a natural isomorphism

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR}},\mathcal{O})\cong R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X)\;,

where the right-hand side denotes usual de Rham cohomology of
X
. Moreover, there is an equivalence

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR}})\cong\{\text{vector bundles with connection on $X$}\}\;,

where the connection in the target category is always assumed to be flat.

Proof.

See
[
1
, Prop. 5.2.1, Rem. 5.2.3]
.
∎

The analytic de Rham stack also has a filtered variant. While this is usually defined as living over the quotient
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, it will be more convenient for us to use
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
as the base, where we recall that
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
denotes the overconvergent unit disk and
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is the overconvergent unit torus. Recall that we have already shown in
2.21
that
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
and
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
have equivalent categories of perfect complexes; more precisely, by
2.23
, perfect complexes on both of these stacks are equivalent to finitely filtered perfect complexes over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Let us also shortly comment on how the moduli interpretations of
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
and
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
compare. For this, recall that the latter parametrises
generalised Cartier divisors
, i.e. maps
L\rightarrow A
, where
L
is an invertible
A
-module (note that we implicitly need to use
2.3
here). A similar description holds for
A
-points of
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
.

Definition 2.36
.

Let
A
be a Gelfand ring. A
normed line bundle
over
A
is a line bundle
L
over
A
which is
!
-locally equipped with an invertible
A^{\leq 1}
-submodule
L^{\leq 1}\subseteq L
.

Remark 2.37
.

It is probably not true that one can always obtain an actual
A^{\leq 1}
-module
L^{\leq 1}
from a normed line bundle
L
over
A
, i.e. without having to
!
-localise.
∎

Definition 2.38
.

Let
A
be a Gelfand ring. A
normed generalised Cartier divisor
of norm at most
r\geq 0
is a normed line bundle
L
over
A
together with a map
L\rightarrow A
such that,
!
-locally on
A
, the composite
L^{\leq 1}\rightarrow L\rightarrow A
factors through
A^{\leq r}
.

Now it is clear from the definition that the quotient
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
exactly parametrises normed generalised Cartier divisors of norm at most
1
. Moreover, given such a divisor corresponding to a map
\GSpec A\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
, postcomposing with the map
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
exactly corresponds to forgetting the norm. We can now define the filtered version of
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
.

Definition 2.39
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Its (analytic)
Hodge-filtered de Rham stack

X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is the Gelfand stack over
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
obtained by sheafifying the assignment

X^{\mathrm{dR},+}(\GSpec A\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})\coloneqq\{\text{maps}\,\GSpec\Cone(L\otimes_{A}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\rightarrow A)\rightarrow X\}\;,

where
L\rightarrow A
is the normed generalised Cartier divisor classified by the map
\GSpec A\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
.

If
t
denotes the coordinate on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
, let us write

t:\mathcal{O}\langle-1\rangle\rightarrow\mathcal{O}

for the universal normed generalised Cartier divisor over
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
. Given any sheaf
E
on
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
for some Gelfand stack
X
, we can then obtain a filtered complex of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector spaces by taking cohomology of twists of
E
, i.e. there is a diagram

\dots\xrightarrow{t}R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+},E\langle-n-1\rangle)\xrightarrow{t}R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+},E\langle-n\rangle)\xrightarrow{t}R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+},E\langle-n+1\rangle)\xrightarrow{t}\dots\;,

and we will call this the
filtered cohomology
of
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
and write
\Fil^{\bullet}R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+},E)
. (This applies more generally to all Gelfand stacks over
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
, not just filtered de Rham stacks.)

Proposition 2.40
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then there is a natural isomorphism

\Fil^{\bullet}R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+},\mathcal{O})\cong\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Hod}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X)

between filtered cohomology of
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
and Hodge-filtered de Rham cohomology of
X
. Moreover, there is an equivalence of categories

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\{\text{filtered vector bundles with connection on $X$}\}\;,

where the filtration in the target category is always assumed to be locally by direct summands and the connection is always assumed to be flat and Griffiths transversal with respect to the filtration, i.e. it carries
\Fil^{i}E
into
\Fil^{i-1}E\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega_{X}^{1}
.

Proof.

For the first part, see
[
1
, Rem. 5.2.2]
. For the second part, we first construct a natural functor

(2.6.1)

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\rightarrow\{\text{filtered vector bundles with connection on $X$}\}\;

obtained as follows: Pulling back any vector bundle on
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
along the natural map

X\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{dR},+}

induced by the canonical map
A\rightarrow\Cone(L\otimes_{A}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\rightarrow A)
yields a filtered vector bundle
\Fil^{\bullet}E
on
X
by
2.23
. As the underlying unfiltered vector bundle arises via pullback along
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{dR}}
, it is naturally equipped with a flat connection
\nabla:E\rightarrow E\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega_{X}^{1}
by
2.35
.

To check the Griffiths transversality, we may then work locally on
X
and, in particular, assume that it admits a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
. Then the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n})^{\mathrm{dR},+}}

is cartesian as the map
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n})^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is
\dagger
-formally étale. Moreover, we have

(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n})^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;\big/\;\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle^{n}

by definition, where
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle^{n}
acts on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n}
by
t
-scaled translation, where
t:\mathcal{O}\langle-1\rangle\rightarrow\mathcal{O}
is the tautological normed generalised Cartier divisor on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
. Thus, we conclude that

X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong X\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;\big/\;\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle^{n}\;,

and now putting together the Rees equivalence from
2.23
with Cartier duality for
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
as in
2.26
, we see that any vector bundle on
X
with a flat connection and a filtration arising via pullback from
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
as above satisfies Griffiths transversality. This shows that the construction we have given above indeed yields a well-defined natural functor (
2.6.1
). Moreover, the same argument then shows that this functor is actually an equivalence in the situation where
X
is equipped with a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n}
.

This is enough to conclude: As
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is compatible with rational localisations, which one deduces by base change from
4.22
below, we may check whether (
2.6.1
) is an equivalence by localising on
X
, and this finishes the proof.
∎

The proof of the first part of the above statement uses the fact that one can explicitly describe the base change of
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
along
*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
.

Definition 2.41
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack. The (analytic)
Hodge stack

X^{\mathrm{Hod}}
of
X
is defined as

X^{\mathrm{Hod}}\coloneqq X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;.

Then the key to the proof of the first part of
2.40
is that, for a smooth partially proper rigid space
X
, we have

X^{\mathrm{Hod}}\cong(X\times*/\overline{\mathbb{T}})/\mathcal{T}_{X/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle

essentially by deformation theory, see
[
39
, Prop. 5.2.3.(2a)]
; here,
\mathcal{T}_{X/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\dagger}
denotes the overconvergent neighbourhood of the zero section inside the restriction of
\AnSpec_{X}\Sym_{X}^{\bullet}\Omega^{1}_{X/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
to the test category of Gelfand rings and the action on
X\times*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is trivial. Then one deduces by Cartier duality, see
[
39
, Thm. 4.3.13]
and
2.26
, that cohomology of
X^{\mathrm{Hod}}
computes Hodge cohomology of
X
, i.e.

\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Hod}},\mathcal{O}\langle i\rangle)\cong\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}R\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X}^{i})\;.

We also note that the base change of
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
along
*=\overline{\mathbb{T}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
recovers
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
as the normed generalised Cartier divisor
L\rightarrow A
is an isomorphism over this locus and hence
\Cone(L\otimes_{A}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\rightarrow A)
simplifies to
\overline{A}
. Slightly more generally, the same argument works over the larger locus
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
, where
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}
denotes the punctured overconvergent unit disk, and thus, using
2.22
, shows that

X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\cong X^{\mathrm{dR}}\times(0,1]\;.

2.7.
Hyodo–Kato stacks

Let us end by shortly discussion
Hyodo–Kato stacks
, which in a way also belong the circle of ideas around analytic de Rham stacks and were introduced in
[
1
]
. For this, first recall that, for any arc-stack
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, there is an arc-stack

Y_{X}^{\diamondsuit}\coloneqq X\times_{\Spd\mathbb{F}_{p}}\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}

over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, where the structure map is via projection onto the second factor and we point out that the fibre product of course has to be taken as arc-stacks
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
(!), not over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. However, let us emphasise that all the arc-stacks that will occur in this paper will be over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and arc-stacks over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
will only feature implicitly via the above definition. Let us also note that, while
Y_{X}^{\diamondsuit}
is called the “open punctured curve” over
X
in
[
1
]
, we will mostly refer to it as the
Y
-curve
of
X
.

As an arc-stack over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, the arc-stack
X
is equipped with a Frobenius automorphism and this induces a Frobenius

\varphi:Y_{X}^{\diamondsuit}\rightarrow Y_{X}^{\diamondsuit}\;,

which is an isomorphism. Recall that the
Fargues–Fontaine curve

\mathrm{FF}_{X}^{\diamondsuit}
of
X
is defined as

\mathrm{FF}_{X}^{\diamondsuit}\coloneqq Y_{X}^{\diamondsuit}/\varphi{Z}\;.

This leads to the following definition of Hyodo–Kato stacks:

Definition 2.42
.

Let
X
be an arc-stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The
Hyodo–Kato stack

X^{\mathrm{HK}}
of
X
is defined as the Gelfand stack

X^{\mathrm{HK}}\coloneqq(\mathrm{FF}_{X}^{\diamondsuit})^{\mathrm{dR}}\;.

If
X
is a Gelfand stack, then we will also write
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
for the Hyodo–Kato stack of
X^{\diamondsuit}
and simply call it the Hyodo–Kato stack of
X
.

The name of
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
is of course supposed to hint at the fact that cohomology of
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
agrees with Hyodo–Kato cohomology, which in the setting of analytic geometry was probably first introduced by Colmez–Nizioł in
[
16
]
. Recall that this is a cohomology theory for smooth partially proper rigid spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
valued in
(\varphi,N,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-modules over the maximal unramified extension
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Indeed, by one of the main theorems of
[
1
]
, this is also the kind of linear algebraic structure one obtains on cohomology of Hyodo–Kato stacks:

Theorem 2.43
.

The category
\Perf(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}})
of perfect complexes on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
is equivalent to the category of
(\varphi,N)
-modules in
\Perf(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}})
with a smooth
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-action. In particular, vector bundles on
\Vect(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}})
are
(\varphi,N,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-modules over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}
.

Proof.

See
[
1
, Thm. 7.1.1]
.
∎

Example 2.44
.

The isocrystal
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},p^{-1}\varphi)
corresponds to a line bundle on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
which we will denote by
\mathcal{O}\{1\}
. For any Gelfand stack
X
, we will also use
\mathcal{O}\{1\}
to denote the pullback of
\mathcal{O}\{1\}
along the induced map
X^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
.
∎

In order to apply the theorem above and extract
(\varphi,N,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-modules from the cohomology of the stack
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
, we should show that pushforward along
X^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
preserves perfect complexes, at least when
X
is a smooth partially proper qcqs rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Lemma 2.45
.

For any qcqs arc-stack
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, the map
X^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
is prim.

Proof.

By
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.8]
, we may pass to a prim descendable cover of the source and thus, using
[
1
, Prop. 4.7.12]
, we may pass to a finite strict closed cover of
X
and hence assume that
X
admits an étale map to
(\mathbb{A}^{n})^{\diamondsuit}
for some
n\geq 0
. By quasicompactness, this map factors through a closed disk of finite radius, which we may without loss of generality assume to have radius
1
, and after changing coordinates on the target, we may thus assume that there is an étale map
X\rightarrow(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n})^{\diamondsuit}
for some
n\geq 0
.

First observe that
X^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n})^{\mathrm{HK}}
is prim. Indeed, the map
\mathrm{FF}_{X}^{\diamondsuit}\rightarrow\mathrm{FF}_{(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n})^{\diamondsuit}}^{\diamondsuit}
is qcqs and étale and any such map induces a prim map on de Rham stacks by the proof of
[
1
, Prop. 5.1.4]
. Therefore, it remains to show that
(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n})^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
is prim, which we may reduce to the case
n=1
by compatibility of the Hyodo–Kato stack with limits.

Then recall from
[
1
, Rem. 6.2.6.(1)]
that

\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{HK}}\cong\mathbb{T}_{\infty,\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{\mathrm{HK}}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)^{\mathrm{sm}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}\cong(\mathrm{FF}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{\diamondsuit}\times_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{T}_{\infty})^{\mathrm{dR}}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)^{\mathrm{sm}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}\;,

where
\mathbb{T}_{\infty}\coloneqq\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle
denotes the perfectoid
n
-dimensional torus, the semidirect product is defined by letting
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
act on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
by multiplication,
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
acts on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
via the usual Galois action and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
acts on
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
via
\theta.T^{1/p^{m}}=[\varepsilon^{1/p^{m}}]^{\theta}T^{1/p^{m}}
, where
\varepsilon=(1,\zeta_{p},\zeta_{p^{2}},\dots)\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl},\flat}
is as usual. Moreover, we have

\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}\cong(\mathrm{FF}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{\diamondsuit})^{\mathrm{dR}}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}\;.

Now note that

\mathbb{T}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\infty}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\;,

where
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\infty}=\GSpec\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{m}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T^{\pm 1/p^{m}}\rangle_{\leq 1}
, since the
p
-th power map on
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
is an isomorphism. Thus, we see that
\mathbb{T}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{dR}}
is even weakly cohomologically proper as the same is true for
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\infty}
and
*/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
, where in the latter case we use
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
via the logarithm and
[
39
, Prop. 4.2.5.(2)]
. As also
*/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}
is prim by
[
39
, Prop. 6.2.4]
, we overall conclude that
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
is prim, as desired.
∎

Corollary 2.46
.

For any smooth partially proper qcqs rigid space
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, pushforward along
X^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
preserves perfect complexes. In particular, the pushforward of any perfect complex is naturally equipped with the structure of a
(\varphi,N,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-module in
\Perf(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}})
.

Proof.

The map
X^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
is prim by
2.45
and also suave by
[
1
, Thm. 6.3.1.(2)]
, hence pushforward preserves dualisable objects by combining
[
31
, Lem.s 4.4.9.(ii), 4.5.11.(ii)]
and then the claim follows from
2.3
. The final assertion follows using
2.43
.
∎

With the above result, it is now sensible to make the following definition.

Definition 2.47
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper qcqs rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We define the
Hyodo–Kato cohomology

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(X)
of
X
as the
(\varphi,N,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-module in
\Perf(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}})
corresponding to the pushforward of the structure sheaf along
X^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
.

According to
[
1
, Rem. 6.1.3]
, it is expected that the above definition agrees with classical definitions of Hyodo–Kato cohomology. More specifically, one should be able to deduce this from the fact that the cohomology of the Hyodo–Kato stack defines a realisation of Berkovich motives according to the recent PhD thesis of Ko Aoki, see
[
3
, Thm. 1.7]
, together with the interpretation of Hyodo–Kato cohomology via motives from
[
7
]
. While we will not use this expected relation in any of our arguments, we will still implicitly assume it when we formulate some of our theorems in more classical language or compare them to previous results.

3.
Analytic prismatisation over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}

We now recall some facts about (rational) analytic prismatisation of Gelfand stacks over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, which has been developed by Anschütz–Le Bras–Rodríguez Camargo–Scholze. From the outset, let us emphasise that, because we restrict both our geometry and coefficients to characteristic
0
, i.e. we allow Gelfand stacks over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
as inputs and our cohomology theories will have
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-coefficients, the overall situation is very simple: Basically, the rational analytic prismatisation
X{\Delta}
of a Gelfand stack
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
will just combine the Hyodo–Kato stack
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
and the stack
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
from
[
2
]
, the definition of which we will review further below.

Roughly speaking, the construction of
X{\Delta}
for a Gelfand stack
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
goes as follows. First, recall that nilperfectoids form a basis for the
!
-topology on Gelfand rings, hence it suffices to specify
X{\Delta}(A)
for nilperfectoid rings
A
. For any such nilperfectoid, we will define a derived Berkovich space
Y_{A}
, which will be a
\dagger
-thickening of the
Y
-curve
Y_{\overline{A}}
of the perfectoid
\overline{A}
equipped with a Frobenius
\varphi:Y_{A}\rightarrow Y_{A}
compatible with the one on
Y_{\overline{A}}
. Then
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
will be the moduli space of degree
1
divisors
D\subseteq Y_{A}
, where the degree
1
condition is asked after pullback to
Y_{\overline{A}}
, and
X{\Delta}
will be defined as the Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
given by

X{\Delta}(\GSpec A\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta})=\{\text{maps}\,D\rightarrow X\}\;,

where
D\subseteq Y_{A}
is the degree
1
divisor classified by the map
\GSpec A\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, the Gelfand stack
X{\Delta}
will have the following features:

(i)

There is an isomorphism
(X{\Delta})^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
.

(ii)

By virtue of (i), there is a radius map

\kappa:X{\Delta}\rightarrow(0,\infty)\;.

(iii)

There is a Frobenius
\varphi:X{\Delta}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
which is sent to multiplication by
p
under
\kappa
.

(iv)

Over
(1,\infty)
, the map
X{\Delta}\rightarrow Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
induced by (i) is an isomorphism and, in particular, Frobenius induces an isomorphism
X{\Delta}_{(1,\infty)}\xrightarrow{\cong}X{\Delta}_{(p,\infty)}
.

(v)

By (iv), there is a map
i_{\mathrm{dR}}:X^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
which factors through
X{\Delta}_{[p,p]}
and there are further copies of
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
sitting inside
X{\Delta}
at all positive Frobenius translates.

(vi)

Frobenius also induces an isomorphism
X{\Delta}_{(0,1)}\xrightarrow{\cong}X{\Delta}_{(0,p)}
.

(vii)

There is a map
i_{\mathrm{HT}}:X^{\mathrm{HT}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
which factors through
X{\Delta}_{[1,1]}
and there are further copies of
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
sitting inside
X{\Delta}
at all negative Frobenius translates; here,
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
denotes the
analytic Hodge–Tate stack
from
[
2
]
.

The situation is summarised by the schematic picture in
Fig.

1
.

\varphi

Figure 1.
A schematic picture of
X{\Delta}

3.1.
Y
-curves of nilperfectoids

Let us first recall the definition of
Y_{\overline{A}}
for
\overline{A}
perfectoid. For this, recall that
Y_{\overline{A}}^{\diamondsuit}
may be obtained as the arc-stack corresponding to the adic space

\Spa\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\overline{A})\setminus\{p[\pi^{\flat}]=0\}\;,

where
\pi\in\overline{A}
is a perfectoid pseudouniformiser. Transporting this into the world of Gelfand stacks, we set

Y_{\overline{A}}\coloneqq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{r\rightarrow 0^{+},s\rightarrow\infty}\GSpec\mathbb{B}_{[r,s]}(\overline{A})\;,

where we use the notation

\mathbb{B}_{[r,s]}(\overline{A})\coloneqq\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\overline{A})[\tfrac{1}{[\pi^{\flat}]}]\langle\tfrac{p^{s}}{[\pi^{\flat}]},\tfrac{[\pi^{\flat}]}{p^{r}}\rangle

and we emphasise that this definition depends on the choice of
\pi
even though this is not apparent from the notation on the left-hand side. By
[
1
, Cor. 4.4.10]
, the functor
\overline{A}\mapsto Y_{\overline{A}}
sends arc-hypercovers of totally disconnected perfectoids to
!
-equivalences of Gelfand stacks and hence satisfies hyperdescent for the arc-topology. In particular, we may extend it to a functor

Y_{(-)}:\mathrm{arcStk}\rightarrow\mathrm{GelfStk}\;,\hskip 8.5359ptX\mapsto Y_{X}\;.

We point out that
Y_{\overline{A}}
is always equipped with a Frobenius
\varphi:Y_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}
induced by the one on
\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\overline{A})
and that this in turn induces a Frobenius
\varphi:Y_{X}\rightarrow Y_{X}
on
Y_{X}
for any arc-stack
X
. Moreover, recall that Fontaine’s map

\theta:\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\overline{A})=W(\overline{A}^{\flat\circ})\rightarrow\overline{A}^{\circ}\;,\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{n\geq 0}[a_{n}]p^{n}\mapsto\sum_{n\geq 0}a_{n}^{\sharp}p^{n}

induces a map

\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}

via which
\GSpec\overline{A}
is a divisor in
Y_{\overline{A}}
. Consequently, for any arc-stack
X
, we obtain an analogous map
\iota:X\rightarrow Y_{X}
, which makes
X
a divisor in
Y_{X}
, where we view
X
as a Gelfand stack via the left-adjoint of the diamond functor.

Example 3.1
.

For
X=\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we obtain

Y_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\cong Y_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{cont}}\cong Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}/\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{cont}}\;.\qed

Remark 3.2
.

For any arc-stack
X
, it is indeed true that the diamond of
Y_{X}
agrees with
Y_{X}^{\diamondsuit}
as defined previously, i.e. it is isomorphic to
X\times_{\Spd\mathbb{F}_{p}}\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. However, in line with
[
1
, Rem. 6.1.7]
, let us emphasise that
Y_{X}
is
not
just obtained by applying the left-adjoint of the diamond functor to
Y_{X}^{\diamondsuit}
. One way to explain this is to point out that
\Spa\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\mathbb{C}_{p})\setminus\{p[p^{\flat}]=0\}
is not a perfectoid space, but only becomes perfectoid after a further base change along e.g.
\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\rightarrow\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}
or
\Spa\mathbb{C}_{p}\rightarrow\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}
; however,

\widehat{\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\neq\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\;,

see
[
1
, Ex. 4.5.6]
. Said differently, while the image of

Y_{X}^{\diamondsuit}=X\times_{\Spd\mathbb{F}_{p}}\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}

under the left-adjoint of the diamond functor is computed via arc-descent to perfectoids on both
X
and
\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, the definition of
Y_{X}
as above only involves arc-descent to perfectoids on
X
.
∎

Recall from above that the definition of
\mathbb{B}_{[r,s]}(\overline{A})
for a perfectoid
\overline{A}
depends on the chosen perfectoid pseudouniformiser
\pi
. However, note that, for perfectoids over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(p^{1/p^{\infty}})
, there is a somewhat canonical choice: we can take
\pi=p
. For any such perfectoid
\overline{A}
, recall that the algebras
\mathbb{B}_{[r,s]}(\overline{A})
are idempotent on
Y_{\overline{A}}
by
[
1
, Lem. 4.4.9]
and hence we obtain a canonical radius map

\kappa:Y_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow(0,\infty)

with the property that the preimage of any interval
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
is given by

\mathbb{B}_{[r,s]}^{\dagger}(\overline{A})\coloneqq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{[r,s]\subseteq[r^{\prime},s^{\prime}]}\mathbb{B}_{[r^{\prime},s^{\prime}]}(\overline{A})\;.

In fact, we can even make sense of this map when
\overline{A}
is not a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(p^{1/p^{\infty}})
-algebra: In that case, we can just define the map by descent thanks to
[
1
, Cor. 4.4.10]
; indeed, for any two choices of
p^{\flat}=(p,p^{1/p},p^{1/p^{2}},\dots)
, the corresponding elements
[p^{\flat}]
will differ by a power of
[\varepsilon]=[(1,\zeta_{p},,\zeta_{p^{2}},\dots)]
, which has norm
1
.

From this canonical radius map
\kappa:Y_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
, we also obtain a canonical definition of
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(\overline{A})
for all perfectoids
\overline{A}
: We can just define

\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(\overline{A})\coloneqq\mathcal{O}(Y_{\overline{A},[r,s]})\;,

where
Y_{\overline{A},[r,s]}
is the preimage of
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
under
\kappa
. Thus, from now on, whenever we refer to
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(\overline{A})
or the radius map
\kappa:Y_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
for a perfectoid
\overline{A}
, we will always assume that they are defined with respect to
\pi=p
. In particular, this has the consequence that the image of
\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}
is supported above
\{1\}\subseteq(0,\infty)
under the radius map.

Let us now define
Y_{A}
for arbitrary nilperfectoids
A
; in fact, we only define
Y_{A}
for totally disconnected nilperfectoids, but one could then define
Y_{A}
for all nilperfectoids (and in fact all Gelfand stacks) using the descent result from
3.6
below. For totally disconnected nilperfectoids, we first define the algebras
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)
for any
r\geq 0
and then glue their associated Gelfand stacks.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
A
be a totally disconnected nilperfectoid. For any
r\geq 0
, the Gelfand ring
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)
is defined as the pullback

(3.1.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n=0}^{r}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n=0}^{r}\overline{A}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n}\theta\circ F^{-n}}

The
Y
-curve
of
A
is defined as the Gelfand stack

Y_{A}\coloneqq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{r}\GSpec\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)\;.

Remark 3.4
.

We could also make the above definition more generally for uniformly totally disconnected perfectoid rings
A
, i.e. Gelfand rings
A
whose uniform completion
A^{u}
is totally disconnected perfectoid, by replacing
\overline{A}
by
A^{u}
. However, the total disconnectedness cannot be removed and is necessary for the descent statement in
3.6
below.
∎

\varphi
0
1/p^{3}
1/p^{2}
1/p
1
p
p^{2}
p^{3}
\infty
\GSpec\overline{A}
\GSpec\overline{A}
\GSpec\overline{A}
\GSpec A
\GSpec A
\GSpec A
\GSpec A
\kappa

Figure 2.
A schematic picture of
Y_{A}

We immediately record some structures on and properties of
Y_{A}
.

Remark 3.5
.

(a)

If
A
is perfectoid, then
Y_{A}=Y_{\overline{A}}
, so the notation is consistent. Indeed, in that case, the lower horizontal map in (
3.1.1
) is an isomorphism and hence the upper horizontal map is an isomorphism as well.

(b)

Note that the map
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)\rightarrow\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})
is a
\dagger
-nilpotent thickening for any
r\geq 0
since the same is true for the bottom row in (
3.1.1
). In particular, for any nilperfectoid
A
, the ring
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)
is indeed Gelfand. Moreover, the Gelfand stack
Y_{A}
is a derived Berkovich space in the sense of
[
1
, §4.3]
and the natural map
Y_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow Y_{A}
induces an isomorphism on underlying topological spaces.

(c)

There is a natural Frobenius morphism
\varphi:Y_{A}\rightarrow Y_{A}
induced by the Frobenius morphism of
Y_{\overline{A}}
and the left shift

\prod_{n=0}^{r}A\rightarrow\prod_{n=0}^{r-1}A\;.

(d)

Note that, by definition, there is a projection map
\theta:\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[1,1]}(A)\rightarrow A
inducing a map

\iota:\GSpec A\rightarrow Y_{A}\;.

As the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\overline{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\overline{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

commutes, there is no clash of notation and the above recovers the previously defined map
\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}
if
A
is already perfectoid. However, let us warn the reader that, contrary to what happens in the perfectoid case, the map
\iota:\GSpec A\rightarrow Y_{A}
does
not
generally cut out a divisor anymore.

(e)

The radius map
\kappa:Y_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
extends to
Y_{A}
. Indeed, as
Y_{A}
is a
\dagger
-nilpotent thickening of
Y_{\overline{A}}
, they have the same de Rham stack and hence we obtain a map

\kappa:Y_{A}\rightarrow Y_{A}^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong Y_{\overline{A}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\xrightarrow{\kappa^{\mathrm{dR}}}(0,\infty)^{\mathrm{dR}}=(0,\infty)\;,

where the last step uses the idempotency of the functor
(-)^{\mathrm{dR}}
and
2.32
. We note that
\GSpec\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)
is exactly the preimage
Y_{A,[p^{-r},p^{r}]}
of the interval
[p^{-r},p^{r}]\subseteq(0,\infty)
under the map
\kappa
. We can then furthermore define
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(A)
for any interval
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
as the ring of global functions on the preimage of
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
under
\kappa
. ∎

We have the following descent result for the association
A\mapsto Y_{A}
, which allows us to define
Y_{A}
for arbitrary nilperfectoids
A
, and in fact for all Gelfand stacks.

Lemma 3.6
.

For any
r\geq 0
, the functor
A\mapsto\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)
from totally disconnected nilperfectoids to Gelfand rings preserves
!
-equivalences. In particular, the functor
A\mapsto Y_{A}
from totally disconnected nilperfectoids to Gelfand rings preserves
!
-equivalences.

Proof.

The claim is true for the functor
A\mapsto\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})
by
[
1
, Cor. 4.4.10.(1)]
and for the functor
A\mapsto\overline{A}
by
[
1
, Lem. 4.4.3]
. In other words, for any
!
-equivalence
A\rightarrow A_{\bullet}
of totally disconnected nilperfectoids, the pro-system
(\Tot_{\leq k}\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A}_{\bullet}))_{k}
is pro-constant and equal to
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A}))
and, similarly, the pro-system
(\Tot_{\leq k}\Nil^{\dagger}(A_{\bullet}))_{k}
is pro-constant and equal to
\Nil^{\dagger}(A)
in
\mathcal{D}(A)
. From this one deduces the result using the exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n=0}^{r}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

which is immediate from the definition.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

Note that the above proof works just as well if we only assume
A
to be uniformly totally disconnected perfectoid and use the extended definition of
Y_{A}
from
3.4
. Indeed, we just have to replace
\Nil^{\dagger}(A_{\bullet})
by
\fib(A_{\bullet}\rightarrow A_{\bullet}^{u})
in that case.
∎

The previous lemma guarantees that the association
A\mapsto Y_{A}
from totally disconnected nilperfectoids
A
to Gelfand stacks extends to a unique colimit-preserving functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{(-)}:\mathrm{GelfStk}\rightarrow\mathrm{GelfStk}\;,\hskip 8.5359ptX\mapsto Y_{X}}

and we write
Y_{A}\coloneqq Y_{\GSpec A}
for any Gelfand ring
A
. All the properties and structures from
3.5
extend to
Y_{X}
for arbitrary Gelfand stacks
X
by descent and by descent from the case of totally disconnected nilperfectoids we obtain a chain of maps

\widehat{Y}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}\rightarrow Y_{X}\rightarrow Y_{X}^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\;.

The association
A\mapsto Y_{A}
also preserves finite étale maps and open embeddings.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a finite étale map of totally disconnected nilperfectoids. Then the induced map
Y_{B}\rightarrow Y_{A}
is finite étale as well, i.e.
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(B)
is a finite étale
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(A)
-algebra for all
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
.

Proof.

It suffices to check the claim for intervals of the form
[p^{-r},p^{r}]
. Then first observe that
B\otimes_{A}\overline{A}
is a finite étale
\overline{A}
-algebra and hence perfectoid. As the map
B\rightarrow B\otimes_{A}\overline{A}
is surjective and its kernel is contained in
\Nil^{\dagger}(B)
, we conclude that
B\otimes_{A}\overline{A}\cong\overline{B}
. In particular,
\overline{B}
is a finite étale
\overline{A}
-algebra and this implies that
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{B})
is a finite étale
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})
-algebra.

We now first prove that
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B)
is a finite projective
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)
-module. For this, after localising on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A))\cong\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A}))
, we may assume that
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{B})
is a free
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})
-module. Via the maps
\theta\circ F^{-n}:\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{B})\rightarrow\overline{B}
for
0\leq n\leq r
, this yields bases of
\overline{B}
as an
\overline{A}
-module, any lift of which will yield bases of
B
as an
A
-module: indeed, isomorphisms of finite projective modules may be checked after base change to the
\dagger
-reduction as the
\dagger
-nilradical is contained in the Jacobson radical by
[
39
, Prop. 2.6.16.(2)]
. By definition, we then conclude that
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B)
is a free
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)
-module. Moreover, this argument also shows that the canonical map
\theta:\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B)\rightarrow B
induces an isomorphism

(3.1.2)

\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B)\otimes_{\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)}A\cong B\;.

Finally, given the finite projectivity of
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B)(*)
as an
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)(*)
-module, we may check whether it is étale after base change to any residue field of
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)(*)
and, in particular, after base change to the
\dagger
-reduction
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})
as the
\dagger
-nilradical is contained in the Jacobson radical. However, now we are done if we can show that the natural map induces an isomorphism

\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B)\otimes_{\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)}\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})\cong\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{B})\;.

To check this last isomorphism, note that the right-hand side is the quotient of
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B)
by
\prod_{n=0}^{r}\Nil^{\dagger}(B)
while the left-hand side is the quotient of
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B)
by

\begin{split}\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B)\otimes_{\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)}\prod_{n=0}^{r}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)&\cong\left(\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B)\otimes_{\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)}\prod_{n=0}^{r}A\right)\otimes_{\prod_{n=0}^{r}A}\prod_{n=0}^{r}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\\
&\cong\prod_{n=0}^{r}B\otimes_{\prod_{n=0}^{r}A}\prod_{n=0}^{r}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\cong\prod_{n=0}^{r}\Nil^{\dagger}(B)\;,\end{split}

where the penultimate isomorphism is (
3.1.2
) while the last one just amounts to
B\otimes_{A}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\cong\Nil^{\dagger}(B)
, which in turn follows from
B\otimes_{A}\overline{A}\cong\overline{B}
by passing to fibres of the canonical maps from
B
.
∎

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
U\rightarrow\GSpec A
be an open embedding of derived Berkovich spaces with
A
totally disconnected nilperfectoid. Then the induced map
Y_{U}\rightarrow Y_{A}
is an open embedding as well. More precisely, there is a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|U|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}(A)\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

where
Y_{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(A)
is given by
Y_{A}\rightarrow Y_{A}^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong Y_{\overline{A}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\overline{A})\cong\mathcal{M}(A)
for the map
Y_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\overline{A})
coming from
[
1
, Lem. 4.4.5]
.

Proof.

It suffices to check the claim for open embeddings of the form
U=\{|f|<1\}
and
U=\{|f|>1\}
for
f\in A
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{M}(A)\cong\mathcal{M}(\overline{A})\cong\mathcal{M}(\overline{A}^{\flat})
, we may assume that
f
is a lift of an element
f^{\flat\sharp}\in\overline{A}
for some
f^{\flat}\in\overline{A}^{\flat}
. We only treat the case
U=\{|f|<1\}
, the other one is completely analogous. Moreover, we may assume that
A
is a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(p^{1/p^{\infty}})
-algebra by descent.

To begin the argument in this case, let
B_{\varepsilon}\coloneqq A\langle f\rangle_{\leq p^{-\varepsilon}}
and note that
B_{\varepsilon}^{u}\cong\overline{A}\langle p^{-\varepsilon}f\rangle
is perfectoid with tilt
\overline{A}\langle(p^{\flat})^{-\varepsilon}f^{\flat}\rangle
, hence
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(B_{\varepsilon}^{u})=\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(\overline{A})\langle[(p^{\flat})^{-\varepsilon}f^{\flat}]\rangle
. Furthermore, letting
f_{n}
denote an arbitrary lift of
(f^{\flat\sharp})^{1/p^{n}}
to
A
and
f_{0}\coloneqq f
, observe that
B_{\varepsilon}=A\langle f_{n}\rangle_{\leq p^{-\varepsilon/p^{n}}}
by
[
1
, Cor. 2.2.14]
. Hence, using
3.7
, we know that
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B_{\varepsilon})
sits in a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B_{\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})\langle(p^{\flat})^{-\varepsilon}f^{\flat}\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n=0}^{r}A\langle f_{n}\rangle_{\leq p^{-\varepsilon/p^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n=0}^{r}\overline{A}\langle(p^{\flat})^{-\varepsilon/p^{n}}(f^{\flat\sharp})^{1/p^{n}}\rangle\;.}

In particular, letting
f^{\flat}\in\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)
denote the gluing of
f^{\flat}\in\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})
and
(f_{0},f_{1},\dots,f_{r})\in\prod_{n=0}^{r}A
, we see that there are canonical maps

\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)\langle(p^{\flat})^{-\varepsilon}f^{\flat}\rangle\rightarrow\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)\langle f^{\flat}\rangle_{\leq p^{-\varepsilon}}\;,

from which we conclude that

Y_{U,[p^{-r},p^{r}]}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\varepsilon>0}Y_{B_{\varepsilon},[p^{-r},p^{r}]}=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\varepsilon>0}\GSpec\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(B_{\varepsilon})\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\varepsilon>0}\GSpec\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)\langle f^{\flat}\rangle_{\leq p^{-\varepsilon}}

by cofinality, and the right-hand side is exactly
\{|f^{\flat}|<1\}\subseteq Y_{A,[p^{-r},p^{r}]}
. Taking the colimit over
r
, the claim follows.
∎

Intuitively, one should think of
Y_{A}
as obtained by replacing the image of
\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}
by the thickening
\GSpec A
and then freely adding Frobenius translates of this thickening, see
Fig.

2
. This is made precise by the following proposition.

Proposition 3.10
.

For any totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0}\GSpec\overline{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\overline{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0}\GSpec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqcup_{n\geq 0}\varphi^{n}\circ\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqcup_{n\geq 0}\varphi^{n}\circ\iota}

is a pushout in the category of derived Berkovich spaces. In particular, the map
Y_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow Y_{A}
is an isomorphism away from
\{1,p,p^{2},\dots\}\subseteq(0,\infty)
.

Proof.

The second assertion follows from the first since the image of the
n
-th Frobenius translate of
\GSpec A
in
Y_{A}
is supported over
\{p^{n}\}\subseteq(0,\infty)
under the radius map. To prove the first assertion, we check the universal property, i.e. that the natural map

(3.1.3)

\Hom(Y_{A},X)\rightarrow\Hom(Y_{\overline{A}},X)\times_{\prod_{n\geq 0}X(\overline{A})}\prod_{n\geq 0}X(A)

is an equivalence for any derived Berkovich space
X
. For this, first note that the diagram in question induces a pushout on underlying topological spaces: Indeed, this is because both
Y_{A}
and
Y_{\overline{A}}
as well as
\GSpec A
and
\GSpec\overline{A}
have the same underlying topological space. Thus, it suffices to check that (
3.1.3
) separately induces equivalences on the fibres of the left- and right-hand side over
\Hom_{\mathrm{cont}}(|Y_{A}|,|X|)
.

Thus, fix a map
|Y_{A}|\rightarrow|X|
of topological spaces. Taking an affine rational cover of
X
whose pullback along this map can be refined by a strict rational cover of
|Y_{A}|
, we can reduce to the case where
X=\GSpec B
is affine. In that case, however, the claim follows from the fact that each
\mathcal{O}(Y_{A,[p^{-r},p^{r}]})=\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)
is by definition the pullback of the rings of global functions on
Y_{\overline{A},[p^{-r},p^{r}]}
and
\bigsqcup_{n=0}^{r}\GSpec A
over the ring of global functions on
\bigsqcup_{n=0}^{r}\GSpec\overline{A}
.
∎

From the definition of
Y_{A}
and the picture in
Fig.

2
, one should have the intuition that the Frobenius
\varphi:Y_{A}\rightarrow Y_{A}
is an isomorphism away from from the image of

\GSpec\overline{A}\xrightarrow{\iota}Y_{\overline{A}}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{-1}}Y_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow Y_{A}\;,

which we will at times just denote by
\varphi^{-1}\circ\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{A}
in the future. This is made precise by the following lemma.

Lemma 3.11
.

For any totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\overline{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{-1}\circ\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

is a pushout in the category of derived Berkovich spaces.

Proof.

Note that Frobenius induces an isomorphism
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(\overline{A})\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r-1},p^{r-1}]}(\overline{A})
for any
r\geq 0
. As the shift map
\prod_{n=0}^{r}A\rightarrow\prod_{n=0}^{r-1}A
is surjective, we conclude that

F:\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)\rightarrow\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r-1},p^{r-1}]}(A)

is surjective as well and that the kernel agrees with the kernel of the induced map on
\dagger
-nilradicals. Since this is the shift map
\prod_{n=0}^{r}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\rightarrow\prod_{n=0}^{r-1}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)
, we conclude that there is an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Nil^{\dagger}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r-1},p^{r-1}]}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

which in turn implies that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r-1},p^{r-1}]}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

is cartesian. Now one deduces the desired pushout by the same argument as in the proof of
3.10
.
∎

3.2.
Analytic prismatisation

Recall the notion of degree
1
Cartier divisors on
Y_{\overline{A}}
from
[
25
, Def. II.1.19]
: These are defined to be exactly those Cartier divisors arising as maps

\GSpec\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}

for an untilt
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}
of
\overline{A}^{\flat}
. Moreover, they are always cut out by an element of the form
p+a[\pi^{\flat}]
for some
a\in W(\overline{A}^{\flat\circ})
and some perfectoid pseudouniformiser
\pi\in\overline{A}
; any such element is called
distinguished
. Also note that, for us, a Cartier divisor on an arbitrary Gelfand stack is defined as a map
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
. By abuse of notation, we will often simply say that
D\subseteq X
is a Cartier divisor, where
D
denotes the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Let us point out that the above is also an abuse of notation in a second sense: the map
D\rightarrow X
is
not
an injection, i.e. its diagonal is not an isomorphism (note that this already happens for usual effective Cartier divisors when one works in the setting of derived algebraic geometry).

Definition 3.12
.

Let
A
be totally disconnected nilperfectoid. A Cartier divisor
D\subseteq Y_{A}
is called
of degree
1
if its pullback
D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{\overline{A}}
is a degree
1
Cartier divisor in
Y_{\overline{A}}
.

Remark 3.13
.

Note that any degree
1
Cartier divisor
D\subseteq Y_{A}
is affine. Indeed, any degree
1
Cartier divisor in
Y_{\overline{A}}
is fully contained in
Y_{\overline{A},[r,s]}
for some
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
and hence
D
is already a Cartier divisor in
Y_{A,[r,s]}=\GSpec\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{A,[r,s]}
.
∎

Example 3.14
.

The map
\varphi^{-1}\circ\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{A}
is a degree
1
Cartier divisor. This is because the image of this composition is supported over
\{1/p\}\subseteq(0,\infty)
under the radius map and
Y_{A}
is isomorphic to
Y_{\overline{A}}
over
(0,1)
by
3.10
.
∎

We quickly record a rigidity property of degree
1
Cartier divisors on
Y_{A}
which will occasionally be useful in the sequel.

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
A
be totally disconnected nilperfectoid. Then any map
D_{1}\rightarrow D_{2}
between degree
1
Cartier divisors on
Y_{A}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

First recall that the statement is true after pullback to
Y_{\overline{A}}
. Indeed, we have

D_{1}\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{\overline{A}}\cong\GSpec\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp_{1}}\;,\hskip 8.5359ptD_{2}\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{\overline{A}}\cong\GSpec\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp_{2}}

for two characteristic zero untilts
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp_{1}}
and
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp_{2}}
of
\overline{A}^{\flat}
and the map
D_{1}\rightarrow D_{2}
corresponds to a map
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp_{2}}\rightarrow\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp_{1}}
. However, as perfectoid spaces over
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp_{2}}
are equivalent to perfectoid spaces over
\overline{A}^{\flat}
via tilting and the map
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp_{2}}\rightarrow\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp_{1}}
induces an isomorphism on tilts, it must be an isomorphism.

Returning to the situation on
Y_{A}
, first observe that the given map induces a map
\mathcal{O}(-D_{2})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(-D_{1})
between the ideal sheaves. Now recall that
\mathcal{O}(-D_{1})
and
\mathcal{O}(-D_{2})
yield invertible modules over any
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(A)(*)
by
2.3
, and hence checking whether
\mathcal{O}(-D_{2})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(-D_{1})
is an isomorphism may be done in each residue field separately by Nakayama’s lemma. However, as
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(A)\rightarrow\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(\overline{A})
is a
\dagger
-thickening and
\Nil^{\dagger}(\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(A))(*)
is contained in the Jacobson radical of
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(A)(*)
by
[
39
, Prop. 2.6.16.(2)]
, this reduces the claim to the situation after pullback to
Y_{\overline{A}}
, which we have discussed in the previous paragraph.
∎

Let us also note another useful lemma with a similar proof.

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
A
be totally disconnected nilperfectoid. For any degree
1
Cartier divisor
D\subseteq Y_{A}
, there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{O}(-D)\cong\mathcal{O}
.

Proof.

First recall from
[
25
, Prop. II.2.3]
that such an isomorphism exists after pullback to
Y_{\overline{A}}
. Moreover, as
D
is supported on
Y_{A,[r,s]}
for some interval
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
, we have
\mathcal{O}(-D)\cong\mathcal{O}
on
Y_{A,(0,r^{\prime})}
and on
Y_{A,(s^{\prime},\infty)}
for
r^{\prime}<r
and
s^{\prime}>s
, so it suffices to trivialise
\mathcal{O}(-D)
on
Y_{A,[r^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s^{\prime}+\varepsilon]}
for some
\varepsilon>0
.

In other words, we are done as soon as we show that
\mathcal{O}(-D)
yields a trivial invertible module over any
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(A)(*)
via
2.3
. However, this may be checked separately in each residue field by Nakayama’s lemma and hence reduces to the claim after pullback to
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[r,s]}(\overline{A})(*)
as in the proof of the previous lemma, where we already know it.
∎

We are now ready to define the prismatisation. For this, recall that totally disconnected nilperfectoids form a basis for the
!
-topology on Gelfand rings and hence any Gelfand stack is uniquely determined by its points on totally disconnected nilperfectoid rings.

Definition 3.17
.

The (rational)
analytic prismatisation
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is the Gelfand stack
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
defined by

\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}(A)=\{\text{degree $1$ Cartier divisors $D\subseteq Y_{A}$}\}

for totally disconnected nilperfectoids
A
. For an arbitrary Gelfand stack
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we define its (rational) analytic prismatisation as the Gelfand stack
X{\Delta}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
given by

X{\Delta}(\GSpec A\xrightarrow{D}\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta})=\{\text{maps $D\rightarrow X$}\}

for totally disconnected nilperfectoids
A
.

Note that
3.6
guarantees that sheafification does not change the value of
X{\Delta}
on totally disconnected nilperfectoids. Moreover, the Frobenius on
Y_{A}
induces a Frobenius
\varphi:\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
via pullback of divisors and hence also a Frobenius
\varphi:X{\Delta}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
for any
X
. Finally, observe that it is immediate from the definition that the association
X\mapsto X{\Delta}
commutes with limits.

Example 3.18
.

The degree
1
divisor
\varphi^{-1}\circ\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{A}
from
3.14
yields a map
i_{\mathrm{dR}}:\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
called the
de Rham map
.
∎

Before we move on, let us describe the de Rham stack of
X{\Delta}
because it is particularly easy.

Proposition 3.19
.

Let
X
be any Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then there is a natural map

Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}

which induces an isomorphism on de Rham stacks.

Proof.

To construct the desired map, we have to give a map
Y_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
for any map
\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow X
from a totally disconnected perfectoid to
X
. As
X{\Delta}
is defined by sheafification from its value on totally disconnected perfectoids, we have to give compatible degree
1
divisors
D\subseteq Y_{\overline{B}}
equipped with maps
D\rightarrow X
for every totally disconnected perfectoid
\GSpec\overline{B}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}
. However, for any such
\overline{B}
, the map
\GSpec\overline{B}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}
uniquely extends to a map
Y_{\overline{B}}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}
and then we can take
D
to be the pullback of
\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}
along this map, which will automatically be equipped with a map to
X
since
\GSpec\overline{A}
is.

Now let us see that the above defines an isomorphism on de Rham stacks. For this, note that, for any totally disconnected perfectoid
\overline{A}
, an
\overline{A}
-point of
X{\Delta}
amounts to a characteristic zero untilt
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}
of
\overline{A}^{\flat}
together with a map
\GSpec\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}\rightarrow X
, which we may equivalently write as
\Spd\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}\rightarrow X^{\diamondsuit}
. However, this is exactly the same as an
\overline{A}
-point of
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}^{\diamondsuit}=X^{\diamondsuit}\times_{\Spd\mathbb{F}_{p}}\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, so we are done.
∎

Thanks to the radius map
\kappa:Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
, the previous proposition allows us to equip
X{\Delta}
with a radius map for any Gelfand stack
X
via the composition

\kappa:X{\Delta}\rightarrow(X{\Delta})^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\xrightarrow{\kappa^{\mathrm{dR}}}(0,\infty)^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong(0,\infty)\;,

where the last step again uses idempotency of
(-)^{\mathrm{dR}}
and
2.32
. As usual, we will denote the preimage of any interval
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
under this map by
X{\Delta}_{[r,s]}
.

Moving on, observe that pulling back a degree
1
divisor along
\iota:\GSpec A\rightarrow Y_{A}
induces a map

\mu:\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\;.

However, we can actually do slightly better.

Lemma 3.20
.

The map
\mu:\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
can be refined to a map
\mu:\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
, where
\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}
denotes the open unit disk.

Proof.

As being a unit on
Y_{A}
may be checked after pullback to
Y_{\overline{A}}
by
[
39
, Prop. 2.6.16.(2)]
and the pullback of
D
to
Y_{\overline{A}}
is cut out by a distinguished element, we may choose a global section of
\mathcal{O}(D)
cutting out
D
which pulls back to a distinguished element on
Y_{\overline{A}}
. We claim that, after pullback along
\iota:\GSpec A\rightarrow Y_{A}
, all such global sections differ only by units of norm
1
and have themselves norm less than
1
. This will then define the desired map
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
.

As an element having norm in
[r,s]\subseteq[0,\infty)
may be checked after
\dagger
-reduction, it will be enough to show the following assertions:

(a)

Any distinguished element
p+a[\pi^{\flat}]
in
W(\overline{A}^{\flat\circ})
maps to a topologically nilpotent element under Fontaine’s map
\theta:W(\overline{A}^{\flat\circ})\rightarrow\overline{A}^{\circ}
.

(b)

If two distinguished elements of
W(\overline{A}^{\flat\circ})
differ by a global unit
u
on
Y_{\overline{A}}
, the image of
u
under Fontaine’s map has norm
1
.

For (a), note that the kernel of
\theta
is generated by a distinguished element
p+b[\varpi^{\flat}]
as well and the difference
(p+a[\pi^{\flat}])-(p+b[\varpi^{\flat}])=a[\pi^{\flat}]-b[\varpi^{\flat}]
is already topologically nilpotent in
W(\overline{A}^{\flat\circ})
, whence the claim. For (b), we observe that two distinguished elements
p+a[\varpi^{\flat}]
and
p+b[\varpi^{\flat}]
in
W(\overline{A}^{\flat\circ})
differing by a global unit
u
on
Y_{\overline{A}}
will be nonvanishing in a rational neighbourhoods of
V([\pi^{\flat}])
and of
V(p)
in
\Spa W(\overline{A}^{\flat\circ})
as they cut out a degree
1
divisor of
Y_{\overline{A}}
. Thus, we can even assume that
u
is a unit in
W(\overline{A}^{\flat\circ})
and this will of course imply that
\theta(u)\in(\overline{A}^{\flat\circ})^{\times}
, i.e.
\theta(u)
has norm
1
.
∎

For any Gelfand stack
X
, the above yields a composite map

\mu:X{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\xrightarrow{\mu}\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;.

The divisor cut out by this map plays a distinguished role.

Definition 3.21
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack. The (analytic)
Hodge–Tate stack

X^{\mathrm{HT}}
of
X
is defined as the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HT}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

It is a Cartier divisor in
X{\Delta}
and we let
\mathcal{I}\in\Pic(X{\Delta})
denote its ideal sheaf.

By
3.28
below, the above indeed agrees with the analytic Hodge–Tate stack as defined by Anschütz–Le Bras–Rodríguez Camargo–Scholze in
[
2
]
.

Definition 3.22
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, the
Breuil–Kisin line bundle

\mathcal{O}\{1\}
on
X{\Delta}
is defined by

\mathcal{O}\{1\}\coloneqq\bigotimes_{n\geq 0}(\varphi^{n})^{*}\mathcal{I}\;.

Note that the infinite tensor product occurring in the definition above makes sense: Indeed, the divisor
X^{\mathrm{HT}}\subseteq X{\Delta}
is supported over
\{1\}
under the radius map
\kappa:X{\Delta}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
and hence its inverse images under Frobenius are supported over
1/p,1/p^{2},\dots
. In particular, over each
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
, only finitely many of the line bundles
(\varphi^{n})^{*}\mathcal{I}
are nontrivial and hence the infinite tensor product above is actually a finite tensor product locally on
X{\Delta}
.

3.3.
Relation to the Hyodo–Kato stack and the perfect prismatisation

Recall from
3.19
that, for any Gelfand stack
X
, there is a natural map
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
and that it induces an isomorphism on de Rham stacks. Thus, understanding where this map is an isomorphism amounts to understanding where the natural map
X{\Delta}\rightarrow(X{\Delta})^{\mathrm{dR}}
is an isomorphism.

Proposition 3.23
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, the natural map

X{\Delta}\rightarrow(X{\Delta})^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}^{\mathrm{dR}}

is an isomorphism over the locus
(1,\infty)
under the radius map.

Proof.

We claim that a map
\GSpec A\rightarrow X{\Delta}
classifying a degree
1
Cartier divisor
D\subseteq Y_{A}
together with a map
D\rightarrow X
factors through
X{\Delta}_{(1,\infty)}
if and only if
D
is supported on
Y_{A,(0,1)}
. Once we know this, the claim follows: Indeed, the divisor
\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}
is supported over
1\in(0,\infty)
and hence its Frobenius translates are supported over
p,p^{2},p^{3},\dots
. By
3.10
, this implies that the natural map induces an isomorphism
Y_{\overline{A},(0,1)}\cong Y_{A,(0,1)}
.

To establish the remaining claim, first note that
D
being supported on
Y_{A,(0,1)}
may be checked after pullback to
Y_{\overline{A},(0,1)}
as units may be detected after
\dagger
-reduction by
[
39
, Prop. 2.6.16.(2)]
. Similarly, by definition of the radius map,
\GSpec A\rightarrow X{\Delta}
factoring through
X{\Delta}_{(1,\infty)}
may be checked on the level of de Rham stacks by definition of the radius map. By descent for
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
, we may thus reduce to the case where
A=\overline{A}
and
X=\GSpec\overline{B}
is affinoid perfectoid and we may even assume that both
\overline{A}
and
\overline{B}
are
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(p^{1/p^{\infty}})
-algebras.

Now
D
corresponds to a characteristic zero untilt
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}
of
\overline{A}^{\flat}
and
D
being supported on
Y_{\overline{A},(0,1)}
translates to
|p|<|[p^{\flat}]|
on
\GSpec\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}\subseteq Y_{\overline{A}}
. Similarly, the image of
\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{B}}
being supported over
(1,\infty)
translates to
|[p^{\flat}]|<|p|
after pullback along

\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}\cong Y_{\overline{A}^{\sharp\flat}}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{B}}\;,

where the last map is induced by the given map
\GSpec\overline{A}^{\sharp\flat}\rightarrow\GSpec\overline{B}
. Indeed, these two conditions match as the isomorphism
Y_{\overline{A}}\cong Y_{\overline{A}^{\sharp\flat}}
exactly interchanges
p
and
[p^{\flat}]
, so we are done.
∎

Example 3.24
.

Recall that there is a natural map
\iota:X^{\diamondsuit}\rightarrow Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
for any Gelfand stack
X
, the image of which is supported over
1\in(0,\infty)
under the radius map. As Frobenius is an isomorphism on
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
, we also obtain a map
\varphi\circ\iota:X^{\diamondsuit}\rightarrow Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
, whose image is now supported over
p
. Thus, by the previous proposition, there is an induced map

X^{\mathrm{dR}}\xrightarrow{(\varphi\circ\iota)^{\mathrm{dR}}}Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},(1,\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong X{\Delta}_{(1,\infty)}\rightarrow X{\Delta}

and one checks that this map identifies with the map
i_{\mathrm{dR}}
from
3.18
by unwinding definitions. Since the map
X^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
is a closed embedding as
X^{\diamondsuit}\rightarrow Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
is a Zariski-closed immersion, see
[
1
, Prop. 4.7.12.(1)]
, we conclude that
i_{\mathrm{dR}}
is a closed embedding.
∎

From the above we conclude that Frobenius induces an isomorphism
\varphi:X{\Delta}_{(1,\infty)}\xrightarrow{\cong}X{\Delta}_{(p,\infty)}
. Moreover, quotienting out by Frobenius on this locus exactly yields the Hyodo–Kato stack of
X
, i.e. the natural map
X{\Delta}\rightarrow(X{\Delta})^{\mathrm{dR}}
induces an isomorphism
