.

Let us now also say something about
X{\Delta}
over the locus
(0,p)
. As already announced in the beginning, this will be related to the stack
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
from
[
2
]
, which is the quotient of the perfect prismatisation from loc. cit. by Frobenius and whose definition we quickly recall.

Definition 3.25
.

For any totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
, we define the
modified Fargues–Fontaine curve

\mathrm{FF}_{A}
over
A
as the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\overline{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FF}_{\overline{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FF}_{A}}

in the category of derived Berkovich spaces, where
\mathrm{FF}_{\overline{A}}=Y_{\overline{A}}/\varphi{Z}
is the usual Fargues–Fontaine curve of
\overline{A}
.

Remark 3.26
.

(a)

Instead of defining
\mathrm{FF}_{A}
as a pushout in derived Berkovich spaces, we could also have defined the rings of functions on affine pieces of
\mathrm{FF}_{A}
by a pullback of rings similarly to how we defined
Y_{A}
. Indeed, the same argument as in the proof of
3.10
shows that this alternative definition is equivalent to the one given above.

(b)

By a similar argument as in
3.6
, the association
A\mapsto\mathrm{FF}_{A}
from totally disconnected nilperfectoids to Gelfand stacks preserves
!
-equivalences and hence extends to a colimit-preserving functor

\mathrm{FF}_{(-)}:\mathrm{GelfStk}\rightarrow\mathrm{GelfStk}\;,\hskip 8.5359ptX\mapsto\mathrm{FF}_{X}\;.\qed

As in the case of
Y_{A}
, we define degree
1
Cartier divisors on
\mathrm{FF}_{A}
to be exactly those whose pullback to
\mathrm{FF}_{\overline{A}}
has degree
1
.

Definition 3.27
.

The Gelfand stack
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}=\mathrm{Div}^{1}
is defined by

\mathrm{Div}^{1}(A)=\{\text{degree $1$ Cartier divisors $D\subseteq\mathrm{FF}_{A}$}\}

on totally disconnected nilperfectoids
A
. For an arbitrary Gelfand stack
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we define
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
as the Gelfand stack over
\mathrm{Div}^{1}
given by

X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}(\GSpec A\xrightarrow{D}\mathrm{Div}^{1})=\{\text{maps $D\rightarrow X$}\}

on totally disconnected nilperfectoids
A
.

Recall that pulling back a degree
1
Cartier divisor along the natural map
\iota:\GSpec A\rightarrow\mathrm{FF}_{A}
induces a morphism

\mu:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}

and hence we obtain
\mu:X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\xrightarrow{\mu}\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Moreover, recall that, in
[
2
]
, the analytic Hodge–Tate stack
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
of
X
is defined as the divisor in
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
classified by this morphism and we let
\mathcal{I}
denote its ideal sheaf. Using the following proposition, one can check that this is equivalent to our previous definition of
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
.

Proposition 3.28
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, Frobenius induces an isomorphism
\varphi:X{\Delta}_{(0,1)}\xrightarrow{\cong}X{\Delta}_{(0,p)}
and we have

\lxSVG@closescope {{

However, (i) follows from
3.11
as the image of
\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}
is supported over
1\in(0,\infty)
under the radius map. Moreover, (ii) follows from
3.10
.
∎

Putting together
3.23
and
3.28
above, we see that understanding
X{\Delta}
exactly amounts to understanding
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
and
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
. Thus, let us quickly recall some explicit descriptions of
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
in special cases.

Proposition 3.29
.

There is an isomorphism

\mathrm{Div}^{1}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\;,

where the action of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
is by exponentiation, i.e.
\gamma.(q,q^{1/p},\dots)=(q^{\gamma},q^{\gamma/p},\dots)
, and the map from
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})
to
\mathrm{Div}^{1}
sends an
A
-point
(q,q^{1/p},\dots)
of the source to the degree
1
Cartier divisor on
\mathrm{FF}_{A}
cut out by the function obtained by gluing
\log[q^{\flat}]
on
\mathrm{FF}_{\overline{A}}
with
\log q
on
\GSpec A
for any totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
. Furthermore, as normed multiplicative group stacks over
\mathrm{Div}^{1}
, we have

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\;,

where
\theta\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}
acts on a sequence
(x,x^{1/p},\dots)
of
p
-power roots via

\theta.(x,x^{1/p},\dots)=(q^{\theta}x,q^{\theta/p}x^{1/p},\dots)\;.

Proof.

See
[
2
]
.
∎

As
X^{\mathrm{HK}}=(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
for any Gelfand stack
X
by the argument from the proof of
3.19
, the above immediately implies the following presentations of Hyodo–Kato stacks.

Corollary 3.30
.

There are isomorphisms

\begin{split}\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}&\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}\;,\\
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{HK}}&\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}\;,\end{split}

where the actions are as in the previous proposition.

Finally, recall that both the functors
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{HK}}
and
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
have good cohomological properties; in particular, they preserve smooth maps in the sense of the theorem below. To state the result, recall that
\mathcal{I}
denotes the ideal sheaf of the Cartier divisor
X^{\mathrm{HT}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and, moreover, recall from
[
2
]
, that there is a canonical map
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow*/\varphi{Z}
. Via pullback along this map, any isocrystal over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
yields a vector bundle on
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
, and we let
\mathcal{O}\{1\}
denote the line bundle on
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
obtained by tensoring
\mathcal{I}
with the line bundle obtained from the isocrystal
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},p^{-1}\varphi)
. Also recall the line bundle
\mathcal{O}\{1\}
on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
, which corresponds to the isocrystal
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},p^{-1}\varphi)
via the equivalence from
2.43
.

Theorem 3.31
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a rigid smooth morphism of derived Berkovich spaces which is pure of relative dimension
d
. Then …

(i)

…the induced map
X^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{HK}}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf
\mathcal{O}\{d\}[2d]
.

(ii)

…the induced map
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf
\mathcal{O}\{d\}[2d]
.

Proof.

See
[
1
, Thm. 6.3.1]
and
[
2
]
.
∎

From this, we at once deduce the following properties of the functor
X\mapsto X{\Delta}
.

Corollary 3.32
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a rigid smooth morphism of derived Berkovich spaces which is pure of relative dimension
d
. Then the induced map
X{\Delta}\rightarrow Y{\Delta}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf
\mathcal{O}\{d\}[2d]
.

Proof.

The claim is local on the target and hence we may separately consider the maps
X{\Delta}_{(1,\infty)}\rightarrow Y{\Delta}_{(1,\infty)}
and
X{\Delta}_{(0,p)}\rightarrow Y{\Delta}_{(0,p)}
. However, then
3.23
and
3.28
supply pullback diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{(0,p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y{\Delta}_{(0,p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{(1,\infty)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y{\Delta}_{(1,\infty)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HK}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\mathrm{HK}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

which at once imply the result by
3.31
.
∎

We also have a smoothness result for
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
over
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Theorem 3.33
.

The maps
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
are cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf
\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2]
.

Proof.

For the first assertion, see
[
2
]
. The second assertion is obtained by combining
[
1
, Prop. 5.7.1]
and
[
39
, Prop. 6.2.4]
due to
3.30
.
∎

Corollary 3.34
.

The map
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf
\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2]
.

Proof.

We may check this on an open cover of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
, but for
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(1,\infty)}{\Delta}
, the claim follows from the previous theorem as the maps
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(1,\infty)}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
are cohomologically étale by
3.23
and
3.28
.
∎

4.
Analytic syntomification over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}

We now move on to defining the analytic syntomification
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
of a Gelfand stack
X
and studying its geometry. To set the stage, recall how one proceeds for the “algebraic” syntomification in the sense of Bhatt–Lurie and Drinfeld, see
[
8
]
or
[
18
]
. There, the main part of the work lies in constructing a filtered refinement of the prismatisation of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
called the (Nygaard-)filtered prismatisation or the
Nygaardification
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
; from here, it is not too hard to define the Nygaardification of any
p
-adic formal scheme
X
. Finally, to obtain the syntomification, one then identifies two disjoint copies of the prismatisation inside the Nygaardification and glues them together.

In our analytic situation, the picture will be entirely similar. However, to get to the filtered prismatisation, we will take a slightly different approach than
[
8
]
and
[
18
]
and instead use a convenient reformulation of the definition of the filtered prismatisation due to Gardner–Madapusi, see
[
27
, §6]
. Namely, in loc. cit., they show that one can alternatively obtain the Nygaardification of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
directly from the prismatisation of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
by taking a suitable pullback, and this will be the definition that we will transport to the analytic setting.

Even more so than the analytic prismatisation, the analytic Nygaardification
X^{\mathrm{N}}
of a Gelfand stack
X
will come with a plethora of extra structures, some of which we list here:

(i)

There is a map
\pi:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
, via which
X^{\mathrm{N}}
acquires a radius map defined as the composite
\kappa:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
.

(ii)

There is a map
t:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
and a map
u:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
.

(iii)

There are isomorphisms
X{\Delta}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=1}
and
X{\Delta}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=1}
and we denote the corresponding closed embeddings
X{\Delta}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}
by
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
, respectively. Moreover, the composition
\pi\circ j_{\mathrm{dR}}
is the identity while
\pi\circ j_{\mathrm{HT}}
is the Frobenius on
X{\Delta}
.

(iv)

There is an isomorphism
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|ut|\neq 0}\cong(X{\Delta}\setminus X^{\mathrm{dR}})\times[0,1]
, where we view
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
as a closed substack of
X{\Delta}
via
i_{\mathrm{dR}}
.

(v)

There is an isomorphism
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=0}
and we denote the corresponding closed embedding
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}
by
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}
.

The situation is summarised by the schematic pictures in
Fig.

3
and
Fig.

4
, the first of which studies
X^{\mathrm{N}}
via the maps
(t,u):X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
while the second studies
X^{\mathrm{N}}
via the radius map
\kappa:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
.

At the end of the section, we will prove two somewhat technical results that will be invaluable several times later on: First, we prove that the assignment sending a derived Berkovich space
X
to its Nygaardification
X^{\mathrm{N}}
preserves Berkovich étale maps and that
X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
is a
!
-cover if
X\rightarrow Y
is a Berkovich étale
!
-cover of derived Berkovich spaces. This will be very useful later on when we study Nygaardifications of Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich spaces as it basically allows us to reduce to the case where
X=\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
is the overconvergent
n
-dimensional torus. Second, we show that the stack
\mathbb{Q}_{p,[r,s]}^{\mathrm{N}}
is nicely coverable in the sense of
2.11
whenever
p^{1/2}<r\leq s<p^{3/2}
; using the results of the next section, this will allow us to conclude that the restriction of the Nygaardification of
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
to any
[r,s]\subseteq(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})
is nicely coverable for all
n\geq 0
. This will later enable us to apply the spreading out results for perfect complexes from §
2
to
X^{\mathrm{N}}
for any smooth derived Berkovich space
X
.

4.1.
Nygaard-filtered prismatisation

0
1
t
0
1
u
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
j_{\mathrm{HT}}(X{\Delta})
j_{\mathrm{dR}}(X{\Delta})
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
X^{\mathrm{dR}}

Figure 3.
A schematic picture of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
via the map
(t,u)

As already announced above, our strategy to define
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
will be to imitate the pullback definition of the algebraic Nygaardification of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
given in
[
27
, §6]
. For this, first recall from
3.20
that the map
\mu:\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
obtained by pulling back a degree
1
divisor along
\iota:\GSpec A\rightarrow Y_{A}
can be refined to a map
\mu:\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
. By the same arguments, the map
\widetilde{\mu}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
obtained by instead pulling back a degree
1
divisor along
\varphi^{-1}\circ\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{A}
may be refined to a map

\widetilde{\mu}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow(\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\;.

With this in place, we can make the following definition.

Definition 4.1
.

The (rational)
analytic Nygaard-filtered prismatisation
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is the Gelfand stack
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
defined as the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

Here, the subscript
(-)_{+}
or
(-)_{-}
indicates whether
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
acts on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
by multiplication or division, respectively, and the bottom map is induced by the multiplication map on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
and the division map
\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}},(r,s)\mapsto r/s
.

In the future, we will mostly suppress the subscripts
(-)_{+}
or
(-)_{-}
from the notation and only use them for particular emphasis. Moreover, in the setting above, we will often also use
t
and
u
not only to denote the maps from
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
to
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
and
(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
coming from the diagram above, but also the coordinates on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}
and
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-}
, respectively.

Remark 4.2
.

Note that, since
\widetilde{\mu}
factors through
(\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\subseteq(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
, the stack
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is already supported on the locus

\{|ut|<1\}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}

via the map
(t,u)
.
∎

Let us unpack the definition of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
and describe its functor of points on totally disconnected nilperfectoids
A
. Namely, an
A
-point consists of a triple
(D\subseteq Y_{A},t:L\rightarrow A,u:\overline{A}\rightarrow K)
, where …

(i)

…
D\subseteq Y_{A}
is a degree
1
Cartier divisor,

(ii)

…
t:L\rightarrow A
is a normed generalised Cartier divisor on
A
of norm at most
1
,

(iii)

…
u:\overline{A}\rightarrow K
is the dual of a normed generalised Cartier divisor on
\overline{A}
of norm at most
1
,

together with an isomorphism

(4.1.1)

D\times_{Y_{A}}\GSpec\overline{A}\cong\GSpec\Cone(L\otimes_{A}K^{\vee}\xrightarrow{ut}\overline{A})

of normed generalised Cartier divisors on
\overline{A}
. Here, the map
\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{A}
occurring in the pullback on the left-hand side is of course the map
\varphi^{-1}\circ\iota
also used to define
\widetilde{\mu}
and the fact that we have refined
\widetilde{\mu}
to a map with target
(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
endows the left-hand side of (
4.1.1
) with the structure of a normed generalised Cartier divisor on
\overline{A}
.

Keeping the notation from the previous paragraph, let us introduce the abbreviation
\overline{L}\coloneqq L\otimes_{A}\overline{A}
. Then the map
u:K^{\vee}\rightarrow\overline{A}
induces a map

\GSpec\Cone(\overline{L}\rightarrow\overline{A})\rightarrow\GSpec\Cone(L\otimes_{A}K^{\vee}\rightarrow\overline{A})

and, using the isomorphism (
4.1.1
), we obtain a composite map

(4.1.2)

\GSpec\Cone(\overline{L}\rightarrow\overline{A})\rightarrow D\times_{Y_{A}}\GSpec\overline{A}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}D\;.

Using this map, we can define a new Gelfand stack
D^{+}
as the pushout

(4.1.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\Cone(\overline{L}\rightarrow\overline{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\Cone(L\rightarrow A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{+}}

in the category of derived Berkovich spaces.

\varphi^{-2}(X^{\mathrm{HT}})
\varphi^{-1}(X^{\mathrm{HT}})

Figure 4.
A schematic picture of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
via the radius map

Remark 4.3
.

Recall that
D
is affine and observe that
\Cone(L\rightarrow A)
is a
\dagger
-nilpotent thickening of
\Cone(\overline{L}\rightarrow\overline{A})
. Thus, by the same argument as in the proof of
3.10
, we could have equivalently defined
\mathcal{O}(D^{+})
as the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(D^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cone(L\rightarrow A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cone(\overline{L}\rightarrow\overline{A})}

and then set
D^{+}\coloneqq\GSpec\mathcal{O}(D^{+})
. Using this, a similar argument as in the proof of
3.6
shows that the assignment given by sending a totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
to
D^{+}
satisfies descent.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

We note that there is a natural map
D^{+}\rightarrow Y_{A}
induced by the maps

\GSpec\Cone(L\rightarrow A)\rightarrow\GSpec A\xrightarrow{\iota}Y_{A}

and
D\subseteq Y_{A}\xrightarrow{\varphi}Y_{A}
, which one easily checks to be compatible.
∎

The above construction allows us to define
X^{\mathrm{N}}
for an arbitrary Gelfand stack
X
.

Definition 4.5
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, we define its (rational)
analytic Nygaard-filtered prismatisation
as the Gelfand stack
X^{\mathrm{N}}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
given by

X^{\mathrm{N}}(\GSpec A\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}})\coloneqq\{\text{maps}\,D^{+}\rightarrow X\}

for any totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
.

Note that, as in the case of
X{\Delta}
, it is immediate from the definition that the association
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
commutes with limits.

Note that any map
D^{+}\rightarrow X
induces a map
D\rightarrow X
by precomposition with
D\rightarrow D^{+}
and hence we obtain a map

\pi:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}

for any Gelfand stack
X
living over the map
\pi:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
. Moreover, using this we can equip
X^{\mathrm{N}}
with a radius map given by the composition

\kappa:X^{\mathrm{N}}\xrightarrow{\pi}X{\Delta}\xrightarrow{\kappa}(0,\infty)\;,

see also
Fig.

4
.

Before we move on to discussing the geometry of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
in more detail, let us give a convenient repackaging of the above definition in terms of yet another stack associated to
X
.

Definition 4.6
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, we define its
cone stack
to be the Gelfand stack
X^{\Cone}
over
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
obtained by sheafifying the assignment

X^{\Cone}(\GSpec A\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})\coloneqq\{\text{maps}\,\GSpec\Cone(L\rightarrow A)\rightarrow X\}\;.

Let us immediately emphasise that the assignment
X\mapsto X^{\Cone}
has very bad properties: e.g., we do not even know whether it sends finite étale maps to surjections of Gelfand stacks. Nevertheless, using
X^{\Cone}
, we can give the following alternative definition of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
for any derived Berkovich space
X
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
X
be a derived Berkovich space. Then there is a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\Cone}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\Cone})^{\mathrm{dR}}\;,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

where the map
X{\Delta}\rightarrow(X^{\Cone})^{\mathrm{dR}}
is induced by the map (
4.1.2
).

Proof.

Immediate from the definition of
D^{+}
as a pushout in derived Berkovich spaces.
∎

Let us point out that, conceptually, the reason why the formal properties of the functor
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
are much better than those of
X\mapsto X^{\Cone}
, as we will see below, e.g. in
4.20
, is that
X^{\Cone}
only enters into the definition of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
via its fibres over
(X^{\Cone})^{\mathrm{dR}}
, which are much better behaved than
X^{\Cone}
itself.

4.2.
Interesting loci inside
X^{\mathrm{N}}

We now turn to studying the geometry of the stack
X^{\mathrm{N}}
by describing some interesting loci inside it.

4.2.1.
The map
j_{\mathrm{dR}}

Note that the composition

\overline{\mathbb{T}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{mult}}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}

is an isomorphism and hence the pullback of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
along the first map is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
via
\pi:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
. In other words,
\pi
induces an isomorphism
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=1}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
.

Furthermore, over the locus
\{|t|=1\}\subseteq\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
, the normed generalised Cartier divisor
t:L\rightarrow A
identifies with the identity on
A
and hence

\GSpec\Cone(\overline{L}\rightarrow\overline{A})=\emptyset=\GSpec\Cone(L\rightarrow A)\;.

Consequently, over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=1}^{\mathrm{N}}
, we have
D^{+}\cong D
and this implies that
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=1}\cong X{\Delta}
via
\pi
for any Gelfand stack
X
. The corresponding closed embedding of
X{\Delta}
into
X^{\mathrm{N}}
is denoted by

j_{\mathrm{dR}}:X{\Delta}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}\;.

4.2.2.
The map
j_{\mathrm{HT}}

Now let us study the locus
\{|u|=1\}
inside
X^{\mathrm{N}}
. Unwinding definitions, we see that an
A
-point of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|=1}^{\mathrm{N}}
for
A
totally disconnected nilperfectoid corresponds to a degree
1
divisor
D\subseteq Y_{A}
together with a lift
t:L\rightarrow A
of the normed generalised Cartier divisor
D\times_{Y_{A}}\GSpec\overline{A}
on
\overline{A}
to
A
. First noting that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}}

is cartesian (both the top and the bottom row are
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
-torsors), we see that it is equivalent to just lift
D\times_{Y_{A}}\GSpec\overline{A}
from
\overline{A}
to
A
as a divisor, i.e. forgetting the norm.

Moreover, by
3.16
, we see that the divisor

D\times_{Y_{A}}\GSpec\overline{A}\subseteq\GSpec\overline{A}

has trivial ideal sheaf, hence
L\cong A
and we just have to lift the global section cutting out
D\times_{Y_{A}}\GSpec\overline{A}
from
\overline{A}
to
A
. As
D
is also just cut out by an element of some
\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)
by loc. cit., the pullback diagram

(4.2.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r+1},p^{r+1}]}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

from the proof of
3.11
shows that we may repackage the data
(D\subseteq Y_{A},t:A\rightarrow A)
as a divisor
D^{\prime}\subseteq Y_{A}
whose Frobenius pullback identifies with
D
. Moreover, note that
D^{\prime}
has degree
1
if and only if
D
has degree
1
as this may be checked after pullback to
Y_{\overline{A}}
, where Frobenius is an isomorphism.

What about
D^{+}
? By
4.3
, the Gelfand stack
D^{+}
is affine and there is a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(D^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cone(L\rightarrow A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cone(\overline{L}\rightarrow\overline{A})\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

where the vertical map on the right just comes from pulling back a function on
D
to a function on

D\times_{Y_{A}}\GSpec\overline{A}\cong\GSpec\Cone(\overline{L}\rightarrow\overline{A})\;.

Letting
\xi\in\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r+1},p^{r+1}]}(A)
denote the function cutting out
D^{\prime}
as above, we can rewrite the pullback diagram above as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(D^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r},p^{r}]}(A)/F(\xi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A/\theta(\xi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{A}/\theta(\xi)}

and hence (
4.2.1
) shows that
\mathcal{O}(D^{+})\cong\mathbb{B}^{\dagger}_{[p^{-r+1},p^{r+1}]}(A)/\xi
, i.e.
D^{+}\cong D^{\prime}
. Overall, this discussion yields the following description of
X_{|u|=1}^{\mathrm{N}}
:

Proposition 4.8
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, there is an isomorphism

X{\Delta}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=1}

whose postcomposition with …

(i)

…
\pi:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
identifies with the Frobenius on
X{\Delta}
,

(ii)

…
t:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\xrightarrow{t}\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
identifies with
\mu:X{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\xrightarrow{\mu}\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
.

We denote the corresponding closed embedding of
X{\Delta}
into
X^{\mathrm{N}}
by

j_{\mathrm{HT}}:X{\Delta}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}\;.

4.2.3.
The locus
\{|ut|\neq 0\}

We now want to describe the locus
\{|ut|\neq 0\}
inside
X^{\mathrm{N}}
. For this, recall that
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\cong(0,1]
by
2.22
and that
(0,1]^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong(0,1]
by
2.32
and idempotency of the functor
(-)^{\mathrm{dR}}
. Furthermore recalling that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is actually supported over
\{|ut|<1\}
by
4.2
, we see that, over the locus
\{|ut|\neq 0\}
, the pullback diagram defining
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
simplifies drastically to yield

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p,|ut|\neq 0}^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p,|\widetilde{\mu}|\neq 0}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((0,1]\times(0,1])\setminus\{(1,1)\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0,1)\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

To move on, note that there is an isomorphism

(0,1)\times[0,1]\xrightarrow{\cong}((0,1]\times(0,1])\setminus\{(1,1)\}

given by sending a pair
(x,y)
to the unique intersection point
(t,u)
of the hyperbola
\{ut=x\}
and the line through
(1,1)
and
(1-y,y)
. Twisting the above pullback diagram by this isomorphism, we obtain the following result:

Proposition 4.9
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, there is an isomorphism

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|ut|\neq 0}\cong(X{\Delta}\setminus X^{\mathrm{dR}})\times[0,1]\;,

where we view
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
as a closed substack of
X{\Delta}
via
i_{\mathrm{dR}}
. Moreover, this isomorphism identifies …

(i)

…
\pi
with the first projection,

(ii)

…the restriction of
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
to
X{\Delta}\setminus X^{\mathrm{dR}}
with
\id\times\{0\}
,

(iii)

…the restriction of
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
to
X{\Delta}\setminus X^{\mathrm{HT}}
with
\varphi\times\{1\}
.

Proof.

The above discussion already shows that

\mathbb{Q}_{p,|ut|\neq 0}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,|\widetilde{\mu}|\neq 0}{\Delta}\times[0,1]\;.

Moreover, since the generalised Cartier divisor
t:L\rightarrow A
is an isomorphism over
|t|\neq 0
, we see that
D^{+}\cong D
over
\{|ut|\neq 0\}
, which implies the above isomorphism for any Gelfand stack
X
in place of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The only remaining claim is that
X{\Delta}_{|\widetilde{\mu}|\neq 0}\cong X{\Delta}\setminus X^{\mathrm{dR}}
or, in other words, that
X^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong X{\Delta}_{|\widetilde{\mu}|=0}
via
i_{\mathrm{dR}}
. By construction, this reduces to the case
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, where we observe that
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|\widetilde{\mu}|=0}{\Delta}
is the moduli space of degree
1
Cartier divisors
D\subseteq Y_{A}
equipped with a factorisation of
\varphi^{-1}\circ\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{A}
through
D
. However, as
\varphi^{-1}\circ\iota
already defines a degree
1
Cartier divisor itself, such a factorisation is actually an isomorphism
D\cong\GSpec\overline{A}
by rigidity, see
3.15
, and this implies the claim.
∎

4.2.4.
The map
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}

Let us now study the locus
\{|u|=0\}
inside
X^{\mathrm{N}}
. For this, first note that
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|=0}^{\mathrm{N}}
sits inside a pullback square

(4.2.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|=0}^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

where we have used that
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|\widetilde{\mu}|=0}{\Delta}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by the proof of
4.9
and the vertical map
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
on the right is given by the composition

\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR}}\xrightarrow{i_{\mathrm{dR}}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\mu}}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\;,

which factors through
*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
. We warn the reader that this map is
not
the canonical map
*\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
! To understand this map more explicitly, let us first establish the following moduli description of
*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
:

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack. Then there is an equivalence between

\{\text{maps }X\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\}\cong\{\text{normed line bundles on $X^{\diamondsuit}$ for the arc-topology}\}\;.

In particular, a map
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
corresponds to a
\mathbb{C}_{p}
-linear continuous character of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
of norm
1
, i.e. a continuous group morphism
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\times}
.

Proof.

Since
!
-covers induce arc-covers on diamonds, we are immediately reduced to the case
X=\GSpec B
for
B
nilperfectoid by descent. Then a
B
-valued point of
*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
amounts to a
\overline{B}
-valued point of
*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\diamondsuit}
, which is equivalent to a normed line bundle on
\GSpec\overline{B}
for the arc-topology almost by definition. The final assertion follows from the fact that
\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}\cong\Spd\mathbb{C}_{p}/\underline{\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
as all line bundles on
\Spd\mathbb{C}_{p}
are trivial.
∎

Under the identification from the previous lemma, the map
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
classifies the cyclotomic character
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, which in terms of line bundles is the Tate twist. Indeed, for
\overline{A}=\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
, pulling back the ideal sheaf of the divisor
\varphi^{-1}\circ\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow Y_{\overline{A}}
classified by
i_{\mathrm{dR}}
along
\varphi^{-1}\circ\iota
, we obtain the invertible
\overline{A}
-module

p\log[\varepsilon]W(\overline{A}^{\flat})[\tfrac{1}{p}]/(p\log[\varepsilon])^{2}W(\overline{A}^{\flat})[\tfrac{1}{p}]\;,

where
\varepsilon=(1,\zeta_{p},\zeta_{p^{2}},\dots)
, as usual, and
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
acts on this via the cyclotomic character.

Returning to the pullback diagram (
4.2.2
), this tells us that
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|=0}^{\mathrm{N}}
classifies triples
(D\subseteq Y_{A},t:L\rightarrow A,u:\overline{A}\rightarrow K)
for a totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
together with an identification between
u
and the zero map as well as an isomorphism
L\otimes_{A}K^{\vee}\cong\overline{A}(1)
, which we may rewrite as
K\cong\overline{L}(-1)
. In other words, we see that the map

i_{\mathrm{dR},+}:\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}

given by sending a normed generalised Cartier divisor
t:L\rightarrow A
on a totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
to the triple
(\GSpec\overline{A}\subseteq Y_{A},t:L\rightarrow A,\overline{A}\xrightarrow{0}\overline{L}(-1))
is an isomorphism onto
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|=0}^{\mathrm{N}}
.

What happens to
D^{+}
after pullback along
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}
? By
4.3
, the Gelfand stack
D^{+}
is affine and there is a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}(D^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cone(L\rightarrow A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cone(\overline{L}\rightarrow\overline{A})\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

where we have used that
D\cong\GSpec\overline{A}
after pullback along
i_{\mathrm{dR}}
and the vertical map on the right is the canonical map. Thus, we conclude that

D^{+}\cong\GSpec\Cone(L\otimes_{A}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\rightarrow A)\;,

whence the map
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}:\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
induces a map

i_{\mathrm{dR},+}:X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}\;.

The above discussion then shows the following:

Proposition 4.11
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, the map
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}
induces an isomorphism

X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=0}\;.

4.2.5.
The locus
\{|t|=0\}

Finally, let us shortly discuss the locus
\{|t|=0\}
inside
X^{\mathrm{N}}
. In some sense, this is the most mysterious and not directly comparable to any of the stacks we already know. Still, let us at least give it a name.

Definition 4.12
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, we write

X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}\coloneqq X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0}

and
X^{\mathrm{HT},+}
for the base change of
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
along
\{t=0\}\rightarrow\{|t|=0\}
.

The above notation is motivated by the fact that
X^{\mathrm{HT},+}
is a filtered refinement of the Hodge–Tate stack while
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
is a filtered refinement of the overconvergent neighbourhood

X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\coloneqq(X^{\mathrm{HT}}\subseteq X{\Delta})^{\dagger}=X{\Delta}_{|\mu|=0}

of
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
inside
X{\Delta}
. Indeed, we can at least prove the following.

Proposition 4.13
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, there is an isomorphism

X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}_{|u|\neq 0}\cong X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\times(0,1]

intertwining …

(i)

…the map
\id\times\{1\}:X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\times(0,1]
with
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
,

(ii)

…the map
\varphi\circ\mathrm{pr}_{1}:X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\times(0,1]\rightarrow X{\Delta}
with
\pi
.

In particular, we have
X^{\mathrm{HT},+}_{|u|\neq 0}\cong X^{\mathrm{HT}}\times(0,1]
.

Proof.

We first consider the case
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, where the definition of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
supplies a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|\neq 0}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(0,1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

where we have used that
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\cong(0,1]
by
2.22
and that
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|\widetilde{\mu}|=0}{\Delta}\cong\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
via
i_{\mathrm{dR}}
as in the proof of
4.9
. As the horizontal map on the bottom agrees with

\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(0,1]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\;,

the claim now follows from the fact that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}

is cartesian by
4.8
. The case of general
X
now follows by verifying that the pullback of
D^{+}
to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|=1}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
identifies with the universal degree
1
Cartier divisor
D
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
, but this is similarly deduced from the proof of
4.8
.
∎

4.3.
Syntomification

Finally, we can define the syntomification. As already announced above, for this, we will glue the two copies of
X{\Delta}
embedded into
X^{\mathrm{N}}
via
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
, respectively.

Definition 4.14
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack. The (rational)
analytic syntomification
of
X
is the Gelfand stack
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
defined by the coequaliser diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\;.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{HT}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}}

An object of the category
\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
is called an
analytic (prismatic)
F
-gauge
on
X
.

The most important example of an analytic
F
-gauge is the Breuil–Kisin twist. Recall from
3.22
that we have already defined the Breuil–Kisin twist on
X{\Delta}
.

Definition 4.15
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack. The
Breuil–Kisin twist
is the line bundle
\mathcal{O}\{1\}
on
X^{\mathrm{N}}
defined by

\mathcal{O}\{1\}\cong\pi^{*}\mathcal{O}\{1\}\otimes t^{*}\mathcal{O}\langle-1\rangle\;,

where we recall that
t:\mathcal{O}\langle-1\rangle\rightarrow\mathcal{O}
is the universal normed generalised Cartier divisor on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
. It descends to
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and we also use
\mathcal{O}\{1\}
to denote this descended line bundle.

Indeed, to see that the Breuil–Kisin twist descends to
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, one calculates

j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{*}\mathcal{O}\{1\}\cong\mathcal{O}\{1\}\cong\varphi^{*}\mathcal{O}\{1\}\otimes\mathcal{I}\cong j_{\mathrm{HT}}^{*}\mathcal{O}\{1\}

using
4.8
as well as the fact that
t\circ j_{\mathrm{HT}}=\mu
classifies the Hodge–Tate divisor while
t\circ j_{\mathrm{dR}}
factors through
\overline{\mathbb{T}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
. With this, we can finally define analytic syntomic cohomology of Gelfand stacks in our sense.

Definition 4.16
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack. For any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
analytic syntomic cohomology
of weight
i
of
X
is defined as

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))\coloneqq R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},\mathcal{O}\{i\})\;.

As already hinted at by the terminology in the above definition, there is a general notion of
Hodge–Tate weights
for perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, which we shall now define. For this, note that precomposing the map
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}
with the canonical map
X^{\mathrm{Hod}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
yields a map

i_{\mathrm{Hod}}:X^{\mathrm{Hod}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}\;.

Moreover, recall from
2.20
that perfect complexes on
X\times*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
identify with graded perfect complexes on
X
.

Definition 4.17
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack and
E
a perfect complex on
X^{\mathrm{N}}
. Identifying the pullback of
E
along

X\times*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Hod}}\xrightarrow{i_{\mathrm{Hod}}}X^{\mathrm{N}}

with a graded perfect complex
M^{\bullet}
on
X
, the
Hodge–Tate weights
of
E
are defined to be those integers
i\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
M^{i}
is nonzero. The Hodge–Tate weights of a perfect analytic
F
-gauge
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
on
X
are defined to be the Hodge–Tate weights of its pullback to
X^{\mathrm{N}}
.

Example 4.18
.

The line bundle
\mathcal{O}\{i\}
only has a single Hodge–Tate weight, which is equal to
-i
. Indeed, one easily calculates
i_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{*}\mathcal{O}\{1\}\cong\mathcal{O}\langle-1\rangle
and this yields the claim.
∎

4.4.
Compatibility with étale localisation

We now want to prove compatibility of the functor
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
with Berkovich étale localisation. For this, we first extend the definition of Berkovich étale maps between derived Berkovich spaces from
[
1
, Def. 4.3.4.(3)]
to maps between arbitrary Gelfand stacks as follows.

Definition 4.19
.

Let
X\rightarrow Y
be a map of Gelfand stacks. It is called
Berkovich étale
if
X\times_{Y}\GSpec A
is a derived Berkovich space for any totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
with a map
\GSpec A\rightarrow Y
and the map

X\times_{Y}\GSpec A\rightarrow\GSpec A

is a Berkovich étale map of derived Berkovich spaces in the sense of
[
1
, Def. 4.3.4.(3)]
.

Similarly, we can define finite étale maps between Gelfand stacks as well as open embeddings and rational localisations, and we note that all these notions will be stable under base change as an immediate consequence of the definition. With this notation in place, the result we want to prove reads as follows.

Theorem 4.20
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a Berkovich étale map of derived Berkovich spaces. Then the induced map
f^{\mathrm{N}}:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
is Berkovich étale as well. Moreover, if
f
is a
!
-cover, then the same is true for the map
f^{\mathrm{N}}
.

Note that, according to the discussion of various loci inside
X^{\mathrm{N}}
above, the theorem above implies the corresponding statement for all other stacks associated to
p
-adic cohomology theories we have introduced so far, e.g.
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
or
X{\Delta}
, by suitable base changes.

To prove
4.20
, recall that a Berkovich étale map is defined as being finite étale locally in a strict closed cover of the source and target by rational localisations. Thus, the theorem we want to prove breaks up into two parts and we start by treating finite étale maps. Before we begin the proof, let us mention that the key is that the finite étale site is invariant under tilting and under
\dagger
-reduction, see
[
1
, Prop. 3.2.11]
for the latter, together with the fact that finite étale maps are
\dagger
-formally étale.

Proposition 4.21
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a finite étale map of affine Gelfand stacks. Then the induced map
f^{\mathrm{N}}:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
is finite étale. In particular,
f^{\mathrm{N}}
is a
!
-cover.

Proof.

We have to show that, for any totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
with a map
\GSpec A\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
, the Gelfand stack
X^{\mathrm{N}}\times_{Y^{\mathrm{N}}}\GSpec A
is affine and represented by a finite étale
A
-algebra. Thus, take any
A
-valued point of
Y^{\mathrm{N}}
lying over an
A
-valued point
(D\subseteq Y_{A},L\rightarrow A,\overline{A}\rightarrow K)
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
. Then the given
A
-valued point of
Y^{\mathrm{N}}
corresponds to a map
D^{+}\rightarrow Y
, which by
4.3
is equivalent to compatible maps
D\rightarrow Y
and
\GSpec\Cone(L\rightarrow A)\rightarrow Y
. In particular, pulling back
D
to
Y_{\overline{A}}
, we obtain an untilt
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}
of
\overline{A}
with a map to
Y
and pulling this back along the map
X\rightarrow Y
will yield a finite étale perfectoid
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}
-algebra
\overline{B}^{\flat\sharp}
, which will correspond to a unique finite étale perfectoid
\overline{A}
-algebra
\overline{B}
, as the notation suggests, since the finite étale site is invariant under tilting. Finally, by
[
1
, Prop. 3.2.11]
, this is the reduction of a unique nilperfectoid finite étale
A
-algebra
B
, which will in fact be totally disconnected (as it is finite étale over a totally disconnected ring).

Now recall from
3.8
that the map
Y_{B}\rightarrow Y_{A}
is finite étale. Thus, applying loc. cit. once again shows that
D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{B}\cong D\times_{Y}X
since both of these lift the finite étale
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}
-algebra
\overline{B}^{\flat\sharp}
to a finite étale Gelfand stack over
D
and hence the degree
1
divisor
D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{B}\subseteq Y_{B}
is equipped with a map to
X
. In other words, we have constructed a
B
-point of
X{\Delta}
fitting into a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y{\Delta}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

We will now further produce a similar diagram for Nygaardifications. For this, observe that the data we have produced so far already yields a map

(4.4.1)

\GSpec\Cone(L\otimes_{A}\overline{B}\rightarrow\overline{B})\rightarrow X

by construction of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
and the only missing piece is a lift of this to a map

(4.4.2)

\GSpec\Cone(L\otimes_{A}B\rightarrow B)\rightarrow X\;.

However, note that, again,
\Cone(L\otimes_{A}\overline{B}\rightarrow\overline{B})
is a finite étale
\Cone(L\otimes_{A}\overline{A}\rightarrow\overline{A})
-algebra that can uniquely be lifted to a finite étale
\Cone(L\rightarrow A)
-algebra, from which we conclude that there is an isomorphism

\GSpec\Cone(L\otimes_{A}B\rightarrow B)\cong\GSpec\Cone(L\rightarrow A)\times_{Y}X

as both the left- and the right-hand side are possible lifts. Via the expression on the right-hand side we obtain a map as in (
4.4.2
) which is indeed compatible with the map (
4.4.1
) we are already given and thus, we overall obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\mathrm{N}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Let us check that this diagram is cartesian. For this, we take a totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
-algebra
C
and have to verify that any
C
-valued point of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
compatible with the given
A
-valued point of
Y^{\mathrm{N}}
uniquely comes from a map
B\rightarrow C
via the diagram above. This
C
-valued point of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
corresponds to compatible maps
D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{C}\rightarrow X
and
\GSpec\Cone(L\otimes_{A}C\rightarrow C)\rightarrow X
. By compatibility with the given
A
-point of
Y^{\mathrm{N}}
, the first of these maps induces a map

D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{C}\rightarrow D\times_{Y}X\cong D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{B}\;,

where the map
D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{C}\rightarrow D
is the first projection, and passing to
\dagger
-reductions on both sides induces a map
\overline{B}^{\flat\sharp}\rightarrow\overline{C}^{\flat\sharp}
for the unique untilt
\overline{C}^{\flat\sharp}
of
\overline{C}^{\flat}
over
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}
. By tilting, we obtain a unique map
\overline{B}\rightarrow\overline{C}
and hence a composite map
B\rightarrow\overline{C}
. However, as
A\rightarrow B
is finite étale and hence
\dagger
-formally étale, the map
B\rightarrow\overline{C}
lifts uniquely to a map
B\rightarrow C
of
A
-algebras.

Finally, we have to check that the maps
D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{C}\rightarrow X
and
\GSpec\Cone(L\otimes_{A}C\rightarrow C)\rightarrow X
are the same as the ones obtained via pullback from the
B
-valued point of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
we have constructed above. However, by construction, they agree after passing to the
\dagger
-reduction of the source and then the fact that
X\rightarrow Y
is
\dagger
-formally étale implies that they must be the same as they both lift the given
A
-point of
Y^{\mathrm{N}}
.
∎

Let us move on to the case of open covers and rational localisations. Now the key is that the Berkovich space is invariant under tilting and
\dagger
-nilpotent thickenings.

Proposition 4.22
.

Let
X\rightarrow Y
be an open embedding of derived Berkovich spaces. Then the induced map
X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
is an open embedding as well. Moreover, if
\{X_{i}\rightarrow Y\}_{i\in I}
is a family of open localisations which jointly cover
Y
, then the maps
\{X_{i}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}\}_{i\in I}
also jointly cover
Y^{\mathrm{N}}
. The same is true for rational localisations and strict closed covers.

Proof.

For
A
totally disconnected nilperfectoid, take any
A
-valued point of
Y^{\mathrm{N}}
, which corresponds to an
A
-valued point
(D\subseteq Y_{A},L\rightarrow A,\overline{A}\rightarrow K)
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
together with compatible maps
D\rightarrow Y
and
\GSpec\Cone(L\rightarrow A)\rightarrow Y
. In particular, the pullback of
D
to
Y_{\overline{A}}
yields an untilt
\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp}
of
\overline{A}^{\flat}
and we let
U
be the open subspace of
\GSpec A
defined by the open subset

(4.4.3)

|D|\times_{|Y|}|X|\cong\mathcal{M}(\overline{A}^{\flat\sharp})\times_{|Y|}|X|\cong\mathcal{M}(A)\times_{|Y|}|X|\subseteq\mathcal{M}(A)\;,

where we have used that the Berkovich space is invariant under tilting and
\dagger
-nilpotent thickenings. We claim that there is a cartesian diagram

(4.4.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\mathrm{N}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

For this, first note that, by
[
1
, Lem. 4.4.5]
, there is a map
Y_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(A)
with the property that the preimage of any closed subset
Z\subseteq\mathcal{M}(A)
is given by
Y_{\overline{A}_{Z}}\subseteq Y_{\overline{A}}
, where
\overline{A}_{Z}
denotes the rational localisation of
\overline{A}
corresponding to
Z\subseteq\mathcal{M}(A)
. Thus, by construction, we have

D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{U}\cong D\times_{\mathcal{M}(A)}|U|\cong D\times_{Y}X

and, moreover,

\GSpec\Cone(L\rightarrow A)\times_{\GSpec A}U\cong\GSpec\Cone(L\rightarrow A)\times_{\mathcal{M}(A)}|U|\cong\GSpec\Cone(L\rightarrow A)\times_{Y}X\;,

where we use that there is a map
\GSpec A\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(A)
, see
[
1
, §3.2]
. These two isomorphisms provide compatible maps
D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{U}\rightarrow X
and
\GSpec\Cone(L\rightarrow A)\times_{\GSpec A}U\rightarrow X
which induce a map
U\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}
making the diagram (
4.4.4
) commute.

Let us check that the diagram is cartesian. For this, we take a totally disconnected
A
-algebra
C
together with a
C
-point of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
arising from compatible maps
D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{C}\rightarrow X
and
\GSpec\Cone(L\otimes_{A}C\rightarrow C)\rightarrow X
which lifts the given
A
-point of
Y^{\mathrm{N}}
. However, letting
\overline{C}^{\flat\sharp}
denote the untilt of
\overline{C}^{\flat}
obtained from the
\dagger
-reduction of
D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{C}
, we see that
\mathcal{M}(\overline{C}^{\flat\sharp})\cong\mathcal{M}(C)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(A)
must factor through
|U|
by definition and hence
\GSpec C\rightarrow\GSpec A
factors through
U
. Moreover, the given maps
D\times_{Y_{A}}Y_{C}\rightarrow X
and
\GSpec\Cone(L\otimes_{A}C\rightarrow C)\rightarrow X
must be the same as those arising via pullback from
U
: Indeed, both are compatible with the given
A
-point of
Y^{\mathrm{N}}
, i.e. they yield the same maps after postcomposing with
X\rightarrow Y
, and the fact that
X\rightarrow Y
is an open embedding implies that derived Berkovich spaces over
X
embed fully faithfully into derived Berkovich spaces over
Y
. This shows that (
4.4.4
) is indeed cartesian and hence
X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
is an open localisations, as desired.

Now assume that
\{X_{i}\rightarrow Y\}_{i\in I}
form an open cover. Pulling back to a totally disconnected nilperfectoid
\GSpec A
as above, we get open subspaces
U_{i}
of
\GSpec A
with the property that

|U_{i}|=\mathcal{M}(A)\times_{|Y|}|X_{i}|

by the above argument. As the maps
\{|X_{i}|\rightarrow|Y|\}_{i\in I}
are jointly surjective, we deduce the same for the maps
\{|U_{i}|\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(A)\}_{i\in I}
, and we can even reduce to
|I|<\infty
by compactness of
\mathcal{M}(A)
. Finally, by our standing qfd assumption, we know that
\mathcal{M}(A)
has finite cohomological dimension and hence the family of maps
\{|U_{i}|\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(A)\}_{i\in I}
forms a
!
-cover by
[
46
, Prop. II.1.1]
. As the maps
U_{i}\rightarrow\GSpec A
are pulled back from the maps
|U_{i}|\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(A)
, this implies the claim.

Finally, we discuss the case of rational localisations. For this, we may reduce to the case of rational localisations of the form
\{|f|\leq 1\}
or
\{|f|\geq 1\}
for some global function
f
on
Y
, and we only discuss the first case, the second one is similar. In that case, note that

\{|f|\leq 1\}=\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{\varepsilon>0}\,\{|f|<1+\varepsilon\}\;,

hence we can write
X=\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{\varepsilon>0}X_{\varepsilon}
for
X_{\varepsilon}\coloneqq\{|f|<1+\varepsilon\}\subseteq Y
. As
(-)^{\mathrm{N}}
commutes with limits and is also compatible with open localisations by the argument above, we conclude that any
A
-valued point
\GSpec A\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
as above gives rise to a cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{\varepsilon>0}U_{\varepsilon}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\mathrm{N}}}

with open subspaces
U_{\varepsilon}\subseteq\GSpec A
. Moreover, by the description of these open subspaces from (
4.4.3
), we see that
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{\varepsilon>0}U_{\varepsilon}
is a rational localisation of
\GSpec A
, and this is what we wanted to show. As the preservation of strict closed covers now follows from the preservation of open covers, this concludes the proof.
∎

As already discussed above, the two previous propositions imply
4.20
since a Berkovich étale map is defined as being finite étale locally in a strict closed cover of the source and target by rational localisations.

4.5.
Nice coverability of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}

To end the section, we prove that
\mathbb{Q}_{p,[r,s]}^{\mathrm{N}}
is nicely coverable in the sense of
2.11
whenever
[r,s]\subseteq(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})
. This will be the key technical input powering our comparison theorems in §
8
and §
9
.

Proposition 4.23
.

For any
[r,s]\subseteq(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})
, the Gelfand stack
\mathbb{Q}_{p,[r,s]}^{\mathrm{N}}
is nicely coverable.

Proof.

First note that
\mathbb{Q}_{p,[r,s]}{\Delta}\cong Y_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p},[r,s]}^{\mathrm{dR}}
by
3.23
and recall that

Y_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong Y_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\,/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}\;.

The quotient stack

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}

clearly has a
!
-cover by
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}
whose Čech nerve is given by

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)\times(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}})^{\bullet}\;,

where we note that
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}})=C^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a flat
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra by
2.15
. Moreover, note that the endomorphism
q\mapsto q^{p}
of
1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}
is
\dagger
-formally étale and therefore

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\,\big/\,\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\;.

As also
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger})=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{n}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle p^{-n}T\rangle
is a flat
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra, the Čech cover

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)\times(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger})^{\bullet}\rightarrow\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}

provides a nice hypercover of the base change of
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}
to
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
due to the fact that
\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{[r,s]}
is affine and flat over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Indeed, in
5.4
below, we will compute that the preimage of
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
in the stack
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}
is cut out by the inequality

p^{-s}\leq\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{n}|q^{1/p^{n}}-1|^{p^{n-1}(p-1)}\leq p^{-r}\;,

where we normalise the norm such that
|p|=1/p
, and we note that this is actually equivalent to the inequalities

p^{-s}\leq|q^{1/p^{n}}-1|^{p^{2}(p-1)}\leq p^{-r}

for all
n\geq 3
due to
|(1+t)^{p}-1|=|t|^{p}
whenever
p^{-1/p}<|t|<1
. Thus, we have

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{[r,s]}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{n\geq 3,q\mapsto q^{p}}\left(1+\overline{\mathbb{T}}\left(p^{-s/(p^{n-1}(p-1))},p^{-r/(p^{n-1}(p-1))}\right)\right)\;,

where
\overline{\mathbb{T}}(a,b)
denotes the overconvergent torus of inner radius
a
and outer radius
b
for any
a<b
, and the right-hand side is affine, static and flat over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Putting everything together and applying
2.14
and
2.13
, we see that
\mathbb{Q}_{p,[r,s]}{\Delta}
is nicely coverable and in fact we have even found a nice hypercover where all the algebras that occur are static and flat over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Now note that
(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
is nicely coverable: Indeed, the Čech nerve of the map
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
is given by
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}})^{\bullet}
; moreover,
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
and
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
are themselves covered by
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
and
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
, respectively, with the respective Čech nerves being given by

\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{\bullet}\;,\hskip 8.5359pt\text{resp.}\hskip 8.5359pt\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger})^{\bullet}\;.

As
\overline{\mathbb{D}},\overline{\mathbb{T}},\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
and
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
are all affine static Gelfand stacks which are flat over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by
2.15
, this yields that
(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
is nicely coverable by
2.12
and
2.13
. Clearly, also
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is nicely coverable by the Čech cover
\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\bullet}
. Again, note that we have actually produced nice hypercovers which only involve static flat
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebras.

Denoting by
X_{\bullet}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,[r,s]}{\Delta}
and
Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
the nice hypercovers we have produced above, the definition of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
2.13
now reduce us to showing that all
X_{\bullet}\times_{(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}}Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}
are nicely coverable by
n
-truncated affines for some fixed
n\geq 0
. However, note that the maps
X_{\bullet}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
and
Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
all canonically factor through
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
and hence we obtain

\begin{split}X_{\bullet}\times_{(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}}Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}&\cong X_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times_{(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}}\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}})\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\\
&\cong X_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}})\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\\
&\cong X_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times Y_{\bullet^{\prime}})\;,\end{split}

where the map
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
is given by the composition

\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\xrightarrow{\text{mult}}\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\;.

By another application of
2.13
, we may now check everything after passing to the nice hypercover
\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger})^{\bullet^{\prime\prime}}
of
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
from above. However, note that we can make each term in this hypercover act on
Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}
such that the map
Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
becomes equivariant (e.g. by first projecting onto
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
and then letting
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
act by multiplication on one of the factors
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
in
Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}
) and, after twisting by this action, we may compute the fibre product above by replacing the map
(\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger})^{\bullet^{\prime\prime}})\times Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
by

(\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger})^{\bullet^{\prime\prime}})\times Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\;.

In particular, we may focus on the fibre products
X_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}
because the additional factors
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
and
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
do not play any further role for our argument due to
\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathbb{T}})
and
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger})
being static and flat over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Now we can do a similar trick again: the maps
X_{\bullet}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
and
Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
admit a canonical factorisation through
\overline{\mathbb{D}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
, which tells us that

X_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\cong X_{\bullet}\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times Y_{\bullet^{\prime}})\;,

where the map
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}
is given by

\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times Y_{\bullet^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{add}}\overline{\mathbb{D}}\;.

Finally, note that up to base changing to
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
, the above pullback is of the form

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}\setminus\{1\})\right)\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}})\times(\,\dots)\;,

where the term in brackets is a finite product of affine Gelfand stacks which are flat and static over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and hence is irrelevant for our argument.

Summarising, we are done once we know that, for some
n\geq 0
, the single (!) pullback

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}\setminus\{1\})\right)\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}})\;,

where the map
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}
is given by
(x,y,z)\mapsto x+yz
, is an affine
n
-truncated Gelfand stack after base changing to
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
. However, as we have already shown that
\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{[r,s]}
is affine and static, this just follows from the fact that the map
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}
is flat: On rings, this is given by

\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle_{\leq 1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{X\}^{\dagger}\langle Y,Z\rangle_{\leq 1}\;,\hskip 8.5359ptT\mapsto X+YZ

and both the map

\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle_{\leq 1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{X\}^{\dagger}\langle W\rangle_{\leq 1}\;,\hskip 8.5359ptT\mapsto X+W

as well as the map

\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle W\rangle_{\leq 1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle Y,Z\rangle_{\leq 1}\;,\hskip 8.5359ptW\mapsto YZ

are flat. Indeed, for the first map, this follows from
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{X\}^{\dagger}\langle W\rangle_{\leq 1}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{X\}^{\dagger}\langle X+W\rangle_{\leq 1}
and flatness of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{X\}^{\dagger}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
while, for the second map, we observe that, as a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle W\rangle_{\leq 1}
-module, we have

\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle Y,Z\rangle_{\leq 1}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle W,Y\rangle_{\leq 1}\oplus Z\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle W,Z\rangle_{\leq 1}

and then the claim follows from flatness of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle Y\rangle_{\leq 1}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by base change.
∎

Remark 4.24
.

Modifying the above argument slightly even yields nice coverability of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,[r,s]}^{\mathrm{N}}
for any
[r,s]\subseteq(1,\infty)
. Moreover, one can also prove the same for
[r,s]\subseteq(0,p)
using that

\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}\cong\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(0,p)}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\;,

which one may deduce by combining
3.28
and
3.29
. However, we will only need the case
[r,s]\subseteq(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})
in the sequel.
∎

5.
Nygaardifications of smooth derived Berkovich spaces

Our aim in this section is to find explicit local presentations of some Nygaardifications. While the need for this might not be fully apparent at the moment, having such explicit presentations will be very useful later when we want to show that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is cohomologically smooth and that the functor
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
carries rigid smooth maps of derived Berkovich spaces to cohomologically smooth maps and quasicompact locally finitely presented maps of derived Berkovich spaces to weakly cohomologically proper maps.

Let us give a brief idea of what we would like to do. We immediately warn the reader that this will ultimately
not
quite work and cause us to work with a slight variant of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
instead, but it still seems valuable to explain why we introduce this variant and why it is easier to handle than
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
itself. Trying to find an explicit local presentation of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
, we first think about
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
: Recalling the presentations

\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\;,\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}

from
3.29
and
3.30
and the relation between
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
and the stacks
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
, we are led to assume that there should be a surjection

(5.0.1)

\rho_{q\mathrm{dR}}:\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\;.

Indeed, such a map exists: For any totally disconnected nilperfectoid ring
A
and any sequence
(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)
of
p
-power roots in
1+A^{<1}
not equal to
(1,1,1,\dots)
, i.e. any
A
-point of the source, we obtain a global section
[q^{\flat}]
of
Y_{A}
by gluing
[q^{\flat}]\in\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\overline{A})
, where
q^{\flat}=(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)\in\overline{A}^{\flat\circ}
, and the element
(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)\in\prod_{n=0}^{\infty}A
; moreover,
[q^{\flat}]\in H^{0}(Y_{A},\mathcal{O})
has canonical
p
-power roots obtained by shifting the sequence
(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)
we started with. Then the section

\frac{[q^{\flat}]-1}{[q^{\flat}]^{1/p}-1}=1+[q^{\flat}]^{1/p}+\dots+[q^{\flat}]^{(p-1)/p}\in H^{0}(Y_{A},\mathcal{O})

cuts out a degree
1
Cartier divisor in
Y_{A}
and this defines the map (
5.0.1
) above. We remark that one should think of
\rho_{q\mathrm{dR}}
as analogous to the surjection from the
q
-de Rham prism onto the algebraic prismatisation of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in the sense of Bhatt–Lurie and Drinfeld, see
[
10
, §3.8]
.

One checks that the map (
5.0.1
) is compatible with the presentations of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
above and hence, base changing to
(1,\infty)\subseteq(0,\infty)
, we obtain a map

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(1,\infty)}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(1,\infty)}{\Delta}

which presents the target as the quotient of the source by
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
. How can we obtain a presentation of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(1,\infty)}^{\mathrm{N}}
from here? For this, let us first observe that the postcomposition of the map above with
\widetilde{\mu}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
can alternatively be factored as

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(1,\infty)}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\;,

where the first map is given by

(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)\mapsto\frac{q^{p}-1}{q-1}=1+q+\dots+q^{p-1}\;.

Thus, ignoring the quotient by
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
for the moment, we can compute the successive pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}_{-})\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(1,\infty)}\,\Big/\,\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(1,\infty)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}_{-})/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

and the top left term, in which
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
only acts on the product
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}_{-}
, will be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
-torsor over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(1,\infty)}^{\mathrm{N}}
. Can we also incorporate the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
-action? In principle, we would now like to write a formula like

\mathbb{Q}_{p,(1,\infty)}^{\mathrm{N}}\overset{?}{\cong}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}})\times_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(1,\infty)}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;,

where
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
acts on
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
via the quotient
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
while
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
acts trivially on
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
and by multiplication or division on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
or
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
, respectively. However, for the formula above to make sense, we at least also have to prescribe an action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
as
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
already acts nontrivially on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
through the action of
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
. Moreover, this action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
on the product
\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
has to be such that the induced action on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
under the multiplication map agrees with the action induced from
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
. This latter task, however, seems at least very hard, if not impossible: The
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
-action on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
induced via the map
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
is given by

\gamma.\frac{q^{p}-1}{q-1}=\frac{q^{p\gamma}-1}{q^{\gamma}-1}

and it is not even clear how to rewrite the right-hand side purely in terms of
\frac{q^{p}-1}{q-1}
, i.e. without explicit reference to
q
.

Summarising, the trouble with the above approach seems to come from the fact that the map

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}\;,\hskip 8.5359pt(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)\mapsto\frac{q^{p}-1}{q-1}=1+q+\dots+q^{p-1}

induced by (
5.0.1
) does not play very well with the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
-action on the source. However, recall from
3.29
that the map
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
is slightly different from the one to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
: Instead of sending
(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)
to the divisor cut out by
1+[q^{\flat}]^{1/p}+\dots+[q^{\flat}]^{(p-1)/p}
in
Y_{A}
, it sends it the divisor cut out by
\log[q^{\flat}]
in
\mathrm{FF}_{A}
! In particular, the composition

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\xrightarrow{\mu}\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}

factors through

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}\;,\hskip 8.5359pt(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)\mapsto\log q

which now interacts a lot better with the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
-action: We have
\log q^{\gamma}=\gamma\log q
and hence the map above intertwines the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
-action on the source with the multiplication action on the target.

Overall, we see that we would be much better off if we could replace
1+q+\dots+q^{p}
by
\log q
. However, note that the latter is not even always contained in
\overline{\mathbb{D}}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{1}
and, in particular, will generally differ from
1+q+\dots+q^{p}
by more than just multiplication by a unit of norm
1
; nevertheless, at least after restricting further to
(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})\subseteq(1,\infty)
, we will be able to arrange that the two differ by a unit. Concluding the discussion, we see that our situation would overall be much improved if we replaced all occurrences of
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
in the definition of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
by occurrences of
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, which is precisely what we will do below.

This yields a slightly different stack
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
which we call the
reduced Nygaardification
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, for which we will construct a map

\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}\coloneqq(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}

which presents the target as a quotient of the source by an explicit group stack, which we will describe below. Also note that the effect of passing from
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is not too grave: Basically, the map
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is a
(0,\infty)
-torsor and thus, in particular, understanding
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is still enough to prove cohomological smoothness of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. Also observe that we really only need to care about
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}
: Indeed, using that

\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}\times[0,1]\;,\hskip 8.5359pt\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p,\infty)}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p,\infty)}{\Delta}\times[0,1]

via
\pi
by
4.9
, understanding
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
over the locus
(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})\subseteq(1,\infty)
suffices as we already have a good understanding of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p,\infty)}{\Delta}
due to
3.28
and
3.23
, respectively.

After this lengthy discussion of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
, let us briefly outline what happens in the rest of the section: One easily sees that the definition of the derived Berkovich space
D^{+}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
in fact descends to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
and, using this, one can define
X^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
for any Gelfand stack
X
. Using the results of the discussion above, we will then be able to control
X^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\rightarrow Y^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
for arbitrary Berkovich smooth maps
X\rightarrow Y
