between derived Berkovich spaces. Indeed, by compatibility of
X\mapsto X^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
with Berkovich étale localisation, which is proved exactly the same as in
4.20
, this formally reduces to understanding
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n})^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
; by commutation of
X\mapsto X^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
with limits, we can furthermore reduce to
n=1
. In this latter case, we are going to construct a map

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\times\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}

which presents the target as the quotient of the source by an explicit group stack over
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
, which we shall describe.

Finally, we specialise to the locus
\{|t|=0\}
, i.e. to the stack
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
. Note that this closed substack of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
is already very interesting (and in fact captures almost all of the additional information that
X^{\mathrm{N}}
holds compared to
X{\Delta}
): Namely, it provides a deformation from
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}_{|t|=0}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=|t|=0}
to
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0,|u|=1}
. To understand this deformation, we shall want slightly finer information compared to what we obtain from the discussion above: Namely, we will establish an explicit presentation of the stack
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
itself in the case where
X
is equipped with a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
. Using this, we will be able to give an explicit description of the category of perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
for such
X
, which will be useful later when we want to describe vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and their cohomology.

5.1.
The Nygaardification of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}

Let us now carry out the strategy to describe
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
more explicitly laid out above. We start by defining
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
as mentioned previously.

Definition 5.1
.

The
reduced Nygaardification
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is the Gelfand stack
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
defined as the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

We first want to clarify the relationship between
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
. For this, we introduce another definition.

Definition 5.2
.

The
extended Nygaardification
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is the Gelfand stack
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}
defined as the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

Recalling that the map
\widetilde{\mu}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
factors through
(\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
, we observe that the stack
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}
is supported over the locus
\{|ut|<1\}
. Moreover, we immediately see that

(5.1.1)

\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|\leq 1,|u|\leq 1}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}\;.

The relation between
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is thus fully explained by the following result:

Lemma 5.3
.

The canonical map
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
induced by
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is a
(0,\infty)
-torsor.

Proof.

We first note that there is a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-})^{\mathrm{dR}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

where
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}
acts diagonally on
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-})^{\mathrm{dR}}
in the top left corner. From this, we conclude that there is a cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-})^{\mathrm{dR}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

from which we in turn deduce a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-})^{\mathrm{dR}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,u)}

Observing that the lower horizontal map is a torsor for
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong(0,\infty)
, where the isomorphism is due to
2.22
, the claim follows.
∎

Note that, just as
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
, the stacks
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}
are equipped with a radius map
\kappa
towards
(0,\infty)
via the respective maps
\pi
towards
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
. We remark that the argument above even shows that
\mathbb{Q}_{p,[r,s]}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}
is a
(0,\infty)
-torsor over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,[r,s]}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
for any
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
. Similarly, the isomorphism (
5.1.1
) is compatible with base change to any
[r,s]\subseteq(0,\infty)
as well.

Moving on, recall that our goal is to obtain an explicit presentation of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
starting from the map

\rho_{q\mathrm{dR}}:\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}

from (
5.0.1
), which we now study in slightly more detail. Let us point out here that the source should really be more accurately denoted by
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{(1,1,1,\dots)\}
, which in turn refers to the colimit

\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{n}\operatornamewithlimits{lim}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}\xleftarrow{q^{p}\mapsfrom q}1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}\xleftarrow{q^{p}\mapsfrom q}\dots\xleftarrow{q^{p}\mapsfrom q}1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}\xleftarrow{q^{p}\mapsfrom q}1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}\xleftarrow{q^{p}\mapsfrom q}1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}\xleftarrow{q^{p}\mapsfrom q}\dots)\;,

where the first occurrence of the pointed open unit disk
1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}
around
1
is in the
n
-th spot.

As we will eventually want to base change the map
\rho_{q\mathrm{dR}}
to
(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})
, our first task is to make the composite

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\xrightarrow{\rho_{q\mathrm{dR}}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\xrightarrow{\kappa}(0,\infty)

more explicit. For this, recall that the radius map on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
is induced by the one on
Y_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, which in turn is obtained by descent from the map
Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
defined by

\kappa^{-1}([r,s])=\{|p|^{s}\leq|[p^{\flat}]|\leq|p|^{r}\}\subseteq Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\;.

Moreover, unwinding definitions, we find that the map

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\xrightarrow{\rho_{q\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta})^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong Y_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}

identifies with the composition

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\cong Y_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow Y_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\;,

where the first isomorphism is given by
q^{1/p^{n}}\mapsto[\varepsilon]^{1/p^{n}}
for
\varepsilon=(1,\zeta_{p},\zeta_{p^{2}},\dots)
, as usual, see
[
1
, Lem. 6.2.1.(2)]
. In other words, we have to understand
|[p^{\flat}]|
on
Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
in terms of the
p
-power roots of
[\varepsilon]
.

Lemma 5.4
.

For any continuous seminorm on
Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
, we have

|[p^{\flat}]|=\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{n}|[\varepsilon]^{1/p^{n}}-1|^{p^{n-1}(p-1)}\;.

Proof.

First note that
(\varepsilon-1)^{p-1}
and
(p^{\flat})^{p}
differ by a unit in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}}
. Indeed, the latter is a valuation ring whose valuation is given by
v(x)\coloneqq v(x^{\sharp})
and normalising the valuation on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
such that
v(p)=1
yields

v(\varepsilon-1)=v\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{n}(\zeta_{p^{n}}-1)^{p^{n}}\right)=\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{n}p^{n}v(\zeta_{p^{n}}-1)=\frac{p}{p-1}

while
v(p^{\flat})=1
, which proves the claim. Thus, also
[p^{\flat}]^{p}
and
[\varepsilon-1]^{p-1}
differ by a unit in
A_{\mathrm{inf}}
and hence they have the same norm on
Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
.

It remains to show that

|[\varepsilon-1]|=\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{n}|[\varepsilon]^{1/p^{n}}-1|^{p^{n}}\;.

However, this actually holds as an equality in
A_{\mathrm{inf}}
even before taking norms: Indeed, for each
n\geq 0
, the element
[\varepsilon]^{1/p^{n}}-1\in A_{\mathrm{inf}}=W(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}})
lifts
(\varepsilon-1)^{1/p^{n}}=\varepsilon^{1/p^{n}}-1\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\flat}}
and this implies the claim.
∎

Normalising the norm such that
|p|=1/p
, the previous lemma shows that the preimage of
(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})\subseteq(0,\infty)
in
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}
under the radius map is described by

p^{-p^{3/2}}<\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{n}|q^{1/p^{n}}-1|^{p^{n-1}(p-1)}<p^{-p^{1/2}}\;.

Noting that
|(1+t)^{p}-1|=|t|^{p}
holds whenever
p^{-1/p}<|t|<1
, we see that the above is equivalent to requiring

p^{-1/(p^{1/2}(p-1))}<|q^{1/p^{3}}-1|<p^{-1/(p^{3/2}(p-1))}\;.

Corollary 5.5
.

The base change of
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}
to
(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})\subseteq(0,\infty)
sits in a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{T}}(p^{-1/(p^{1/2}(p-1))},p^{-1/(p^{3/2}(p-1))})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}\;,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{4}}

where
\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{T}}(a,b)
denotes the open torus of inner radius
a
and outer radius
b
for any
a<b
.

Our next task is to understand the composition

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\xrightarrow{\rho_{q\mathrm{dR}}}\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\mu}}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}\;.

For this, note that the first map factors through the projection onto the analytic de Rham stack of the source since
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta}\cong(\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta})^{\mathrm{dR}}
by
3.23
and observe that there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\mathrlap{\cong(\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta})^{\mathrm{dR}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)\,\Big/\,\varphi{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FF}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FF}_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\mathrlap{=\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}\cong(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

in which the top composition identifies with
\rho_{q\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
while the bottom composition sends
(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)
to the degree
1
Cartier divisor in
\mathrm{FF}_{\overline{A}}
cut out by
\log[q^{\flat}]
. Further noting that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathllap{\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta}\cong\,}(\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FF}_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{\mathrm{dR}}}

commutes, where the lower horizontal map is defined by pulling back a degree
1
Cartier divisor in
\mathrm{FF}_{\overline{A}}
along
\iota:\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow\mathrm{FF}_{\overline{A}}
for any totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
, we conclude:

Lemma 5.6
.

The composition

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\xrightarrow{\rho_{q\mathrm{dR}}}\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\mu}}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}

factors through the map

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}\;,\hskip 8.5359pt(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)\mapsto\log q\;.

Using this result, we can already establish the following presentation of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
:

Proposition 5.7
.

There is an isomorphism

(5.1.2)

\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\cong(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}})\times_{(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\;\Bigg/\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\;,

where the right-hand side is defined as follows:

(i)

The maps
(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}
and
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}
defining the pullback are given by multiplication and
\log\circ\operatorname{pr}_{1}
, respectively.

(ii)

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
acts on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
by multiplication and on
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
via the formula

\gamma.(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)=(q^{\gamma},q^{\gamma/p},q^{\gamma/p^{2}},\dots)\;.

(iii)

\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}
by division and on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
by multiplication.

Proof.

We first explain how the right-hand side of (
5.1.2
) fits into the pullback square defining
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
. For the map towards
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta}
, note that the second projection “in the numerator” and the map

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}

induce a map from the right-hand side of (
5.1.2
) to

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta}\;,

where the isomorphism follows from
3.23
and
3.30
. Similarly, the map

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}

and the projection onto
(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}
“in the numerator” induce a map from (
5.1.2
) to
(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
. For the map to
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, recall that there is a canonical map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}
by
2.33
, whose restriction to
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
identifies with the inclusion map
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\subseteq\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Therefore, we obtain a map

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}

and this together with the projection onto
\mathbb{A}^{1}
“in the numerator” induces the map from the right-hand side of (
5.1.2
) to
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

We note that the maps from the right-hand side of (
5.1.2
) to
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta}
are indeed compatible upon mapping to
(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
: This follows from
5.6
together with the fact that the map

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\mu}}(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}

is induced by the map
(q,q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots)\mapsto\log q
“in the numerator” and the canonical map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}
“in the denominator”. Overall, we thus obtain a map

(5.1.3)

(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}})\times_{(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\;\Bigg/\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\;.

To check that (
5.1.3
) is an isomorphism, we may pull back along the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
-torsor

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta}\;.

Then the source of (
5.1.3
) becomes

(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times_{(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}

while target sits inside a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\,\dots)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\log\circ\operatorname{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

Now we are done upon noting that the map
\log\circ\operatorname{pr}_{1}
factors through the canonical map
(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
and that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

is cartesian.
∎

Finally, our goal is to make the presentation of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
from
5.7
even more explicit by eliminating the de Rham stacks “in the numerator”. For this, consider the group stack
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m})
, where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
by division, and observe that, after base change to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, it receives the following two maps from
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
: The first one is given by

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m})\;,\hskip 8.5359pts\mapsto(1,u(1-s^{-1}),s)

while the second one is given by postcomposing the canonical map
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la},\times}
with the inclusion of the first factor. We let
\mathcal{G}
denote the coequaliser of these two maps in the category of group stacks over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Moreover, we define the Gelfand stack
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
by the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\log\circ\operatorname{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{mult}}

Now our main result is the following:

Theorem 5.8
.

There is an isomorphism

\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\cong\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}\,/\,\mathcal{G}\;,

where the action of
\mathcal{G}
on
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
is given by the formula

(\gamma,w,s).(t,u,(q^{1/p^{m}})_{m})\coloneqq\left(\gamma st,\frac{u}{s}+w,\left(q^{\gamma/p^{m}}\exp\left(\frac{\gamma wst}{p^{m}}\right)\right)_{m}\right)

for
\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}},w\in\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
and
s\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
and where
t
and
u
denote the coordinates on
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}
and
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}
, respectively.

Proof.

Note that the exponential makes sense due to
\gamma wst/p^{m}\in\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
. We first check that the action is well-defined, i.e. that the actions of the two copies of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
which sit inside
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m})
via the maps described above agree. For this, we calculate

(1,u(1-s^{-1}),s).(t,u,(q^{1/p^{m}})_{m})=\left(st,u,\left(q^{1/p^{m}}\exp\left(\frac{u(s-1)t}{p^{m}}\right)\right)_{m}\right)=(st,u,(q^{s/p^{m}})_{m})\;,

where the last equality is due to
ut=\log q
on
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\mathrm{N}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
. However, the right-hand side is the image of
(t,u,(q^{1/p^{m}})_{m})
under the action of
(s,0,1)
and hence the two actions agree.

Now observe that there is a canonical map
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}\,/\,\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
using the presentation of the target from
5.7
and checking whether it is an isomorphism may be done after pullback along

(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}})\times_{(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\;.

Then
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}\,/\,\mathcal{G}
becomes
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}\,/\,\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
, where the action of
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
is given by

w.(t,u,(q^{1/p^{m}})_{m})=\left(t,u+w,\left(q^{1/p^{m}}\exp\left(\frac{wt}{p^{m}}\right)\right)_{m}\right)\;.

However, now we are done due to

\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}\,/\,\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\cong(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}})\times_{(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathrm{dR}}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\;.

Indeed, to see this last isomorphism, observe that, given an
A
-point
(t,u,(q^{1/p^{m}})_{m})
of the target for some nilperfectoid
A
, once we lift
u
from
\overline{A}
to
A
, the condition
ut=\log q
in
A
specifies a unique lift of
q
from
\overline{A}
to
A
and then the fact that the map
q\mapsto q^{p}
on
1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}
is
\dagger
-formally étale also yields unique lifts of the elements
q^{1/p},q^{1/p^{2}},\dots
. As the ambiguity of lifting
u
from
\overline{A}
to
A
is precisely killed by modding out the
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-action, this finishes the proof.
∎

5.2.
Nygaardifications of smooth maps between derived Berkovich spaces

We now move on to studying
X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
for any Berkovich smooth map
X\rightarrow Y
of derived Berkovich spaces. For this, we first observe that
X^{\mathrm{N}}
descends along
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
for any Gelfand stack
X
. Indeed, note that, given an
A
-point of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
for
A
totally disconnected nilperfectoid, the definition of the Gelfand stack
D^{+}
from (
4.1.3
) only makes use of the data of the
A
-point of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
obtained from the given
A
-point of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
. Thus, we can define
D^{+}
by the diagram (
4.1.3
) for any
A
-point of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
with
A
totally disconnected nilperfectoid and then define, as before:

Definition 5.9
.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, we define its
reduced Nygaardification
as the Gelfand stack
X^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
obtained by sheafifying the assignment

X^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}(\GSpec A\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}})\coloneqq\{\text{maps}\,D^{+}\rightarrow X\}

for any totally disconnected nilperfectoid
A
.

By definition, we have
X^{\mathrm{N}}\cong X^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
for any Gelfand stack
X
and hence studying the map
X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
for
X\rightarrow Y
a Berkovich smooth map of derived Berkovich spaces reduces to studying the map
X^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\rightarrow Y^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
. Moreover, we point out that
4.20
also holds for
X^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
in place of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
without any changes to the proof. By definition of Berkovich smooth maps, this further reduces us to understanding
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n})^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
and, by compatibility of the functor
X\mapsto X^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
with limits, we can even reduce to
n=1
.

In this case, we are going to describe
\mathbb{G}_{m,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
as an explicit quotient using the coordinates on the cover

(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}=\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}

from
5.8
. Recalling that we denote the coordinates on
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}
and
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}
by
t
and
u
, respectively, we can form the pushout
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
in the category of abelian group stacks over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
, where the map
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
is given by multiplication by
u
.

Theorem 5.10
.

Over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
, there is an isomorphism

(5.2.1)

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}

of normed multiplicative group stacks, where the action defining the right-hand side is given by the formula

(\theta,v).x^{1/p^{m}}\coloneqq q^{\theta/p^{m}}\exp\left(\frac{tv}{p^{m}}\right)x^{1/p^{m}}\;,

where
(q^{1/p^{m}})_{m}
are the coordinates on
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})
.

Proof.

Note that the exponential makes sense due to
tv/p^{m}\in\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
. We first check that the action is well-defined, i.e. that the actions of the two copies of
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
sitting inside
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
via postcomposing the canonical map
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}
with the inclusion of the first factor and via postcomposing the multiplication-by-
u
-map on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
with the inclusion of the second factor, respectively, agree. For this, we calculate

(0,u\theta).x^{1/p^{m}}=\exp\left(\frac{tu\theta}{p^{m}}\right)x^{1/p^{m}}=q^{\theta/p^{m}}x^{1/p^{m}}

for
\theta\in\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
, where the last equality is due to
tu=\log q
on
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
. As the right-hand side is also the image of
x^{1/p^{m}}
under the action of
(\theta,0)
, the two actions indeed agree and the formula from the theorem statement defines an action of the pushout
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
.

To move on, note that
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
sits inside a pullback square

(5.2.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\Cone}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\Cone})^{\mathrm{dR}}}

as in
4.7
, where we note that the cone stack descends along
\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
and we use this descended version in the diagram above as well as the rest of the proof. We explain how the right-hand side of (
5.2.1
) fits into the same pullback diagram over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
. For this, first note that
\mathbb{G}_{m}{\Delta}\cong(\mathbb{G}_{m}{\Delta})^{\mathrm{dR}}
as we are working over
(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})
, see
3.23
, and hence
3.30
yields the presentation

(5.2.3)

\mathbb{G}_{m}{\Delta}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}\,/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}

over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
. Now note that the right-hand side of (
5.2.1
) maps to the right-hand side of (
5.2.3
) via the projection onto the de Rham stack “in the numerator” and the map

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}

“in the denominator”.

To obtain the map to
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\Cone}
, we recall from
5.6
that the fact that we are working over
(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})
is equivalent to the inequality

p^{-1/(p^{1/2}(p-1))}<|q^{1/p^{3}}-1|<p^{-1/(p^{3/2}(p-1))}\;,

from which we in particular deduce that
|q-1|<p^{-1}
. As the map
\log:1+\overline{\mathbb{D}}(p^{-1})\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}(p^{-1})
is an isomorphism with inverse given by the exponential, we obtain
q=\exp(tu)
from the requirement
tu=\log q
on
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
. Thus, we can rewrite the action of
(\theta,v)\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
on
x
as

(\theta,v).x=\exp(t(u\theta+v))x\;,

which makes sense due to
tu\theta\in\overline{\mathbb{D}}(p^{-1})
, as we just established. Expanding the exponential on the right-hand side into its power series, we see that
(\theta,v).x=x+t(\,\dots)
, where the term in brackets is an explicit power series in
t,u,\theta,v
and
x
. As the generalised Cartier divisor defining the cone stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is given by multiplication by
t
, this shows that the coordinate
x
on
\mathbb{G}_{m}
defines a map from the right-hand side of (
5.2.1
) to
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\Cone}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
.

As the map

\mathbb{G}_{m}{\Delta}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}\,/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}\rightarrow(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\Cone})^{\mathrm{dR}}

over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is induced by the coordinate on
\mathbb{G}_{m}
as well, the maps from the right-hand side of (
5.2.1
) to
\mathbb{G}_{m}{\Delta}
and
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\Cone}
we have constructed are compatible upon mapping to
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\Cone})^{\mathrm{dR}}
and hence induce a map

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}

over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
.

To see that this is an isomorphism, first note that, since
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is
\dagger
-formally smooth, there is a short exact sequence of group stacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\Cone}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\Cone})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

where
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
acts on
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
via
v.x\coloneqq\exp(tv)x
, and hence (
5.2.2
) shows that
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-torsor over
\mathbb{G}_{m}{\Delta}
. However, note that also the map

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}\,/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}

is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-torsor: Indeed, we have

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}

as the map
x\mapsto x^{p}
on
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is
\dagger
-formally smooth. Thus, we are done upon noting that the map

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}

we have constructed is a map of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-torsors over
\mathbb{G}_{m}{\Delta}
via the isomorphism (
5.2.3
).
∎

Corollary 5.11
.

Let
X
be a derived Berkovich space admitting a map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
which is the composition of a finite étale map
X\rightarrow Z
and a rational localisation
Z\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
. Then the Gelfand stack
X_{[r,s]}^{\mathrm{N}}
is nicely coverable for any
[r,s]\subseteq(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})
.

Proof.

By compatibility of
X\mapsto X^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
with finite étale maps and rational localisations, which one proves as in
4.20
, we may reduce to the case
X=\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
using
2.16
. By commutation with limits, we then conclude from
5.10
that there is an isomorphism

(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n})^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x_{i}\mapsto x_{i}^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}\,\Big/\,(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}

over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
. Now note that the right-hand side has a Čech cover given by

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x_{i}\mapsto x_{i}^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}\,\times\,\left((\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}\right)^{\bullet}

and that the stack
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x_{i}\mapsto x_{i}^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
is affine, static and flat over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Thus, we will be able to conclude that
(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n})^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}_{[r,s]}
is nicely coverable “relative to
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
”, i.e. that every base change to an affine truncated Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is nicely coverable, once we know that
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}
has a
!
-hypercover by Gelfand stacks which are affine, static and flat over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

However, this simply follows from the fact that

(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}\cong(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{la}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}\,\Big/\,(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}

as the right-hand side has a
!
-cover by
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{la}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}
whose Čech nerve is given by

(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{la}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}\times((\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n})^{\bullet}\;,

which is a nice hypercover as
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}})=C^{\mathrm{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})=\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{v\}^{\dagger}
are both flat over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by
2.15
.

Finally, as
(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n})^{\mathrm{N}}\cong(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n})^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
, the claim follows from
2.13
by base changing the nice hypercover of
(\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n})^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
“relative to
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
” from above to the nice hypercover of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,[r,s]}^{\mathrm{N}}
from the proof of
4.23
, where we recall that all constituents of this latter hypercover are static, which yields the uniformity required in
2.13
.
∎

5.3.
The Hodge–Tate to de Rham degeneration

We now turn our attention to the stack
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}=X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0}
, which we want to describe completely explicitly in the case where
X
is equipped with a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
. To set the stage, we begin with the case
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, which is already interesting. For this, consider the group stack
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}})
, where
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
acts on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
by division. Similar to what happened above, this group stack receives two maps from
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
after base change to
\overline{\mathbb{D}}=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle u\rangle_{\leq 1}
: The first one is given by

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}})\;,\hskip 8.5359pts\mapsto(1,u(1-s^{-1}),s)

while the second one is given by postcomposing the canonical map
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la},\times}
with the inclusion of the first factor. We let
\mathcal{G}_{0}
denote the coequaliser of these two maps in the category of group stacks over
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
. Using this, we can describe
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
explicitly as follows:

Proposition 5.12
.

There is an isomorphism

(5.3.1)

\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,\Big/\,\mathcal{G}_{0}\;,

where the action of
\mathcal{G}_{0}
on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
is given by the formula

(\gamma,w,s).(t,u,(\zeta_{p^{m}})_{m})\coloneqq\left(\gamma st,\frac{u}{s}+w,(\zeta_{p^{m}}^{\gamma})_{m}\right)

for
\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}},w\in\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
and
s\in\overline{\mathbb{T}}
and where
t
and
u
denote the coordinates on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
and
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
, respectively. In particular, restricting to
|u|=1
, we have

\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\;.

Proof.

From
5.8
, we already know that

\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\cong(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\;\Big/\;\mathcal{G}

and, in particular,
\log q=tu
is
\dagger
-nilpotent meaning that
q\in(\mu_{p^{\infty}}\subseteq 1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\dagger}
, where
\mu_{p^{\infty}}
is the group stack of
p
-power roots of unity and
(\mu_{p^{\infty}}\subseteq 1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})^{\dagger}
denotes its overconvergent neighbourhood inside
1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}
. As the fact that we are over
(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})\subseteq(0,\infty)
corresponds to the inequality

p^{-1/(p^{1/2}(p-1))}<|q^{1/p^{3}}-1|<p^{-1/(p^{3/2}(p-1))}

by
5.6
, we conclude that
q^{1/p^{3}}
lies in the overconvergent neighbourhood of a primitive
p^{2}
-th root of unity and, consequently,
q^{1/p^{m}}
lies in the overconvergent neighbourhood of a primitive
p^{m-1}
-th root of unity for all
m\geq 1
while
q\in\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
. As the map
q\mapsto q^{p}
is
\dagger
-formally étale on
1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}
, we conclude that
(q^{1/p^{m}})_{m}
is uniquely determined by
q=\exp(tu)
and hence

(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\;.

Next, note that the map

(5.3.2)

\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\rightarrow(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-})^{\mathrm{dR}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}

induced by the canonical maps
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-})^{\mathrm{dR}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}
, see
2.33
, and
\mathcal{G}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}
agrees with the restriction of the map

\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\rightarrow(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-})^{\mathrm{dR}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}

from the proof of
5.3
. Indeed, by loc. cit., this comes down to checking that the postcomposition of the map (
5.3.2
) with
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-})^{\mathrm{dR}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
classifies the Tate twist under the equivalence from
4.10
, but this is clear as the map
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
classifying the Tate twist factors as

\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow*/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\;,

where the first map classifies the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
-torsor
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Now recall that the proof of
5.3
yields a cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-})^{\mathrm{dR}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}}}

and in particular shows that
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is a torsor for
\mathbb{G}_{m}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\cong(0,\infty)
, where the isomorphism is due to
2.22
. However, evidently, by the presentation of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
from (
5.3.2
), the stack

\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,\Big/\,\mathcal{G}_{0}

fits into the same cartesian diagram and is a
(0,\infty)
-torsor over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
as well due to
\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}_{0}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\cong(0,\infty)
and hence we conclude that the induced map

\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,\Big/\,\mathcal{G}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}

is an isomorphism upon noting that it is a map of
(0,\infty)
-torsors. Now the claimed isomorphism (
5.3.1
) follows from
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|\leq 1}^{\mathrm{N}}
, see (
5.1.1
).

Finally, let us comment on how to obtain the claimed presentation of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
. For this, observe that, if
|u|=1
, we have
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}\cong\mathcal{G}_{0}
via the natural map from left to right and hence

\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;.

Twisting the “numerator” of the right-hand side by the automorphism of
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}
given by
(t,u)\mapsto(tu,u)
, the action of
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
becomes trivial and the claim follows.
∎

Corollary 5.13
.

A perfect complex on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
amounts to the following data:

(i)

A diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{i-1}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{i}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{i+1}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

of perfect complexes on
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{r\}^{\dagger}
, where
r=ut
, with the property that
t:\Fil_{\bullet}V\rightarrow\Fil_{\bullet-1}V
becomes an isomorphism for
\bullet\ll 0
and
u:\Fil_{\bullet}V\rightarrow\Fil_{\bullet+1}V
becomes an isomorphism for
\bullet\gg 0
;

(ii)

\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
-linear maps
D:\Fil_{i}V\rightarrow\Fil_{i-1}V
for each
i
satisfying
Du=uD+1
and commuting with
t
;

(iii)

a semilinear locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action on
\Fil_{\bullet}V
commuting with
u
and
D
, where the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
is the usual Galois action and the one on
t
is given by multiplication;

(iv)

for each
i
, an identification between the “Sen operator”
\Theta:\Fil_{i}V\rightarrow\Fil_{i}V
coming from the Lie algebra action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and the operator
uD-i
.

Under this identification, restriction to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
corresponds to forgetting the
u
-filtration and the
D
-maps while restriction to
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|=|t|=0}^{\mathrm{N}}
corresponds to passing to the associated graded of the
u
-filtration and forgetting the
D
-maps. Finally, the isomorphism

\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|=|t|=0}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}

from
4.11
Tate twists the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action on the
i
-th associated graded piece of the
u
-filtration by
i
, which trivialises the Lie algebra action and hence after descent along
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
via the now smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action yields a filtered perfect complex of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector spaces via the
t
-maps.

Proof.

Item (i) is a version of the Rees equivalence in the form from
2.25
while the operator
D
from (ii) comes from the
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-factor in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}})
via Cartier duality, see
2.26
; moreover, the relation
Du=uD+1
comes from the fact that the
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-action on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
corresponds to the endomorphism
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle u\rangle_{\leq 1}
via Cartier duality and the fact that
D
lowers the filtration degree by
1
is due to
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
acting on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
by division. Furthermore, (iii) is clear and the Tate twist occurring in translating between perfect complexes on
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|=|t|=0}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is due to the fact that the isomorphism
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|=|t|=0}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=0}^{\mathrm{dR},+}
itself involves a Tate twist.

The only part that requires a little more explanation is (iv). Here, we note that the Sen operator
\Theta
is obtained by restricting the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
along the canonical map
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
and using the isomorphism
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
via the logarithm together with Cartier duality for
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
in the form from
2.26
. Thus, we have to check that the endomorphism
\Theta
obtained by restricting the action of
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}}
along

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;,\hskip 8.5359pts\mapsto(u(1-s^{-1}),s)\;,

using
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
via the logarithm and Cartier duality for
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
identifies with
uD-i
on
\Fil_{i}V
.

To see this, first note that pulling back the action of
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
along
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}
and using
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
via the logarithm together with Cartier duality for
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
yields an operator
\Psi
which is given by multiplication by
-i
on
\Fil_{i}V\cdot u^{i}
as
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
acts on
u
by division. Thus, we can reformulate our goal as showing that
\Theta=uD+\Psi
. To check this, observe that the
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})
-comodule structure corresponding to a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-representation, where
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
acts on itself via
\ell.w\coloneqq w/\exp\ell
, is given by

\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\frac{\ell^{i}}{i!}\cdot\frac{w^{j}}{j!}\cdot\Psi^{i}D^{j}

in terms of the operators
D
and
\Psi
. Restricting this along
\ell\mapsto(u(1-\exp(-\ell)),\ell)
yields the
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})
-comodule structure given by

\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\frac{\ell^{i}}{i!}\cdot\frac{u^{j}(1-\exp(-\ell))^{j}}{j!}\cdot\Psi^{i}D^{j}

and comparing this with the formula
\sum_{k\geq 0}\tfrac{\ell^{k}}{k!}\cdot\Theta^{k}
, which gives the
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})
-comodule structure corresponding to the operator
\Theta
under Cartier duality, we see that
\Theta=uD+\Psi
by comparing the terms which are linear in
\ell
, as desired.
∎

Let us now move on to the higher-dimensional case and consider a derived Berkovich space
X
equipped with a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
. To explicitly describe
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
in this case, let us again start by introducing some notation: Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
denote the group stack whose underlying Gelfand stack is the product
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}})^{n}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}
with the group operation given by the formula

(\underline{\theta}_{1},\gamma_{1},\underline{v}_{1},w_{1},s_{1})\cdot(\underline{\theta}_{2},\gamma_{2},\underline{v}_{2},w_{2},s_{2})=(\underline{\theta}_{1}+\gamma_{1}\underline{\theta}_{2},\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2},\underline{v}_{1}+\gamma_{1}s_{1}(\underline{v}_{2}+w_{1}\underline{\theta}_{2}),w_{1}+w_{2}/s_{1},s_{1}s_{2})\;,

where
\underline{\theta}_{i}=(\theta_{i}^{(1)},\dots,\theta_{i}^{(n)})
and similarly for
\underline{v}_{i}
and we write

\gamma_{2}\underline{\theta}_{1}\coloneqq(\gamma_{2}\theta_{1}^{(1)},\dots,\gamma_{2}\theta_{1}^{(n)})

and similarly in the other cases. After base change to
\overline{\mathbb{D}}=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle u\rangle_{\leq 1}
, the group stack
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
admits two maps from
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
, where
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
acts on
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}
by multiplication: The first one is given by

(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\mapsto\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\;,\hskip 8.5359pt(\underline{\theta},s)\mapsto(0,1,u\underline{\theta},u(1-s^{-1}),s)

and the second one is given by
(\underline{\theta},s)\mapsto(\underline{\theta},s,0,0,1)
. We let
\mathcal{G}_{n}
be the coequaliser of these two maps in the category of group stacks over
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
. Furthermore, for a derived Berkovich space
X
equipped with a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
, we write

X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\coloneqq X\times_{\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}}\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x_{i}\mapsto x_{i}^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}\;,

where the map
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x_{i}\mapsto x_{i}^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
is the first projection.

Theorem 5.14
.

Let
X
be a derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
equipped with a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
for some
n
. Then there is an isomorphism

(5.3.3)

X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}\cong X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,\Big/\,\mathcal{G}_{n}\;,

where the action of
\mathcal{G}_{n}
on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
is pulled back from the action of
\mathcal{G}_{n}
on
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n,\mathrm{la}}_{\infty}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
given by the formula

(\underline{\theta},\gamma,\underline{v},w,s).((\underline{x}^{1/p^{m}})_{m},t,u,(\zeta_{p^{m}})_{m})\coloneqq\left(\left(\zeta_{p^{m-1}}^{\underline{\theta}}\exp\left(\frac{t(u\underline{\theta}+\underline{v})}{p^{m}}\right)\underline{x}^{1/p^{m}}\right)_{m},\gamma st,\frac{u}{s}+w,(\zeta_{p^{m}}^{\gamma})_{m}\right)\;,

where
(\underline{x}^{1/p^{m}})_{m},t
and
u
denote the coordinates on
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n,\mathrm{la}}_{\infty},\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
and
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
, respectively, and we set
\zeta_{p^{-1}}\coloneqq 1
for notational convenience. In particular, we have

X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\cong X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,\Big/\,(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}})^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\;,

where
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
acts on
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}})^{n}
by multiplication.

Proof.

By compatibility of the functor
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
with Berkovich étale maps, which one deduces from the case
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
treated in
4.20
by base change, we may reduce to the case
X=\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
, from where we may further reduce to
n=1
by compatibility of
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
with limits using that we have already worked out the case
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in
5.12
.

In the case
X=\overline{\mathbb{T}}
, recall that
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
. Thus, putting together
5.12
and
5.10
at once implies the claim as soon as we check that the action

(\theta,v).x^{1/p^{m}}=q^{\theta/p^{m}}\exp\left(\frac{tv}{p^{m}}\right)x^{1/p^{m}}

from loc. cit. transforms into the action described above after base changing along the map
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}
. However, this simply follows by recalling that the proof of
5.12
shows that
q^{1/p^{m}}=\zeta_{p^{m-1}}\exp(tu/p^{m})
for all
m\geq 0
on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
.

Finally, one obtains the claimed formula for
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
in the same way as in the proof of
5.12
: If
|u|=1
, we have
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}})\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}\cong\mathcal{G}_{1}
via the natural map from left to right, where
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
acts on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}
by multiplication, and hence

\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\cong\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\infty}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,\Big/\,(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}})\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;.

Twisting the “numerator” of the right-hand side by the automorphism of
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}
given by
(t,u)\mapsto(tu,u)
, the action of
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
becomes trivial and the claim follows.
∎

Corollary 5.15
.

Let
X
be a derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
equipped with a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
. Then a perfect complex on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
amounts to the following data:

(i)

A diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{i-1}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{i}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{i+1}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

of perfect complexes on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{r\}^{\dagger}
, where
r=ut
, with the property that
t:\Fil_{\bullet}V\rightarrow\Fil_{\bullet-1}V
becomes an isomorphism for
\bullet\ll 0
and
u:\Fil_{\bullet}V\rightarrow\Fil_{\bullet+1}V
becomes an isomorphism for
\bullet\gg 0
;

(ii)

X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
-linear maps
D:\Fil_{i}V\rightarrow\Fil_{i-1}V
for each
i
satisfying
Du=uD+1
and commuting with
t
;

(iii)

a “
t
-connection”
\nabla:V\rightarrow V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}^{1}(-1)
on
V
, i.e. a map satisfying the
t
-deformed Leibniz rule
\nabla(fm)=f\nabla(m)+t\cdot\mathrm{d}f
, which commutes with
D
and whose restriction to
\Fil_{i}V
is equipped with a factorisation through
\Fil_{i-1}V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}^{1}(-1)
; here, the twist by
-1
indicates that
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
acts on
\Omega_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}^{1}
by division, i.e.
\gamma.\omega=\gamma^{-1}\omega
;

(iv)

a semilinear locally analytic
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-action on
\Fil_{\bullet}V
commuting with all the previous data; here,
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
acts on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
by multiplication and the semilinearity is asked with respect to the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
on
X^{\mathrm{la}}_{\infty}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{r\}^{\dagger}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
pulled back from the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
on
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n,\mathrm{la}}_{\infty}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{r\}^{\dagger}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
given by

(\underline{\theta},\gamma).((\underline{x}^{1/p^{m}})_{m},r,(\zeta_{p^{m}})_{m})\coloneqq\left(\left(\zeta_{p^{m-1}}^{\underline{\theta}}\exp\left(\frac{r\underline{\theta}}{p^{m}}\right)\underline{x}^{1/p^{m}}\right)_{m},\gamma r,(\zeta_{p^{m}}^{\gamma})_{m}\right)\;.

(v)

an identification between the “arithmetic Sen operator”
\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}:\Fil_{i}V\rightarrow\Fil_{i}V
coming from the Lie algebra action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and the operator
uD-i
for each
i
;

(vi)

an identification between the “geometric Sen operator”
\Theta^{\mathrm{geom}}:\Fil_{i}V\rightarrow\Fil_{i}V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}^{1}(-1)
coming from the Lie algebra action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
and the
t
-connection
u\nabla
for each
i
.

Under this identification, restriction to
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
corresponds to forgetting the
u
-filtration and the
D
-maps while restriction to
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=|t|=0}
corresponds to passing to the associated graded of the
u
-filtration and forgetting the
D
-maps. Finally, the isomorphism

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=|t|=0}\cong X^{\mathrm{dR},+}_{|t|=0}

from
4.11
Tate twists the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action on the
i
-th associated graded piece of the
u
-filtration by
i
, which trivialises the action of the Lie algebra of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and hence after descent along the map

X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\rightarrow X

via the now smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action yields a filtered perfect complex
\Fil^{\bullet}W
on
X
via the
t
-maps together with a flat connection
\nabla:W\rightarrow W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega^{1}_{X}
satisfying Griffiths transversality, i.e. the restriction of
\nabla
to
\Fil^{i}W
is equipped with a factorisation through
\Fil^{i-1}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega^{1}_{X}
.

Proof.

This follows from
5.14
using the same kind of unraveling as in the proof of
5.13
. Indeed, item (i) is again a version of the Rees equivalence in the form from
2.25
while the operators
D
and
\nabla
in (ii) and (iii) come from the
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
- and
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}
-factors in
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
, respectively, via Cartier duality as in
2.26
. The locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action in (iv) is due to the factor
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{la}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
and the compatibilities in (v) and (vi) follow from the definition of
\mathcal{G}_{n}
as a coequaliser by similar computations as in the proof of
5.13
. Finally, the Tate twist occurring in translating between perfect complexes on
X_{|u|=|t|=0}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
X_{|t|=0}^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is again due to the fact that the isomorphism
X_{|u|=|t|=0}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong X_{|t|=0}^{\mathrm{dR},+}
itself involves a Tate twist.
∎

Remark 5.16
.

In principle, one could compress the data contained in a perfect complex on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
even further: Namely, we could replace (ii) and (v) by

(ii’)

a factorisation of
\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}+i:\Fil_{i}V\rightarrow\Fil_{i}V
through
\Fil_{i-1}V
, where
\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}
is the “arithmetic Sen operator” coming from the Lie algebra action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}

and similarly replace (iii) and (vi) by

(iii’)

a factorisation of the “geometric Sen operator”
\Theta^{\mathrm{geom}}:\Fil_{i}V\rightarrow\Fil_{i}V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}^{1}(-1)
coming from the Lie algebra action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
through
\Fil_{i-1}V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}^{1}(-1)
.

Indeed, by (v) and (vi), these factorisations will precisely be given by
D
and
\nabla
, respectively. However, we have chosen not to do this because we want to emphasise the crucial role that
D
and
\nabla
play, e.g. in describing the restriction functors to
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
and
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=|t|=0}
or in describing cohomology on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
.
∎

Corollary 5.17
.

Let
X
be a derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
equipped with a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
. Under the identifications from
5.15
, the cohomology of a perfect complex on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
is computed by the (underived)
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-invariants of the cohomology on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
of the total complex of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{0}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{-1}V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{-2}V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{2}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-2)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{-1}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{-2}V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{-3}V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{2}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-2)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}

Similarly, the cohomology of the pullback of
E
to
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
is computed by the (underived)
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-invariants of the cohomology on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
of the total complex of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{2}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-2)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{2}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-2)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}

Proof.

Using the presentation of
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
from
5.14
, the canonical projection map
\mathcal{G}_{n}\rightarrow(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{sm}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
, where
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
acts by multiplication on
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{sm}}
, induces a map

(5.3.4)

X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}\rightarrow X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\,\big/\,((\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{sm}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}})\;.

From the description of the category of perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
from
5.15
, it is clear that the total complex of the first double complex above equipped with its induced
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{sm}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
-action computes the pushforward along the map (
5.3.4
). Now note that profinite groups like
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
have no higher smooth cohomology in characteristic
0
and hence computing cohomology on the target of (
5.3.4
) amounts to taking underived
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-invariants of the cohomology on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
, as claimed.

Similarly, for the second assertion, observe that, by the presentation of
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
from
5.14
, the pushforward along

X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\rightarrow X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\,\big/\,((\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{sm}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}})

is computed by taking Lie algebra cohomology for the locally analytic action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
on
V
. Recalling that the arithmetic Sen operator
\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}
is given by
u\nabla
on all
\Fil_{i}V
while the geometric Sen operator
\Theta^{\mathrm{geom}}
is given by
uD
on
\Fil_{i}V(i)
, we see that this Lie algebra cohomology is exactly computed by the total complex of the second double complex above. One concludes as in the previous paragraph.
∎

Having made the data of a perfect complex
E
on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
for a Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich space
X
completely explicit in terms of linear algebra, at least in the case where
X
is equipped with a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
, we want to end with the following somewhat surprising result, which says that all this data can in fact be recovered from the restriction of
E
to
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}_{|u|=0}\cong X^{\mathrm{dR},+}_{|t|=0}
. In §
9.2
, we will come back to this result and make it explicit in terms of the linear algebra involved.

Proposition 5.18
.

Let
X
be a Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then the pullbacks along the maps
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=0}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|ut|=0}
and
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}=X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|ut|=0}
induce equivalences of categories

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|ut|=0})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\;.

Proof.

We only show the first equivalence, the second one is analogous. Moreover, note that the canonical maps induce an isomorphism

(5.3.5)

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=|t|=0}}X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=0}\xrightarrow{\cong}X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|ut|=0}

by a variant of
2.8
and thus it suffices to show that
\Perf(X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=|t|=0})
via pullback along the canonical map
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=|t|=0}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0}
.

To this end, by compatibility of
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
with rational localisations, see
4.22
, we may assume that
X
admits a finite étale map
X\rightarrow Z
to some rational subspace
Z\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
. Then
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[r,s]}
is nicely coverable for any
[r,s]\subseteq(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})
by
5.11
. In particular, using
2.12
and
2.14
, we conclude that
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0}
is nicely coverable by base change.

Now consider the overconvergent normed divisor
Z\coloneqq\{|u|=0\}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0}
and observe that

Z_{\varepsilon}\setminus Z=X^{\mathrm{N}}_{0<|u|\leq\varepsilon,|t|=0}\cong X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\times(0,\varepsilon]

for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
by
4.13
, where we note that
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0}\setminus Z=Z_{1}\setminus Z
. Thus, by
2.6
, we conclude that

\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\varepsilon>0}\Perf(Z_{\varepsilon}\setminus Z)\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0}\setminus Z)\;,

and now the desired equivalence
\Perf(X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=0})\cong\Perf(Z)
follows by applying
2.19
.
∎

6.
Six functors for the syntomification

Using the explicit presentations of Nygaardifications from the previous section, we are now in a position to prove that the functor
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
interacts nicely with the six functor formalism on Gelfand stacks. More explicitly, we want to prove the following properties:

(1)

The map
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf
\mathcal{O}\{1\}[3]
.

(2)

If
f:X\rightarrow Y
is a rigid smooth map of derived Berkovich spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
which is pure of relative dimension
d
, then the induced map
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}:X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf
\mathcal{O}\{d\}[2d]
.

(3)

If
f:X\rightarrow Y
is a map of derived Berkovich spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
which is locally of finite presentation and quasicompact, then
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is weakly cohomologically proper in the sense of
[
39
, Def. 3.1.19]
.

Finally, we will show that analytic syntomic cohomology affords a
strong theory of first Chern classes
in the sense of
[
55
, Def. 5.2.8]
. In particular, for any derived Berkovich space
X
, there is a natural map

c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}}:R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{Berk}\text{-}\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{G}_{m})[1]\rightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2])\;,

where
X_{\mathrm{Berk}\text{-}\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
denotes the Berkovich étale site of
X
, and this induces a natural isomorphism between the pushforward of the structure sheaf along
(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and the sheaf
\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}\{-1\}[-2]
.

In particular, this will let us deduce the following form of relative Poincaré duality for analytic syntomic cohomology: For any rigid smooth morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
of derived Berkovich spaces which is quasicompact and any perfect analytic
F
-gauge
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
on
X
, the pushforward
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}_{*}E
is perfect on
Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and there is an isomorphism

f^{\mathrm{Syn}}_{*}(E^{\vee}\{d\})[2d]\cong(f^{\mathrm{Syn}}_{*}E)^{\vee}\;.

For absolute syntomic cohomology, in view of the lack of weak cohomological properness of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, the only form of Poincaré duality we can obtain is an isomorphism

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},E^{\vee}\{d+1\})[2d+3]\cong R\Gamma_{c}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},E)^{\vee}

for any rigid smooth derived Berkovich space
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
which is pure of relative dimension
d
and any perfect analytic
F
-gauge
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
on
X
; here,
R\Gamma_{c}
denotes the
!
-pushforward to the point. Note that one can somewhat “see” the failure of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
to be weakly cohomologically proper from the geometry: Namely, this should intuitively be explained by the fact that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is “open at
\infty
” with respect to the radius map
\kappa:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
.

Let us quickly say something about the strategy of the proofs: As we can fully describe
X^{\mathrm{N}}
away from
p\in(0,\infty)
in terms of
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and the Hyodo–Kato stack
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
and both cohomological smoothness as well as weak cohomological properness may be checked locally on the source (in an open or finite closed cover, respectively), the main problem is to get a handle on
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}
. However, here the previous section provides explicit presentations in the cases
X=\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

To illustrate how one finishes from here, let us shortly discuss how we prove cohomological smoothness of the stack

(6.0.1)

\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\cong(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\;\Big/\;\mathcal{G}\;,

where the isomorphism is due to
5.8
. The main issue is the infinite limit along
p
-th power maps on the right-hand side of (
6.0.1
): each finite stage of the limit is cohomologically smooth, but the limit is not. To deal with this, similarly to what happens in
[
1
, Prop. 5.7.1]
or in
[
36
]
, the main idea is to approximate the quotient on the right-hand side of (
6.0.1
) by truncating the inverse limit in the “numerator” at finite stages and quotienting out by slightly larger groups
\mathcal{G}^{h\dagger}
for
h>0
; these are defined in the same way as
\mathcal{G}
, but with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
replaced by
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,h\dagger}
as defined in
[
36
, Def. 2.2]
. To make this work, the crucial point will be that the algebras of functions on finite stages of the limit

\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{[(p-1)/p,p^{2}(p-1)]}

equipped with the usual
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action given by
\gamma.q^{1/p^{m}}=q^{\gamma/p^{m}}
satisfy the Tate–Sen conditions from
[
36
, §3]
by a classical calculation of Berger, see
[
5
, Prop. 1.1.12]
and
[
6
, Thm. 4.4]
. Indeed, this will have the effect that the
h\dagger
-analytic vectors in the ring of functions on the limit are already contained in the ring of functions on some finite stage by
[
36
, Cor. 3.7]
