, and this is the key property that powers the approximation argument sketched above.

6.1.
Cohomological smoothness of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}

We start by proving cohomological smoothness of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
over
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. More precisely, we are going to prove:

Theorem 6.1
.

The map
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf
\mathcal{O}\{1\}[3]
.

Let us state
6.1
in a slightly more explicit form. For this, we first introduce some more notation.

Definition 6.2
.

Let
X
be any Gelfand stack and let
f:X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be the structure map. For any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
analytic syntomic cohomology with compact support
of weight
i
of
X
is defined by

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn},c}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))\coloneqq f_{!}\mathcal{O}\{i\}\;.

Corollary 6.3
.

Let
f:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be the structure map. For any perfect analytic
F
-gauge
E\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}})
, there is an isomorphism
f_{*}(E^{\vee}\{1\})[3]\cong(f_{!}E)^{\vee}
. In particular, we have

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))\cong R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn},c}(\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(1-i))^{\vee}[-3]

for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Note that
6.1
implies

(f_{!}E)^{\vee}=\Hom(f_{!}E,1)\cong\Hom(E,f^{!}1)\cong f_{*}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(E,f^{!}1)\cong f_{*}(E^{\vee}\{1\}[3])

by dualisability of
E
, see
2.3
.
∎

As already discussed above, the fact that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
can be completely described in terms of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
away from
p\in(0,\infty)
means that proving
6.1
largely comes down to analysing
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
in a neighbourhood of
p\in(0,\infty)
. Namely, the key step in the proof is the following result:

Proposition 6.4
.

The map
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf concentrated in degree
-2
.

To prove
6.4
, we will use the presentation

\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\cong(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\;\Big/\;\mathcal{G}

from
5.8
. To handle the inverse limit along
p
-th power maps, our strategy will be to invoke the very useful result
[
1
, Lem. 5.3.1]
, which we restate here for the reader’s convenience:

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
S
be a Gelfand stack and consider a diagram

X_{\infty}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow X_{m+1}\rightarrow X_{m}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow X_{0}

of Gelfand stacks over
S
with
!
-able transition maps
f_{\ell m}:X_{\ell}\rightarrow X_{m}
for
0\leq m\leq\ell\leq\infty
. Suppose that the following conditions hold:

(a)

All maps
f_{\ell m}
are prim.

(b)

For any
m
, the natural map
\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\ell\geq m}f_{\ell m,*}1\rightarrow f_{\infty m,*}1
is an isomorphism.

(c)

There is a prim
!
-cover
\widetilde{X}_{\infty}\rightarrow X_{\infty}
such that

\widetilde{X}_{\infty}\times_{X_{\infty}}\widetilde{X}_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}\widetilde{X}_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}}\widetilde{X}_{\infty}

in the category of kernels over
S
.

Then
X_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}X_{m}
in the category of kernels over
S
and, in particular,

\mathcal{D}(X_{\infty})\cong\mathop{\smash[b]{\sideset{}{{}_{!}}{\operatornamewithlimits{lim}}}\vphantom{\operatornamewithlimits{lim}}}_{m}\mathcal{D}(X_{m})

via
(f_{\infty m,!})_{m}
, where the transition functors in the limit are lower-
!
-functors. Moreover, an object
P\in\mathcal{D}(X_{\infty})
is suave over
S
if and only if
P_{m}\coloneqq f_{\infty m,!}P
is suave over
S
for all
m
and, in that case, the suave dual is given by

\SD(P)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}f_{\infty m}^{\flat}\SD(P_{m})\;,

where
f_{\infty m}^{\flat}\cong\delta_{\infty m}\otimes f^{*}_{\infty m}
is the left-adjoint of
f_{\infty m,!}
,
\delta_{\infty m}
is the codualising sheaf of
f_{\infty m}
and all suave duals
\SD(-)
are taken over
S
.

Proof.

See
[
1
, Lem. 5.3.1]
.
∎

To be able to apply
6.5
, let us first set up some notation: For
m\geq 3
, we let the Gelfand stack
X_{m}
be defined by the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{T}}(p^{-1/(p^{m-5/2}(p-1))},p^{-1/(p^{m-3/2}(p-1))})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{1/p^{m}}\mapsto\log q}

where
q^{1/p^{m}}
denotes the coordinate on
1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{T}}(p^{-1/(p^{m-5/2}(p-1))},p^{-1/(p^{m-3/2}(p-1))})
. Note that, by
5.6
and the discussion preceding loc. cit., there are canonical projection maps

X_{\infty}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}\rightarrow X_{m}

for each
m
.

Furthermore, recall that, for any integer
h\geq 1
, we denote by
\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{h})
the overconvergent disk of radius
1/p^{h}
, where we normalise the norm such that
|p|=1/p
; explicitly, this is the Gelfand stack
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T/p^{h}\rangle_{\leq 1}
. Using this and following
[
36
, Def. 2.2]
, we define

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,h\dagger}\coloneqq\bigsqcup_{\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}/(1+p^{h}\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\gamma(1+\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{h}))

and observe that this canonically admits the structure of a multiplicative group stack over
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then let

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}\coloneqq\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,h\dagger}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}})

and note that, over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, this receives two maps from
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
: The first one is given by
s\mapsto(1,u(1-s^{-1}),s)
, where
u
denotes the coordinate on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, while the second one is given by
s\mapsto(s,0,1)
. We define
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}
to be the coequaliser of these two maps in the category of group stacks over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. For notational convenience, we let
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\coloneqq\mathcal{G}_{0}
for
h=\infty
, which we recall was defined in the same way as
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}
above, but with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
replacing
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,h\dagger}
. Then observe that there are canonical maps
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h^{\prime}\dagger}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}
for all
h^{\prime}>h
induced by the canonical maps
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,h^{\prime}\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,h\dagger}
, where
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\infty\dagger}\coloneqq\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
.

Now note that the action of
\mathcal{G}_{0}
on
X_{\infty}
from
5.8
canonically extends to an action of
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}
on
X_{m}
for all
h\geq m
. Namely, the formula

(\gamma,w,s).(t,u,q^{1/p^{m}})=\left(\gamma st,\frac{u}{s}+w,q^{\gamma/p^{m}}\exp\left(\frac{\gamma wst}{p^{m}}\right)\right)_{m}

from loc. cit. makes sense for
\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,h\dagger}
whenever
h\geq m
: Indeed, first note that the exponential of
\gamma wst/p^{m}
does not play a role since
w\in\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
, and then recall from the proof of
5.10
that the condition on
q^{1/p^{m}}
implies that
|q-1|<p^{-1}
and hence
|\log q|<p^{-1}
, from which we conclude that
|\log q^{1/p^{m}}|<p^{m-1}
. As the
p
-adic exponential has radius of convergence
p^{-1/(p-1)}
, we thus see that
\exp((\gamma-1)\log q^{1/p^{m}})
defines a function on
1+\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{m})
, as desired.

With this notation in place, let us already say that our ultimate aim will be to apply
6.5
to the tower

X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow X_{m+1}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{(m+1)\dagger}\rightarrow X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow X_{3}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{3\dagger}\;,

the transition maps in which we denote by
f_{\ell m}:X_{\ell}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}
, and the
!
-cover
X_{\infty}\rightarrow X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}
. This will almost immediately yield
6.4
and thus our main task is to verify the conditions of
6.5
. We start with the following lemma:

Lemma 6.6
.

Fix
m\geq 3
and consider
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}
as a Gelfand stack over
X_{m}
via the composite map
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{m}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{act}}X_{m}
for each
h\geq m
, where the map
X_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
is the projection onto the
u
-coordinate. Then

\mathcal{G}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{h\geq m}\,(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty})

in the category of kernels over
X_{m}
.

Proof.

Our goal is to apply
6.5
to the diagram

\mathcal{G}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}=\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\infty\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}^{(h+1)\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}^{1\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}

and the prim
!
-cover
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}
of
\mathcal{G}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}
, which we note is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
-torsor. We let
g_{h^{\prime}h}:\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h^{\prime}\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}
for
h^{\prime}\geq h
denote the transition maps in the diagram above and note that these are induced by the canonical maps
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h^{\prime}\dagger}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}^{h\dagger}_{0}
.

Then first note that the diagram

(6.1.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h^{\prime}\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h^{\prime}\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}}

induced by the canonical map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,h^{\prime}\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,h\dagger}
is cartesian as both vertical maps are
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
-torsors and the horizontal map on the top is compatible with the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
-action; note that we have implicitly base changed
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}
to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
here, and we will do this throughout. Now observe that the base change of the horizontal map on the top along any closed embedding

(6.1.2)

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,h\dagger}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times(\{1/N\leq|s|\leq N\}\subseteq\mathbb{G}_{m})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}

is a map between affine Gelfand stacks (after also base changing to
\overline{\mathbb{D}}(N)\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{1}
). In other words,
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h^{\prime}\dagger}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}
is a map between affine Gelfand stacks
!
-locally on
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}
and thus it is weakly cohomologically proper by
[
39
, Lem. 3.1.21]
. In particular, the same is true for
g_{h^{\prime}h,*}
by base change.

Next, we show that
\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{h^{\prime}>h}g_{h^{\prime}h,*}1\rightarrow g_{\infty h,*}1
is an isomorphism for all
h\geq 1
. This may be checked
!
-locally on
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times X_{\infty}
and we already know that each
g_{h^{\prime}h}
is prim, hence satisfies base change by
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.13]
. Thus, letting
\widetilde{g}_{h^{\prime}h}:X_{\infty}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h^{\prime}\dagger}\rightarrow X_{\infty}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}
denote the canonical map, our claim reduces to checking that the natural map
\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{h^{\prime}>h}\widetilde{g}_{h^{\prime}h,*}1\rightarrow\widetilde{g}_{\infty h,*}1
is an isomorphism by (
6.1.1
). Further localising along the closed embeddings (
6.1.2
), which jointly cover
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}
, as well as passing to a strict closed cover of
X_{\infty}
by affine Gelfand stacks, this just reduces to the claim that

\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}})\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{h>0}\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,h\dagger})

via the natural map, which holds by definition.

Finally, we claim that

(6.1.3)

(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty})\times_{\mathcal{G}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty})\rightarrow\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{h}\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty})\times_{\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty})

is an isomorphism in the category of kernels over
X_{m}
. For this, note that
X_{\infty}
comes out of the fibre product and compute

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger})\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger})\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0})\;,

where the map
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}
is obtained by letting
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
act on
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}
by the difference of the two morphisms
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}
used to define
\mathcal{G}_{0}
. Twisting by this action, we conclude that

(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty})\times_{\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty})\cong(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\;,

where the maps
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}
are both the canonical maps. Observing that
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{h}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}
as derived Berkovich spaces, we conclude that

(6.1.4)

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{h}\,(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}^{h\dagger}}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}

as derived Berkovich spaces via the natural map and, by the above calculation, it suffices to show that this is also an isomorphism in the kernel category over
X_{m}
to be able to draw the same conclusion about (
6.1.3
).

However, this last claim follows by the argument from the proof of
[
1
, Lem. 5.4.8]
: Indeed, we may pass to strict closed covers of
X_{\infty}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{0}
which are compatible on the
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-factors and then all transition maps on the right-hand side of (
6.1.4
) will become maps between affine Gelfand stacks. Moreover, the structure map to
X_{m}
will also factor through an affine closed subspace of
X_{m}
and then the argument from the proof of
[
1
, Lem. 5.4.8]
applies. As already announced in the beginning, applying
6.5
finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 6.7
.

In the category of kernels over
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we have

X_{\infty}\times_{X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}}X_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}}X_{\infty}\;.

Proof.

For any
m
, we calculate

\begin{split}X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}}X_{\infty}&\cong X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}}(X_{m}\times_{X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}}X_{m})\times_{X_{m}}X_{\infty}\\
&\cong X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}}(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{m})\times_{X_{m}}X_{\infty}\cong X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}}(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty})\;,\end{split}

where the map
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\rightarrow X_{m}
is obtained as the composite
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{m}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{act}}X_{m}
. Now note that

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}\,(X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}}(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}))\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{h\geq m}\,(X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}}(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}))

in the category of kernels by cofinality and thus, it suffices to show that

(6.1.5)

\mathcal{G}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{h\geq m}\,(X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}}(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}))

in the category of kernels.

However, by
6.6
, we know that
\mathcal{G}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{h\geq m}\mathcal{G}_{0}^{h\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}
in the category of kernels over
X_{m}
and hence (
6.1.5
) reduces to

\mathcal{G}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}\,(X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}}(\mathcal{G}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}))\;.

Now note that
\mathcal{G}_{0}
acts on
X_{\infty}
and, twisting by this action, we can achieve that the map
\mathcal{G}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\rightarrow X_{m}
becomes the precomposition of the canonical map
X_{\infty}\rightarrow X_{m}
with the second projection. In other words, we can reduce further to showing that

\mathcal{G}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}\,(\mathcal{G}_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}}X_{\infty}))

and then it suffices to show that

X_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}\,(X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}}X_{\infty})

in the category of kernels over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. However, this is again deduced by the argument from
[
1
, Lem. 5.4.8]
as at the end of the proof of
6.6
: Indeed, we have
X_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}X_{m}
and hence
X_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}\,(X_{\infty}\times_{X_{m}}X_{\infty})
as derived Berkovich spaces.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

For all
m\leq\ell\leq\infty
, the map
f_{\ell m}:X_{\ell}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}
is weakly cohomologically proper.

Proof.

Note that
X_{\ell}\rightarrow X_{m}
becomes a map between affine Gelfand stacks after passing to a strict closed cover of
X_{m}
. Thus, the map
X_{\ell}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}
is a map between affine Gelfand stacks
!
-locally on the target and hence weakly cohomologically proper by
[
39
, Lem. 3.1.21]
. It thus remains to show that
*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}
is weakly cohomologically proper as we can then deduce the same for
X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}
by base change. In fact, we even claim that it is cohomologically proper.

For this, observe that
*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}
-torsor and note that
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}
is prim by
[
36
, Prop. 2.39.(5)]
. Thus, it suffices to show that the diagonal of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}
is cohomologically proper. However, note that there is a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}\times\mathrm{add}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}

in which the vertical arrow on the right is a
!
-cover and the horizontal arrow on the top is a closed embedding of derived Berkovich spaces, hence cohomologically proper: Indeed, twisting the target by the automorphism
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mapsto(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma)
identifies
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\times\mathrm{add}
with the map

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{can}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}\;.

Then note that
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}
is a map between affine Gelfand stacks and that
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger})
is an idempotent algebra over
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger})
: Indeed, if
\ell<\infty
, the map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}
is an embedding of a finite number of closed disks into a finite number of larger closed disks and if
\ell=\infty
, we have
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}})=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\ell^{\prime}<\infty}\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\ell^{\prime}\dagger})
and this is an idempotent
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{m\dagger})
-algebra as the same is true for each
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\ell^{\prime}\dagger})
. As cohomological properness is local on the target, we conclude that
\Delta
is cohomologically proper, and this finishes the proof.
∎

The key lemma that goes into establishing
6.4
is the following:

Lemma 6.9
.

For any fixed
m\geq 5
, the natural map
f_{\ell m,*}1\rightarrow f_{\infty m,*}1
is an isomorphism for
\ell\gg m
.

Proof.

As we already know that the
f_{\ell m}
are prim, see
6.8
, they satisfy base change by
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.13]
. Thus, we may check the claim locally on
X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}
and, in particular, after pullback to
X_{m}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}
via the canonical map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}
. For this, note that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{m}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\ell}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f}_{\ell m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\ell m}}

is cartesian as both vertical maps are
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
-torsors and the map
\widetilde{f}_{\ell m}
is compatible with the
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
-actions.

We may also replace
X_{m}
by

\overline{X}_{m}\coloneqq(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(1+\mathbb{T}(p^{-1/p^{m-3}},p^{-1/p^{m}}))\;,

where we write
\mathbb{T}(a,b)
for the rigid torus of inner radius
a
and outer radius
b
and the coordinate on the torus is
q^{1/p^{m}}
. To see this, first note that
\overline{X}_{m}
contains
X_{m}
as an open subspace and has the feature that
\overline{X}_{\ell}\times_{\overline{X}_{m}}X_{m}\cong X_{\ell}
for all
\ell\geq m
. We set
\overline{X}_{\infty}\coloneqq\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}\overline{X}_{m}
and note that

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}\{p^{-1/p^{m-3}}\leq|q^{1/p^{m}}-1|\leq p^{-1/p^{m}}\}\cong\left(\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{q\mapsto q^{p}}(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}})\setminus\{1\}\right)_{[(p-1)/p,p^{2}(p-1)]}\;.

Moreover, the formula
\gamma.q^{1/p^{m}}\mapsto q^{\gamma/p^{m}}
defining the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}
on
X_{m}
still makes sense on
\overline{X}_{m}
due to
|q-1|\leq p^{-1}
and hence
|\log q|\leq p^{-1}
on
\overline{X}_{m}
. As
X_{m}\rightarrow\overline{X}_{m}
is an open embedding and hence suave, by base change, see
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.3]
, it then suffices to show that
\overline{f}_{\ell m,*}1\cong\overline{f}_{\infty m,*}1
for
\ell\gg m
, where
\overline{f}_{\ell m}:\overline{X}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger}\rightarrow\overline{X}_{m}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}
is the canonical map.

Finally, we may also replace
\overline{X}_{m}
by the exhaustive family of closed subspaces

\overline{X}_{m,N}\coloneqq(\{|u|,|t|\leq N\}\subseteq\overline{X}_{m})

for
N\geq 0
and similarly for all
\overline{X}_{\ell}
. Then our task is to show that
\overline{f}_{\ell m,N,*}1\cong\overline{f}_{\infty m,N,*}1
for all
N
and all
\ell
large enough (independent of
N
), where
\overline{f}_{\ell m,N}
denotes the base change of
\overline{f}_{\ell m}
to
\overline{X}_{m,N}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger}
.

As
\overline{X}_{\ell,N}
is affine for all
\ell,N
, we have
\overline{f}_{\ell m,N,*}1\cong\mathcal{O}(\overline{X}_{\ell,N})^{m\dagger\text{-an}}
by
[
36
, Cor. 2.50]
and, similarly,
\overline{f}_{\infty m,N,*}1\cong\mathcal{O}(\overline{X}_{\infty,N})^{m\dagger\text{-an}}
. Thus, we have to prove that the natural map

\mathcal{O}(\overline{X}_{\ell,N})^{m\dagger\text{-an}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(\overline{X}_{\infty,N})^{m\dagger\text{-an}}

is an isomorphism for
\ell\gg 0
. Now observe that

\mathcal{O}(\overline{X}_{\ell,N})\cong\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}(N))\otimes\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}(N))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}(N^{2}))}B_{[(p-1)/p,p^{2}(p-1)],\ell}

for
B_{[(p-1)/p,p^{2}(p-1)],\ell}
being the ring of functions on
1+\mathbb{T}(p^{-1/p^{\ell-3}},p^{-1/p^{\ell}})
. Recalling that
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
acts trivially on one copy of
\overline{\mathbb{D}}(N)
and by multiplication on the other, we see that the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
on the
\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}(N))
-factors is even analytic globally and thus

\mathcal{O}(\overline{X}_{\ell,N})^{m\dagger\text{-an}}\cong\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}(N))\otimes\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}(N))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}(N^{2}))}B_{[(p-1)/p,p^{2}(p-1)],\ell}^{m\dagger\text{-an}}

by the projection formula for
m\dagger
-analytic vectors, see
[
36
, Prop. 2.39.(5)]
.

Therefore, our claim comes down to showing that

B_{[(p-1)/p,p^{2}(p-1)],\ell}^{m\dagger\text{-an}}\rightarrow B_{[(p-1)/p,p^{2}(p-1)],\infty}^{m\dagger\text{-an}}

is an isomorphism for
\ell
sufficiently large. However, this follows from
[
36
, Cor. 3.7]
as the family of Banach algebras
\{B_{[(p-1)/p,p^{2}(p-1)],\ell}\}_{\ell}
satisfies the Tate–Sen axioms from
[
36
, §3]
by
[
5
, Prop. 1.1.12]
and
[
6
, Thm. 4.4]
.
∎

Finally, we can prove
6.4
:

Proof of
6.4
.

We apply
6.5
to the tower

X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow X_{m+1}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{(m+1)\dagger}\rightarrow X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow X_{3}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{3\dagger}\;.

Note that condition (a) of loc. cit. holds by
6.8
and condition (b) holds by
6.9
. Finally, condition (c) holds for the
!
-cover of
X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}
by
X_{\infty}
due to
6.7
using the fact that
\mathcal{G}_{0}
is prim. Indeed, note that the canonical map
\mathcal{G}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}
is a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}}
-torsor and thus even weakly cohomologically proper by
[
39
, Lem. 3.1.21]
; moreover, the stack
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}=\GSpec C^{\mathrm{sm}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is affine and hence weakly cohomologically proper as well.

To conclude from
6.5
that
X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}
is suave, we have to check that
f_{\infty m,!}1\in\mathcal{D}(X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger})
is suave for all
m
. However, by
6.8
all maps
f_{\ell m}
are weakly cohomologically proper and, in particular, using
6.9
, we conclude that
f_{\infty m,!}1\cong f_{\ell m,!}1
for some finite
\ell\gg m
. As lower-
!
along prim maps preserves suave objects by
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.16]
, it thus suffices to show that
X_{\ell}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}
is suave. In fact, we are going to show that it is cohomologically smooth and that
f_{\ell m}^{*}\SD(1)\cong\SD(1)
whenever
\ell\geq m
, where
\SD(-)
denotes suave duals over the point.

For this, first observe that
X_{\ell}
is a complete intersection inside the open subspace

\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\times(1+\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{T}}(p^{-1/(p^{m-5/2}(p-1))},p^{-1/(p^{m-3/2}(p-1))}))

of
\mathbb{A}^{3}
cut out by the equation
ut=\log q
and hence cohomologically smooth. Moreover, the map
X_{\ell}\rightarrow X_{m}
is finite étale for all
\ell\geq m
and thus
X_{\ell}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}
is cohomologically étale. This together with suave base change for the cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

see
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.13]
, implies that the relative dualising sheaf of
X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}\rightarrow*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}
pulls back to the relative dualising sheaf of
X_{\ell}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}
.

Thus, it remains to show that
*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}
is cohomologically smooth and, for this, we note the factorisation

*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}\xrightarrow{g_{\ell}}*/(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger})^{\mathrm{dR}}\xrightarrow{h_{\ell}}*\;.

For
g_{\ell}
, note that it is a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}}
-gerbe and hence cohomologically smooth: Indeed, this is because the trivial
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}}
-gerbe is cohomologically smooth due to the factorisation

*/(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}})\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow*/\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow*\;,

in which the first map is cohomologically smooth as it is a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-gerbe, see
[
39
, Thm. 4.3.13]
, the second map is cohomologically smooth as it is a
(0,\infty)
-torsor by
2.22
and the last map is cohomologically smooth as this may be checked smooth-locally on the source and
*\rightarrow*/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is a cohomologically smooth
!
-cover due to
\mathbb{G}_{m}
being cohomologically smooth. Moreover, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*/(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,m\dagger})^{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{m}}

is cartesian as both horizontal maps are
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}}
-gerbes and the vertical map on the left is compatible with the
*/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rtimes\overline{\mathbb{T}}
-actions. Thus, by smooth base change, see
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.13]
, we conclude that the pullback of
g_{m}^{!}1
to
*/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}
naturally identifies with
g_{\ell}^{!}1
.

For
h_{\ell}
, note that it factors as

*/(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\ell\dagger})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow*/(1+\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell}))^{\mathrm{dR}}\overset{\log}{\cong}*/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow*/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow*\;,

where the first map is a gerbe for the finite abelian group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}/(1+p^{\ell}\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and hence cohomologically étale (this may be checked étale-locally on the source and finite sets are cohomologically étale), the second map is a torsor for
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})
and thus cohomologically smooth by
[
39
, Prop. 4.3.7]
and the last map is cohomologically smooth as this may be checked smooth-locally on the source and
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}
is cohomologically smooth by
[
1
, Thm. 5.1.7]
. For the compatibility with pullback, we only have to take care of the second map, where it suffices to give a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}
-equivariant isomorphism
\widetilde{h}_{\ell}^{!}1\cong\widetilde{h}_{\ell m}^{*}\widetilde{h}_{m}^{!}1
, where we write
\widetilde{h}_{\ell}:\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow*
, similarly for
m
, and
\widetilde{h}_{\ell m}:\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{m})^{\mathrm{dR}}
. Using the isomorphism
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})
, and similarly for
m
, it thus suffices to give an isomorphism
\overline{h}_{\ell}^{!}1\cong\overline{h}_{\ell m}^{*}\overline{h}_{m}^{!}1
, where we write
\overline{h}_{\ell}:*/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})\rightarrow*
, similarly for
m
, and
\overline{h}_{\ell m}:*/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{m})
. However, by
[
39
, Prop. 4.3.7]
, we have
\overline{h}_{\ell}^{!}1\cong 1[1]
, and similarly for
m
, which clearly yields the desired isomorphism.

Overall, we conclude that
X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}
is suave. To check that the dualising sheaf is invertible, we use the formula from
6.5
. Namely, as all
f_{\infty m}
are weakly cohomologically proper by
6.8
, the dualising sheaf is given by
\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{m}f_{\infty m}^{*}\SD(f_{\infty m,*}1)
, where
\SD(-)
denotes the suave dual over
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Using
6.9
and the fact that
f_{\ell m}
is weakly cohomologically proper by
6.8
, we obtain

\SD(f_{\infty m,*}1)\cong\SD(f_{\ell m,*}1)\cong f_{\ell m,*}\SD(1)\cong f_{\ell m,*}f_{\ell m}^{*}\SD(1)\cong\SD(1)\otimes f_{\ell m,*}1\cong\SD(1)\otimes f_{\infty m,*}1

for each fixed
m
and
\ell\gg m
, where we have used that the dualising sheaves on
X_{\ell}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\ell\dagger}
and
X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}
are compatible under pullback by the argument above. Thus, we find that, on
X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}
, we have

\SD(1)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{m}f_{\infty m}^{*}(\SD(1)\otimes f_{\infty m,*}1)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{m}f_{\infty m}^{*}\SD(1)\otimes\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{m}f_{\infty m}^{*}f_{\infty m,*}1\;.

The first tensor factor is simply
f_{\infty m}^{*}\SD(1)
for any
m
by compatibility of the dualising sheaves on the
X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}
under pullback while the second tensor factor is the tensor unit: Indeed, as
X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger}
in the category of kernels by
6.5
and the maps
f_{\ell m}
are all weakly cohomologically proper, we have

(f_{\infty m,*})_{m}:\mathcal{D}(X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0})\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatornamewithlimits{lim}\nolimits_{*}\mathcal{D}(X_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{0}^{m\dagger})\;,

where the transition functors in the limit are lower-
*
-functors. One readily checks that the left adjoint and hence the inverse to the functor
(f_{\infty m,*})_{m}
is given by
\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{m}f_{\infty m}^{*}
and this yields
\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{m}f_{\infty m}^{*}f_{\infty m,*}1\cong 1
, as desired.

Overall, we conclude that
X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}
is cohomologically smooth and that the dualising sheaf is obtained by pulling back any of the dualising sheaves of the stacks
X_{m}/\mathcal{G}^{m\dagger}
. Tracing through the arguments above and counting shifts, one finds that these dualising sheaves are all concentrated in degree
-3
and hence the dualising sheaf on
X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}
is concentrated in degree
-3
as well.

To finish the proof, recall from
5.8
that
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\cong X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}
and observe that there is a canonical map
\mathcal{G}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
with cokernel
\mathbb{G}_{m}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\cong(0,\infty)
, where we have used
2.22
. Thus, the induced map
X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is a
(0,\infty)
-torsor and hence
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is cohomologically smooth as this may be checked smooth-locally on the source. As the dualising sheaf of
(0,\infty)
is concentrated in degree
-1
and the dualising sheaf of
X_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{0}
is concentrated in degree
-3
by the previous paragraph, we conclude that the dualising sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is concentrated in degree
-2
, as desired.
∎

As already stated in the beginning, having
6.4
now enables us to prove
6.1
without too much difficulty:

Proof of
6.1
.

We first check that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf concentrated in degree
-3
. For this, we may work locally on an open cover of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
as cohomological smoothness is smooth-local on the source. We encourage the reader to consult
Fig.

3
and
Fig.

4
while following the argument.

We first show cohomological smoothness of

(6.1.6)

\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2}),|u|<1,|t|<1}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}\setminus(j_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}{\Delta})\cup j_{\mathrm{HT}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{-1/2},p^{1/2})}{\Delta}))\;,

which we note is an open subspace of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. For this, we observe that (
6.1.6
) is open inside
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N},\mathrm{ext}}
, which is a
(0,\infty)
-torsor over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
by
5.3
and hence cohomologically smooth by
6.4
. Moreover, by loc. cit., the dualising sheaf is concentrated in degree
-3
as the dualising sheaf of
(0,\infty)
is in degree
-1
.

Now consider
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|\neq 0,|u|<1-\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{N}}
for any
\varepsilon>0
and note that it is isomorphic to

\{|\widetilde{\mu}|<|t|(1-\varepsilon)\}\subseteq\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\times(0,1]\;,

where
|t|
is the coordinate on
(0,1]
. Indeed, this follows from the definition of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
using the isomorphism

\begin{split}\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}(1-\varepsilon)/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}&\cong(\{|r|<|t|(1-\varepsilon)\}\subseteq(0,1]\times(\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}(1-\varepsilon)/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}})\\
(t,u)&\mapsto(t,r)\coloneqq(t,ut)\;,\end{split}

where we have used
2.22
. Similarly, using the argument from the proof of
4.8
, we have

\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|\neq 0,|t|<1-\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong(\{|\widetilde{\mu}|<|u|(1-\varepsilon)\}\subseteq\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\times(0,1])\;,

where now
|u|
is the coordinate on
(0,1]
, induced by the isomorphism

\begin{split}\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}(1-\varepsilon)/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}&\cong(\{|r|<|u|(1-\varepsilon)\}\subseteq(\accentset{\circ}{\mathbb{D}}(1-\varepsilon)/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}})\times(0,1]\\
(t,u)&\mapsto(r,u)\coloneqq(ut,u)\;,\end{split}

where we use that
(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
by
2.22
to make the map well-defined. In particular, we conclude that

\begin{split}\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|>1/2,|u|<1/2}^{\mathrm{N}}&\cong(\{|\widetilde{\mu}|<|t|/2\}\subseteq\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\times(1/2,1])\\
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|>1/2,|t|<1/2}^{\mathrm{N}}&\cong(\{|\widetilde{\mu}|<|u|/2\}\subseteq\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\times(1/2,1])\end{split}

and gluing these two along the isomorphism

\mathbb{Q}_{p,|t|=1,|u|<1/2}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,|\widetilde{\mu}|<1/2}{\Delta}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,|u|=1,|t|<1/2}^{\mathrm{N}}

yields an open substack of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
isomorphic to
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|\widetilde{\mu}|<1/2}{\Delta}\times(1/2,3/2)
. As
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf concentrated in degree
-2
by
3.34
, we conclude that this is cohomologically smooth as well, with dualising sheaf concentrated in degree
-3
.

The open substacks we have considered so far cover open neighbourhoods of
\{|ut|=0\}
and
\{|u|=1\}\cup\{|t|=1\}
inside
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
, respectively. Thus, it suffices to consider
\mathbb{Q}_{p,0<|u|<1,0<|t|<1}^{\mathrm{N}}
for some
\varepsilon>0
, which we note is an open substack of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
as the gluing only happens at
\{|u|=1\}
and
\{|t|=1\}
. However, by
4.9
, we have

\mathbb{Q}_{p,0<|u|<1,0<|t|<1}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong(\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\setminus\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR}})\times(0,1)\;,

which is cohomologically smooth as
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\setminus\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR}}
is open inside
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
. Again, the dualising sheaf is concentrated in degree
-3
since the dualising sheaf of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
is concentrated in degree
-2
by
3.34
.

Overall, we conclude that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is cohomologically smooth and that its dualising sheaf is a line bundle
L
shifted in cohomological degree
-3
. By
9.16
below, to identify the line bundle, we just have to compute the corresponding line bundle on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
under the realisation functor
T_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
defined in §
9
below. This, in turn, amounts to computing the pullback of
L
to
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}
together with its transformation behaviour under Frobenius. For this, consider the composite map

\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}\xrightarrow{\id\times\{1/2\}}\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}\times(0,1)\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p),|u|<1,|t|<1}^{\mathrm{N}}\xrightarrow{j}\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}\;,

where the last map is an open embedding and the isomorphism comes from
4.9
. Letting
f:\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
denote the structure map, we have

\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2]\cong(\id\times\{1/2\})^{!}j^{!}f^{!}1\cong(\id\times\{1/2\})^{!}j^{*}L[3]

by
3.34
. To compute
(\id\times\{1/2\})^{!}j^{*}L
, let
j^{\prime}:(0,1/2)\cup(1/2,1)\rightarrow(0,1)
denote the canonical inclusion and recall that
\pi:\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}\times(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}
is the first projection; moreover, observe that
j^{*}L\cong\pi^{*}L^{\prime}
for some line bundle
L^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}
by
2.7
. Thus, we obtain

\begin{split}(\id\times\{1/2\})^{!}j^{*}L&\cong\pi_{*}(\id\times\{1/2\})_{*}(\id\times\{1/2\})^{!}\pi^{*}L^{\prime}\cong\pi_{*}\fib(\pi^{*}L^{\prime}\rightarrow j^{\prime}_{*}{j^{\prime}}^{*}\pi^{*}L^{\prime})\\
&\cong\fib(L^{\prime}\rightarrow L^{\prime}\oplus L^{\prime})\cong L^{\prime}[-1]\end{split}

by excision and
2.7
, from which we overall conclude that
L^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{O}\{1\}
. Thus, to conclude that
L\cong\mathcal{O}\{1\}
, we just have to check that the pullback of
L
to
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}{\Delta}
has the correct transformation behaviour under Frobenius, but this follows from
3.33
.
∎

6.2.
Preservation of smooth and proper maps

Let us move on to the relative case, i.e. to studying
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}
for a map
X\rightarrow Y
of derived Berkovich spaces. The most difficult part is to establish that
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is cohomologically smooth whenever
X\rightarrow Y
is rigid smooth. By compatibility of
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
with finite étale maps and open localisations, see
4.21
and
4.22
, this will quickly reduce to the case
X=\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
Y=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Moreover, similarly as in the proof of
6.1
, the key part will again be to understand the locus
\mathbb{G}_{m,(p^{1/2},p^{/3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}
. Thus, our first goal is to prove:

Proposition 6.10
.

The map
\mathbb{G}_{m,(p^{1/2},p^{/3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf concentrated in degree
-2
.

The method we will use is very similar to the method of the proof of
6.4
, and we again start by establishing some notation: Let
Y_{\infty}\coloneqq\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
and write
Y_{m}
for the
m
-th stage of this limit. For any integer
h\geq 1
, we write

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h\dagger}\coloneqq\bigsqcup_{\theta\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{h}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\theta+\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{h}))

and observe that this canonically admits the structure of an additive group stack over
\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Put

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{h\dagger}\coloneqq\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h\dagger}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}

and note that, over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, this group receives two maps from
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
: The first one is given by
\theta\mapsto(0,u\theta)
, where
u
denotes the coordinate on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, while the second one is given by
\theta\mapsto(\theta,0)
. We define
\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{h\dagger}
to be the coequaliser of these two maps in the category of group stacks over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. For convenience, we write
\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\infty\dagger}\coloneqq\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\coloneqq\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, where the pushout is along the multiplication-by-
u
-map on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
, as before. Observe that the canonical maps
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h^{\prime}\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h\dagger}
for
h^{\prime}>h
induce maps
\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{h^{\prime}\dagger}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{h\dagger}
. Finally, note that, over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
, the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
on
Y_{\infty}
canonically extends to an action of
\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{h\dagger}
on
Y_{m}
for all
h\geq m
as the formula from loc. cit. still makes sense by the argument from the discussion preceding
6.6
.

Working over the cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
from
5.8
throughout, our aim will be to apply
6.5
to the tower

\begin{split}Y_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow Y_{m+1}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{(m+1)\dagger}\rightarrow Y_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{m\dagger}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow Y_{1}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{1\dagger}\;,\end{split}

the transition maps in which we denote by
f_{\ell m}:Y_{\ell}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow Y_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{m\dagger}
, and the
!
-cover of
Y_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}
by
Y_{\infty}
. Let us record the following features of the situation:

Lemma 6.11
.

In the category of kernels over
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
, we have

Y_{\infty}\times_{Y_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}}Y_{\infty}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}Y_{\infty}\times_{Y_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{h\dagger}}Y_{\infty}\;.

Moreover, for all
m\leq\ell\leq\infty
, the map
f_{\ell m}:Y_{\ell}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow Y_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{m\dagger}
is weakly cohomologically proper.

Proof.

As in
6.7
and
6.8
.
∎

The next lemma should be compared to
6.9
and is again the key to proving
6.10
:

Lemma 6.12
.

For any fixed
m\geq 5
, the natural map
f_{\ell m,*}1\rightarrow f_{\infty m,*}1
is an isomorphism for
\ell\gg m
.

Proof.

By
6.11
, we know that the
f_{\ell m}
are prim and hence they satisfy base change by
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.13]
. Thus, as in the proof of
6.9
, we may replace the maps
f_{\ell m}
by the maps

\widetilde{f}_{\ell m}:Y_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow Y_{m}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{m\dagger}\;.

Moreover, we note that everything still makes sense over the base
\overline{X}_{\infty}
from loc. cit. in place of
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
and, by base change, it thus suffices to prove the claim over
\overline{X}_{\infty}
. By the same argument, we may then restrict to
\overline{X}_{\infty,N}\coloneqq\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{m}\overline{X}_{m,N}
for varying
N\gg 0
.

Finally, we may also localise on
Y_{1}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{1\dagger}
and restrict along

Y_{1,k}\coloneqq\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle p^{k}x^{\pm 1}\rangle\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}\cong Y_{1}\;.

For any
\ell
, we let
Y_{\ell,k}
denote the base change of
Y_{\ell}
along this map. We write
\widetilde{f}_{\ell m,k}:Y_{\ell,k}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow Y_{m,k}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{m\dagger}
for the induced transition maps.

Then we note that each
Y_{\ell,k}
is affine over the affine base
\overline{X}_{\infty,N}
and thus
\widetilde{f}_{\ell m,k,*}1\cong\mathcal{O}(Y_{\ell,k})^{m\dagger\text{-an}}
by
[
36
, Cor. 2.50]
and, similarly,
\widetilde{f}_{\infty m,k,*}1\cong\mathcal{O}(Y_{\infty,k})^{m\dagger\text{-an}}
. Thus, we have to prove that the natural map

\mathcal{O}(Y_{\ell,k})^{m\dagger\text{-an}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(Y_{\infty,k})^{m\dagger\text{-an}}

is an isomorphism for
\ell\gg 0
. However, this follows from
[
36
, Cor. 3.7]
as the family of Banach algebras
\{\mathcal{O}(Y_{\ell,k})\}_{\ell}
satisfies the Tate–Sen axioms (C1)–(C4) from
[
36
, §3]
by the following lemma.
∎

Lemma 6.13
.

For each
k\geq 1
, the family of Banach algebras
\{\mathcal{O}(Y_{\ell,k})\}_{\ell}
from the proof of
6.12
satisfies the Tate–Sen axioms (C1)–(C4) from
[
36
, §3]
.

Proof.

Let
A_{\ell}\coloneqq\mathcal{O}(Y_{\ell,k})
, note that

A_{\ell}=\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle p^{k}x^{\pm 1},p^{k/p}x^{\pm 1/p},\dots,p^{k/p^{\ell}}x^{\pm 1/p^{\ell}}\rangle

and let
A
denote the uniform completion of
\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\ell}A_{\ell}
. Then we have trace maps
R_{\ell}:A\rightarrow A_{\ell}
given by
x^{\lambda}\mapsto x^{\lambda}
if
|\lambda|_{p}\leq p^{\ell}
and
x^{\lambda}\mapsto 0
otherwise. Clearly, we have
|R_{\ell}(x)|\leq|x|
and
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}R_{\ell}(x)=x
for all
x\in A
while
R_{\ell}(x)=x
whenever
x\in A_{\ell}
, which already yields (C1). Moreover, we have already discussed above that the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
on
Y_{\ell}
is
\ell\dagger
-analytic and, in particular, locally analytic.

Now note that, for all
\theta\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with
v_{p}(\theta)<k-3
, we have

(6.2.1)

p^{-p^{v_{p}(\theta)-k+3}}\leq|q^{\theta/p^{k}}-1|\leq p^{-p^{v_{p}(\theta)-k}}

on
\overline{X}_{\infty}
. In particular, this implies that, whenever
x\in\Ker R_{\ell}
, we have

|(\theta-\id)(x)|\geq p^{-p^{v_{p}(\theta)-\ell+3}}|x|\;,

which yields (C2). Moreover, we observe that our work so far also shows that the algebras
\{A_{\ell}\}_{\ell}
satisfy the Colmez–Sen–Tate axioms from
[
40
, Def. 2.2.1]
and hence
[
40
, Lem. 2.4.3]
shows that (C4) is satisfied as well. Finally, we note that the upper bound in (
6.2.1
) is true for all
\theta\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and
k\geq 0
, which lets us conclude that

|(\theta-\id)(x)|\leq p^{-1/p^{\ell}}|x|

for all
x\in A_{\ell}
. In particular, we obtain (C3) and this finishes the proof.
∎

Finally, we can prove
6.10
:

Proof of
6.10
.

We apply
6.5
to the tower

Y_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow Y_{m+1}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{(m+1)\dagger}\rightarrow Y_{m}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{m\dagger}\rightarrow\dots\rightarrow Y_{1}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{1\dagger}

of Gelfand stacks over
S=\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
and the
!
-cover
Y_{\infty}\rightarrow Y_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}
. Indeed, conditions (a), (b) and (c) of loc. cit. are ensured by
6.11
and
6.12
, where we note that
\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}
is prim as it is a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-torsor over the affine Gelfand stack
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}
.

As in the proof of
6.4
, the fact that the
f_{\ell m}
are weakly cohomologically proper and
f_{\infty m,*}1\cong f_{\ell m,*}1
for each fixed
m
and
\ell\gg m
reduces the proof that
Y_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf concentrated in degree
-2
to showing that each
Y_{\ell}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\ell\dagger}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf concentrated in degree
-2
and that all these dualising sheaves are compatible under pullback along the transition maps
f_{\ell m}
.

For this, we first note that
X_{\ell}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}
for each
\ell
and that the maps
X_{\ell}\rightarrow X_{m}
are finite étale, hence cohomologically étale. Thus, we only have to worry about the classifying stacks
*/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\ell\dagger}
, where we note the factorisation

*/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\ell\dagger}\rightarrow*/(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\ell\dagger})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow*\;.

While the first map is a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-gerbe and hence cohomologically smooth by
[
39
, Thm. 4.3.13]
, the second map is cohomologically smooth as it factors as

*/(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\ell\dagger})^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow*/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})\rightarrow*/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow*\;,

where the first map is a gerbe for a finite group, hence cohomologically étale, the second map is a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\mathrm{dR}}/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}/\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p^{\ell})
-torsor, hence cohomologically smooth by
[
39
, Prop. 4.3.7]
, and the last map is the structure map of the classifying stack of a cohomologically smooth group by
[
1
, Thm. 5.1.17]
, hence cohomologically smooth as well. Moreover, the compatibility of the dualising sheaves under pullback follows by the same argument as in the proof of
6.4
.

Counting the shifts in the argument from the previous paragraph, we conclude that
Y_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}
is cohomologically smooth with dualising sheaf concentrated in degree
-2
. To finish the proof, recall from
5.10
that
\mathbb{G}_{m,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\cong Y_{\infty}/\mathcal{G}_{1,\mathrm{red}}
over
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}_{p\mathrlap{,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\hphantom{\scriptstyle{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}}
. As cohomological smoothness is local on the target, this shows that
\mathbb{G}_{m,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is cohomologically smooth over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
and then cohomological smoothness of
\mathbb{G}_{m,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}
follows by base change due to

\mathbb{G}_{m,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}}\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}\;.\qed

Having
6.10
, we can now easily deduce the following general cohomological smoothness result for maps between syntomifications:

Theorem 6.14
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a rigid smooth map of derived Berkovich spaces. Then the induced map
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}:X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is cohomologically smooth. If
f
is pure of relative dimension
d
, then the dualising sheaf is given by
\mathcal{O}\{d\}[2d]
.

Proof.

As cohomological smoothness is local on the target, it suffices to check the claim for
f^{\mathrm{N}}:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
. Then recall from
4.21
and
4.22
that the functor
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
preserves finite étale maps and open localisations. As finite étale maps and open embeddings are cohomologically étale and cohomological smoothness may be checked locally on the target and smooth-locally on the source, we may reduce to the case
X=\mathbb{A}^{d}_{Y}
. By commutation of
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
with limits and stability of cohomological smoothness under base change and composition, we may then further reduce to
Y=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Finally, we perform a further open localisation on the source by covering
\mathbb{A}^{1}
by
\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
1+\mathbb{G}_{m}
, and this allows us to reduce to
X=\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

However, now note that
\mathbb{G}_{m,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}
is cohomologically smooth by
6.10
. Moreover, we have

\mathbb{G}_{m,|ut|\neq 0}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong(\mathbb{G}_{m}{\Delta}\setminus\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{dR}})\times[0,1]

by
4.9
and similarly for
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|ut|\neq 0}^{\mathrm{N}}
, whence the map
\mathbb{G}_{m,|ut|\neq 0}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,|ut|\neq 0}^{\mathrm{N}}
is cohomologically smooth by
3.32
. As
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|ut|\neq 0}^{\mathrm{N}}
form an open cover of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
, this proves the claim by locality of cohomological smoothness on the target.

Overall, we conclude that
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}:X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is cohomologically smooth and it remains to compute the dualising sheaf. Going through the argument above, we already see that it is a line bundle concentrated in cohomological degree
-2d
. As at the end of the proof of
6.1
, we can use
9.16
below to conclude that it suffices to identify the pullback of this line bundle to
X{\Delta}_{(0,p)}
together with the automorphism induced by Frobenius. However, using smooth base change, see
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.13]
, this reduces to the computation of the dualising sheaf of
f^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}:X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
, and we already know that this is
\mathcal{O}\{d\}[2d]
by
3.31
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 6.15
.

Let
X
be a rigid smooth derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
which is pure of dimension
d
and let
f:X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be the structure map. For any perfect analytic
F
-gauge
E\in\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
, there is an isomorphism
f_{*}(E^{\vee}\{d+1\})[2d+3]\cong(f_{!}E)^{\vee}
. In particular, we have

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))\cong R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn},c}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(2d+1-i))^{\vee}[-2d-3]

for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

As in
6.3
.
∎

Remark 6.16
.

Let us note that any smooth map between partially proper rigid spaces is rigid smooth and hence satisfies the assumption of
6.14
.
∎

Finally, we would also like to establish a properness statement for maps between syntomifications. For this, we first make the following definition:

Definition 6.17
.

Let
X\rightarrow Y
be a map between derived Berkovich spaces. It is called
locally of finite presentation
if, after passing to a strict closed cover of the source and target by rational localisations, it is of the form
\GSpec B\rightarrow\GSpec A
for
B
a finitely presented animated
A
-algebra.

Remark 6.18
.

We emphasise that the finite presentation assumption above is in the derived sense. This means that a morphism of classical rings
A\rightarrow B
is finitely presented in this sense if and only if it factors as
A\rightarrow A[x_{1},\dots,x_{d}]\rightarrow B
, where
A[x_{1},\dots,x_{d}]\rightarrow B
is the quotient by a regular sequence.
∎

Using this terminology, the properness statement we prove can be formulated as follows. We note that it is likely that the finite presentation assumption below can be weakened, but we have chosen to stick to this version as it is more than enough for our purposes.

Theorem 6.19
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a map between derived Berkovich spaces which is quasicompact and locally of finite presentation. Then the induced map
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}:X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is weakly cohomologically proper.

Before we begin the proof, let us establish the following easier variant, to which we will later reduce by a dévissage:

Proposition 6.20
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a Berkovich smooth map between derived Berkovich spaces which is quasicompact. Then the induced map
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}:X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is weakly cohomologically proper.

Proof.

We first check that
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is prim. This may be done locally on the target and hence reduces to checking primness of
f^{\mathrm{N}}:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
. Now recall from
4.21
and
4.22
that
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
preserves finite étale maps and rational localisations and note that any finite étale map or rational localisation is cohomologically proper; in particular, the functor
(-)^{\mathrm{N}}
sends Berkovich étale maps to cohomologically proper maps. As the map
f:X\rightarrow Y
is quasicompact and primness is local on the target as well as local for finite closed covers of the source, we may assume that
f
factors as
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{d}_{Y}\rightarrow Y
with the first map being Berkovich étale, hence inducing a cohomologically proper map Nygaardifications. By quasicompactness of
f
, the map
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{d}_{Y}
factors through a closed polydisk, which we may assume to be
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{d}_{Y}
after rescaling. We are thus reduced to
X=\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{d}_{Y}
and, by compatibility of
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
with limits and stability of primness under base change, we may further reduce to
X=\overline{\mathbb{D}}
and
Y=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Finally, we may embed
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
into
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
as the closed subspace
1+\overline{\mathbb{D}}(1/p)
and hence we may assume
X=\overline{\mathbb{T}}
.

Let us now study the case
Y=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
X=\overline{\mathbb{T}}
. By
5.10
, we have

\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;\Big/\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}

after base changing to a cover of
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
. Now note that
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is affine, hence weakly cohomologically proper and the map

*\;\Big/\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rightarrow*

is prim as well. Indeed, this factors as

*\;\Big/\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rightarrow*/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}\rightarrow*\;,

where the first map is a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-gerbe, hence prim by
[
39
, Prop. 4.2.5]
, and the second map is prim as
*\rightarrow*/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}
is a weakly cohomologically proper
!
-cover such that the relative cohomology along this map is descendable in
\mathcal{D}(*/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}})
by
[
39
, Prop. 6.2.3]
. Thus, we conclude that
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is prim, whence
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{N}}_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}
is prim by base change.

As primness is local on the target, it thus suffices to show that
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{N}}_{|ut|\neq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,|ut|\neq 0}^{\mathrm{N}}
is prim as well. In this case, recall from
4.9
that

\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{N}}_{|ut|\neq 0}\cong(\overline{\mathbb{T}}{\Delta}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}})\times[0,1]

and similarly for
\mathbb{Q}_{p,|ut|\neq 0}^{\mathrm{N}}
, hence it suffices to show that
\overline{\mathbb{T}}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}
is prim. By
3.23
and
3.28
, this in turn reduces to primness of
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
and
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
. However, this can be seen from the presentations

\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{HK}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\;\Big/\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}\;,\hskip 14.22636pt\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;\Big/\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}

from
3.29
and
3.30
: Indeed, as

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\;,

and
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is affine, the claim reduces the primness of
*/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}},*/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}
and
*/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}
. However,
*/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}
is a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-gerbe over
*/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}
and
*/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\dagger}\cong*/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
via the logarithm. As
*/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
is prim by
[
39
, Prop. 4.2.5]
and we have already argued above that
*/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}
is prim, this proves the claim.

We conclude that
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is prim and thus it remains to check that the diagonal
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\times_{Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}}X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\cong(X\times_{Y}X)^{\mathrm{Syn}}
of
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is weakly cohomologically proper. We will do this by showing that,
!
-locally on the target, this factors into maps which are maps between affine Gelfand stacks
!
-locally on the target; this suffices by the proof of
[
39
, Lem. 3.1.21]
. However, then the same argument as above again reduces us to the case where
X
admits a Berkovich étale map to a closed polydisk
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{d}_{Y}
over
Y
, and then by base change to
Y=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. To move on, note the factorisation

X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\times_{(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{d})^{\mathrm{Syn}}}X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}}X^{\mathrm{Syn}}

of the diagonal map of
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. As the first map is the diagonal of a map which is Berkovich étale
!
-locally on the target, it is an isomorphism and thus we are reduced to studying the second map, which we observe is a base change of the diagonal map of
(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{d})^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. By base change and composition, we reduce to
d=1
and can then again replace
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
by
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
as above. Base changing along
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, we furthermore reduce to the corresponding question for Nygaardifications.

Localising on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
as above, we may separately check the claim for the diagonals of the maps

\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}\;,\hskip 8.5359pt\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\;,\hskip 8.5359pt\text{and}\hskip 8.5359pt\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\;.

Base changing the presentations of the diagonals of the maps
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
obtained from
3.29
and
3.30
to
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}
, we easily see that we get maps between affine Gelfand stacks. For the map
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
,
5.10
shows that the diagonal is given by

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;\Big/\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;\Big/\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\times\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;\Big/\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}

and the base change of this to
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\right)\rightarrow\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;.

Observing that this map admits a factorisation as

\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\right)\rightarrow\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{sm}}\rightarrow\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;,

where the first map is a
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
-torsor and the second map is a map between affine Gelfand stacks then finally finishes the proof.
∎

We are now ready to prove
6.19
.

Proof of
6.19
.

We first show that
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is prim. As primness is local on the target, we may instead consider the map
X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
. Since primness is furthermore local for finite closed covers of the source, the quasicompactness of
X\rightarrow Y
and
4.22
imply that we can reduce to the case where
Y
is affine and
f
factors as
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{d}_{Y}\rightarrow Y
, where the first map is a Zariski-closed immersion given by quotienting out finitely many equations due to our finite presentation assumption. As
f
is quasicompact, the map
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{d}_{Y}
factors through some closed polydisk, which we may assume to be of radius
1
. Then
(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{d}_{Y})^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{N}}
is prim by
6.20
and hence we are reduced to
X\rightarrow Y
being a Zariski-closed immersion of affine Gelfand stacks given by finitely many equations. By composition, we may reduce to the case of a single equation and then
X\rightarrow Y
is pulled back from
\{0\}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
along the map
Y\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
classifying the equation cutting out
X
. Thus, it suffices to show that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{\mathrm{N}}
is prim, but this follows using cancellation from the fact that the diagonal of
(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
is prim by
6.20
.

Having established that
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is prim, it suffices to show that the diagonal is weakly cohomologically proper, for which, as in the proof of
6.20
, we show that,
!
-locally on the target, the map
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\times_{Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}}X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
factors into maps which are maps between affine Gelfand stacks
!
-locally on the target. The same dévissage as in the previous paragraph then reduces us to Nygaardifications and the cases
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
Y=\mathbb{P}^{1}
as well as
X=\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{d}_{Y}
. In the latter case, we note that the claim follows from the proof of
6.20
. For the map
\{0\}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
, we first note that its diagonal agrees with the diagonal of
\{1\}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}
, which, as in the proof of
6.20
, finally reduces us to considering the diagonals of

\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{HK}}\;,\hskip 8.5359pt\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\;,\hskip 8.5359pt\text{and}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\;.

As
\{1\}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}
induces a proper map on diamonds, the diagonal of the first map is even cohomologically proper by
[
1
, Thm. 6.3.1.(1)]
. For the second map, we use the presentation from
3.29
to see that the base change of its diagonal along the cover
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
identifies with

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\times_{\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\;,

which is a map between affine Gelfand stacks. For the last map,
5.10
shows that the base change of its diagonal along the cover
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
is given by

\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\right)\rightarrow\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\right)\times_{\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\right)\;.

Further base changing along the self-product of the cover
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
over
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}
then yields

(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})\rightarrow(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})\times_{\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})\;.

As the source is isomorphic to
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
, this is a map between affine Gelfand stacks, which concludes the proof.
∎

Let us note the following consequence of
6.19
.

Corollary 6.21
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a rigid smooth map of derived Berkovich spaces which is quasicompact. Then the induced map
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}:X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow Y^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is cohomologically smooth and weakly cohomologically proper. In particular, for any perfect analytic
F
-gauge
E\in\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
on
X
, the pushforward
f_{*}^{\mathrm{Syn}}E
is perfect. If
f
is additionally pure of relative dimension
d
, then there is an isomorphism

f^{\mathrm{Syn}}_{*}(E^{\vee}\{d\})[2d]\cong(f^{\mathrm{Syn}}_{*}E)^{\vee}\;.

Proof.

For the first part, put together
6.14
and
6.19
. Then the second part follows from
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.16]
and the third part follows by an analogous calculation as in the proof of
6.3
using that the dualising sheaf of
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is given by
\mathcal{O}\{d\}[2d]
.
∎

Remark 6.22
.

We point out that any smooth proper map
f:X\rightarrow Y
between partially proper rigid spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
satisfies the assumptions of
6.21
.
∎

6.3.
Chern classes

To conclude our discussion of the interaction of the syntomification with six functors, we want to establish that the syntomification of derived Berkovich spaces admits a strong theory of first Chern classes in the sense of
[
55
, §5]
. Concretely, this amounts to the following statement:

Proposition 6.23
.

For any derived Berkovich space
X
, there is a natural morphism

c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}}:R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{Berk}\text{-}\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{G}_{m})[1]\rightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2])\;,

where the left-hand side denotes the cohomology of
\mathbb{G}_{m}[1]
on the Berkovich étale site of
X
. Moreover, for any
d\geq 1
and any derived Berkovich space
X
, the induced morphism

(6.3.1)

\sum_{0\leq k\leq d}(c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}})^{k}\{d-k\}[2d-2k]:\bigoplus_{0\leq k\leq d}\mathcal{O}\{d-k\}[2d-2k]\rightarrow f_{*}^{\mathrm{Syn}}\mathcal{O}\{d\}[2d]

is an isomorphism, where
f:\mathbb{P}^{d}_{X}\rightarrow X
is the projection.

Before we move on to the proof, let us shortly comment on how
c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
induces a morphism (
6.3.1
). Namely, note that
c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
in particular induces a map

H^{1}((\mathbb{P}^{d}_{X})_{\mathrm{Berk}\text{-}\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{G}_{m})\rightarrow H^{0}((\mathbb{P}^{d}_{X})^{\mathrm{Syn}},\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2])\;.

Thus, the tautological line bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{d}_{X}
determines a class in
H^{0}((\mathbb{P}^{d}_{X})^{\mathrm{Syn}},\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2])
, which we may interpret as a map
c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}}:\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2]
on
(\mathbb{P}^{d}_{X})^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. By adjunction, this corresponds to a map
c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}}:\mathcal{O}\rightarrow f^{\mathrm{Syn}}_{*}\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2]
. Similarly, the tensor powers
(c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}})^{k}:\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\{k\}[2k]
correspond to maps
(c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}})^{k}:\mathcal{O}\rightarrow f^{\mathrm{Syn}}_{*}\mathcal{O}\{k\}[2k]
, and these are the maps which yield (
6.3.1
).

Proof of
6.23
.

Note that the functor
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
sends Berkovich étale covers to
!
-covers: Indeed, by locality on the target, this reduces to the claim for
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
, where it is due to
4.20
. Thus, the assignment
X\mapsto R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2])
satisfies descent for Berkovich étale covers, whence it suffices to construct a natural transformation

\tau^{\leq 0}R\Gamma(-,\mathbb{G}_{m}[1])\rightarrow\tau^{\leq 0}R\Gamma((-)^{\mathrm{Syn}},\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2])\;.

Indeed, the sheafification of the source for the Berkovich étale topology will be given by the
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z})
-valued sheaf
R\Gamma((-)_{\mathrm{Berk}\text{-}\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{G}_{m}[1])
, and this will yield the desired map.

To this end, by
[
1
, Rem. 5.1.10.(3)]
, it suffices to construct a map

c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}}:\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow*/\mathbb{G}_{a}\{1\}

of Gelfand stacks over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. Then the desired natural transformation will be given by

\begin{split}\tau^{\leq 0}R\Gamma(X,\mathbb{G}_{m}[1])\rightarrow\map(X,*/\mathbb{G}_{m})&\xrightarrow{\hphantom{*/c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}}}}\map(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},*/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{Syn}})\\
