&\xrightarrow{*/c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}}}\map(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},*/(*/\mathbb{G}_{a}\{1\}))\cong\tau^{\leq 0}R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},\mathcal{O}\{1\}[2])\;.\end{split}

Clearly, it will suffice to construct a map
c_{1}^{\mathrm{N}}:\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow*/\mathbb{G}_{a}\{1\}
of Gelfand stacks over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
and then to check that the restrictions of this map along
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
induce the same map
\mathbb{G}_{m}{\Delta}\rightarrow*/\mathbb{G}_{a}\{1\}
. Moreover, we may proceed locally on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
as morphisms glue.

Over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(0,p)}^{\mathrm{N}}
, we have
\mathbb{G}_{m,(0,p)}^{\mathrm{N}}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m,(0,p)}{\Delta}\times[0,1]
by
4.9
. Furthermore, using
3.28
, we may reduce to constructing a map
c_{1}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}:\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow*/\mathbb{G}_{a}\{1\}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
. However, recalling from
3.29
that

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\,\Big/\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}

and noting that the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}
that occurs here is actually implicitly Breuil–Kisin twisted by
1
, the desired map
c_{1}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
is induced by the canonical map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}
and the projection
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow*
.

Similarly, over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p,\infty)}^{\mathrm{N}}
, our problem reduces to constructing a map
c_{1}^{\mathrm{HK}}:\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow*/\mathbb{G}_{a}\{1\}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
using
4.9
and
3.23
. However, for this we take the first Chern class for Hyodo–Kato cohomology constructed in
[
1
, Lem. 6.2.9]
.

Finally, we have to construct the map
c_{1}^{\mathrm{N}}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}
. To this end, we will actually construct a map

c_{1}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}:\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\rightarrow*/\mathbb{G}_{a}\{1\}

over
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}
and then the desired map will be obtained by base changing to
\mathbb{Q}_{p,(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})}^{\mathrm{N}}
. Now recall from
5.10
that

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\overline{\mathrm{N}}}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\;\Big/\;\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\coprod_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}}^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}

over this locus, where the pushout is along multiplication by
u
on
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
. Noting that the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}
that occurs here should really be twisted by
\pi^{*}\mathcal{O}\{1\}
while the
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
should be twisted by
\mathcal{O}\{1\}\cong\pi^{*}\mathcal{O}\{1\}\otimes t^{*}\mathcal{O}(-1)
, the desired map is induced by the projection
\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow*
and the maps

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}\xrightarrow{\cdot u}\mathbb{G}_{a}\;,\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}\;.

One easily checks that the maps from the previous paragraphs glue and that they induce the same map after pullback along
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
, so that we obtain the desired map
c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. Thus, it remains to verify the claim about the relative syntomic cohomology of projective space. As the map
(\mathbb{P}^{d})^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is weakly cohomologically proper by
6.19
, we are reduced to
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by proper base change. In that case, we note that
(\mathbb{P}^{d})^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is also cohomologically smooth by
6.14
and thus
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}_{*}\mathcal{O}\{d\}[2d]
is a perfect complex. Therefore, by
8.3
below, we may check the claimed isomorphism (
6.3.1
) after applying the realisation functors
T_{\mathrm{dR},+}
and
T_{\mathrm{HK}}
defined in §
8
below. However, as the syntomic first Chern class
c_{1}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
we have constructed is compatible with the first Chern class
c_{1}^{\mathrm{HK}}
for Hyodo–Kato cohomology from
[
1
, Lem. 6.2.9]
and the usual first Chern class
c_{1}^{\mathrm{dR},+}
for filtered de Rham cohomology by
[
1
, Rem. 5.1.10]
, the claim now follows from the fact that relative Hyodo–Kato cohomology and relative filtered de Rham cohomology of projective space may be computed in terms of Chern classes. Indeed, in the second case this is classical and in the first case it is due to
[
1
, Lem. 6.2.9]
. This finishes the proof.
∎

7.
p
-adic Hodge theory for rigid-analytic varieties revisited

Having developed plenty of theory around the syntomification in the previous sections, we now want to show how to put it to use. As our first goal, we want to show how one can recover some results from Scholze’s paper
[
43
]
using the syntomification. To this end, we are going to analyse the equivalence

(7.0.1)

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})

from
5.18
and relate it to the functor

(7.0.2)

\{\text{filtered vector bundles with connection on $X$}\}\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on }X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\}

from
[
43
, §7]
.

Namely, from (
7.0.1
), we obtain a natural functor

\begin{split}\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})&\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})\\
&\rightarrow\Vect((\widehat{X}\subseteq Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}})^{\wedge})\cong\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on }X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\}\end{split}

via pullback and we are going to identify this functor with (
7.0.2
). Indeed, using the results of §
5.3
, we will be able to make the above functor explicit in terms of linear algebra when
X
admits a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
and, in this case, our claim reduces to a fairly simple computation. Then the key is to show that these local identifications glue, i.e. that they are independent of the chosen toric chart
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
.

Finally, we will also be able to recover the classical de Rham comparison theorem

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}\cong R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}

for smooth proper rigid spaces
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
using the syntomification. In fact, we will even be able to recover the relative version for smooth proper morphisms
X\rightarrow Y
from
[
43
, Thm. 8.8]
and, as in loc. cit., using the results of §
9
, we will also be able to allow for coefficients in any de Rham local system on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
.

7.1.
Scholze’s functor via the syntomification

To begin, recall the
structural de Rham sheaf

\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
on the proétale site
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
of any smooth partially proper rigid space
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
defined in
[
43
, §6]
, see also
[
45
]
. This is a
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-algebra equipped with a descending filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
and a
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-linear connection

\nabla:\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega_{X}^{1}\;.

Using this, Scholze defines a functor

(7.1.1)

\begin{split}\{\text{filtered vector bundles with connection on $X$}\}&\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}\\
(\Fil^{\bullet}E,\nabla)&\mapsto\Fil^{0}(E\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}})^{\nabla=0}\;,\end{split}

which he proves to be fully faithful, see
[
43
, Thm. 7.6]
; note that whenever we say “filtered vector bundle with connection”, the filtration is always assumed to be locally by direct summands and the connection is always assumed to be flat and Griffiths transversal with respect to the filtration, i.e. it carries
\Fil^{i}E
into
\Fil^{i-1}E\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega_{X}^{1}
.

Now recall that there is a natural map

\iota:\widehat{X}\rightarrow\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}

which identifies the source with a Cartier divisor in the target and is induced by the maps
\theta:\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\overline{A})\rightarrow\overline{A}
for any totally disconnected perfectoid
\overline{A}
over
X
. Thus, we see that the infinitesimal neighbourhood of the image of
\iota
in
\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
is given by

(\widehat{X}\subseteq\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})^{\wedge}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{n}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow X}\GSpec\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\overline{A})/\xi^{n}\;,

where the colimit runs over all totally disconnected perfectoids
\overline{A}
over
X
and
\xi
denotes a generator of
\Ker\theta
. As
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\overline{A})
is
\xi
-complete, we conclude that

(7.1.2)

\Vect((\widehat{X}\subseteq\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})^{\wedge})\cong\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}\;.

Furthermore, observe that the natural map
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
from
3.19
restricts to a map

(\widehat{X}\subseteq\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})^{\dagger}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}

from the overconvergent neighbourhood of
\widehat{X}
in
\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
to
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
. Further restricting this map to the infinitesimal neighbourhood and using the equivalence (
7.1.2
), we obtain a pullback functor

(7.1.3)

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}\;.

Putting (
7.1.3
) together with the equivalence
\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})
, which one deduces from
5.18
as the argument in loc. cit. just as well applies to any
\Perf^{[a,b]}(-)
in place of
\Perf(-)
, we arrive at a composite functor

(7.1.4)

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}\;,

where the functor
\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})
is of course given by pullback. Our main result in this section is the following:

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Under the identification between vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
and filtered vector bundles with connection on
X
from
2.40
, the functors (
7.1.1
) and (
7.1.4
) are naturally equivalent.

Remark 7.2
.

In principle, we could extract even finer information from a vector bundle on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
. Namely, note that

(\widehat{X}\subseteq\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})^{\dagger}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\GSpec\overline{A}\rightarrow X}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}(\overline{A})\;,

where the colimit again runs over all totally disconnected perfectoids
\overline{A}
over
X
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
is the
overconvergent de Rham period sheaf
on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
from
[
54
, Def. 3.4.1]
. Thus, we even obtain a pullback functor

\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger})

and, in particular, the functor (
7.1.4
) refines to a functor

(7.1.5)

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}\;.

Note that Scholze’s functor (
7.1.1
) admits such a refinement as well: Namely, this is given by

(7.1.6)

\begin{split}\{\text{filtered vector bundles with connection on $X$}\}&\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}\\
(\Fil^{\bullet}W,\nabla)&\mapsto\Fil^{0}(W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{\dagger})^{\nabla=0}\;,\end{split}

where
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
is the structural overconvergent de Rham period sheaf from
[
54
, §3.5]
.

Naturally, one might wonder whether
7.1
refines to an equivalence between the functors (
7.1.5
) and (
7.1.6
). Indeed, one can check that the proof below still works in this situation: One just has to replace the use of
[
43
, Prop. 6.10]
by
[
54
, Thm. 3.5.7]
and, moreover,
[
43
, Thm. 7.2]
is replaced by the analogous fact that, for any
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra
R
, a finite projective
R\{X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\}^{\dagger}
-module with a flat connection has enough horizontal sections, see e.g. the proof of
[
33
, Thm. 9.6.1]
.
∎

The key to proving
7.1
is the following explicit identification of the functor
\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})
from (
7.1.4
) in the case where
X
is equipped with a toric chart.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
X
be a derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
equipped with a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
. For any perfect complex
E
on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
, the filtration
\Fil_{\bullet}V
from
5.15
splits
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-equivariantly, i.e., in the notation of loc. cit., there is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant isomorphism

\Fil_{\bullet}V\cong\left(\bigoplus_{i\leq\bullet}t^{-i}(\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}[r]}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{r\}^{\dagger}\;,

where
r
acts via the transition maps
t^{-i}\Fil^{i}W\rightarrow t^{-i+1}\Fil^{i-1}W
, the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
-action is induced by the one on
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
acts on
t
by multiplication and on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
via the usual Galois action.

Proof.

Recall from loc. cit. that there are
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant isomorphisms

\gr_{i}V\cong t^{-i}(\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}))

for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and for the filtration to split it suffices to show that

\RHom(\gr_{j}V,\gr_{i}V)=0

for all
i\neq j
, where the
\RHom
is taken in
\mathcal{D}(X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,/\,(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{la}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}})
. As
\gr_{j}V
is a perfect complex and hence dualisable, this amounts to showing that the cohomology of

(7.1.7)

\gr_{i}V\otimes(\gr_{j}V)^{\vee}\cong t^{-i+j}(\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\Fil^{j}W)^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}))

on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,/\,(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{la}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
vanishes.

However, note that pushforward along

X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,/\,(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{la}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\,/\,(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n})^{\mathrm{sm}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{sm}}\cong X

is given by taking Lie algebra cohomology. Moreover, by (
7.1.7
), the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
on
\gr_{i}V\otimes(\gr_{j}V)^{\vee}
is smooth while the Lie algebra of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
acts on
\gr_{i}V\otimes(\gr_{j}V)^{\vee}
by multiplication by
j-i\neq 0
. As
j-i
is invertible, this yields

R\Gamma(\operatorname{Lie}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}),\gr_{i}V\otimes(\gr_{j}V)^{\vee})=0\;,

whence the pushforward of
\gr_{i}V\otimes(\gr_{j}V)^{\vee}
to
X
and consequently also its cohomology vanishes, as desired.
∎

The above splitting of the filtration
\Fil_{\bullet}V
already allows us to prove
7.1
in the case where
X
admits a toric chart
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
equipped with an étale map
X\rightarrow\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle
such that the composition

X\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}

is Berkovich étale. Then the functor

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}

from (
7.1.4
) agrees with Scholze’s functor

\{\text{filtered vector bundles with connection on $X$}\}\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}

from (
7.1.1
) under the identification between vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
and filtered vector bundles with connection on
X
from
2.40
.

Proof.

Let
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
denote the adic space

X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\coloneqq X\times_{\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle}\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle

and observe that this is perfectoid. By
7.3
, our task is to check that the base change of

(7.1.8)

\left(\bigoplus_{i}t^{-i}(\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}))\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}[t]}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{t\}^{\dagger}

along the map
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\{t\}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}
identifies
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-equivariantly with

\Fil^{0}(W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}})^{\nabla=0}\;.

Here, we regard
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}
as sheaves on the underlying topological space of
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
; indeed, note that the underlying topological space of the derived Berkovich space
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times_{\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
in the sense of
[
1
, Def. 4.3.1]
is the maximal Hausdorff quotient of the underlying topological space of
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
and hence
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}
can be regarded as a sheaf on the latter via pullback. Moreover, we note that
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\{t\}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}
is the unique map given by
x_{i}^{1/p^{m}}\mapsto[x_{i}^{\flat}]^{1/p^{m}}
and
t\mapsto\log[\varepsilon]\in\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}})
, where we use the proof of
[
43
, Prop. 6.10]
for the existence and uniqueness and write
\varepsilon=(1,\zeta_{p},\zeta_{p^{2}},\dots)
, as usual.

With these preliminaries out of the way, let us move on to the actual proof. Examining the formula (
7.1.8
), it is clear that it will suffice to prove that there is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
-equivariant isomorphism

(7.1.9)

\Fil^{0}(W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}})^{\nabla=0}\cong\sum_{i}\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}t^{-i}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}

of sheaves on the localised site
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}/X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
, where the map
\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
is obtained in the same way as the map
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}
above. However, note that, using
[
43
, Prop. 6.10]
, this map may also be obtained as the composition

\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\cong\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}[\![X_{1},\dots,X_{n}]\!]\rightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\;,

where the last map is reduction mod
(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})
for
X_{i}=x_{i}-[x_{i}^{\flat}]
. Now observe that the natural map

\left(\sum_{i}\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}t^{-i}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\right)^{\nabla=0}\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\rightarrow\sum_{i}\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}t^{-i}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}

is an isomorphism by
[
43
, Thm. 7.2]
. Thus, reducing mod
(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})
, we see that

\left(\sum_{i}\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}t^{-i}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\right)^{\nabla=0}\cong\sum_{i}\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}t^{-i}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}

and this yields the desired isomorphism (
7.1.9
) by
7.5
below.
∎

The following lemma was used in the proof:

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For any filtered vector bundle with connection
(\Fil^{\bullet}W,\nabla)
on
X
, the natural map

\Fil^{0}(W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}})^{\nabla=0}\xrightarrow{\cong}\left(\sum_{i}\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}t^{-i}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\right)^{\nabla=0}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The question is local on
X
and hence we may assume that
X
is equipped with an étale map
X\rightarrow\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle
. Letting
X^{\mathrm{cycl}}_{\infty}
be the base change of
X
to
\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle
, we may further restrict to the localised site
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}/X^{\mathrm{cycl}}_{\infty}
. Then recall that
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\cong\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}[\![X_{1},\dots,X_{n}]\!]
, where
X_{i}=x_{i}-[x_{i}^{\flat}]
, and that the filtration on
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}=\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}[\tfrac{1}{t}]
is given by

\Fil^{i}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}=\sum_{j\geq-i}t^{-j}(t,X_{1},\dots,X_{n})^{i+j}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\;.

Thus, we have

\Fil^{0}(W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}})=\sum_{i}\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\left(\sum_{j\geq i}t^{-j}(t,X_{1},\dots,X_{n})^{j-i}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\right)

and our task is to show that a horizontal section in
\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(t,X_{1},\dots,X_{n})^{\ell}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
is divisible by
t^{\ell}
for all
\ell\geq 1
. Moreover, since the transition maps of the filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}W
are injections, it suffices to check the above for
W
in place of
\Fil^{i}W
.

Localising further on
X
if necessary, we may assume that
W
is trivial and let
e_{1},\dots,e_{m}
be a basis. Then the connection on
W
is given by

\nabla(e_{i})=\sum_{j,k}f_{ijk}e_{k}\mathrm{d}x_{j}

for some global sections
f_{ijk}
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Now letting

\sum_{i}e_{i}\otimes f_{i}\in W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(t,X_{1},\dots,X_{n})^{\ell}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}

be a horizontal section, we obtain the differential equations

(7.1.10)

\frac{\partial f_{k}}{\partial X_{j}}=-\sum_{i}f_{ijk}f_{i}

for the elements
f_{k}\in\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\cong\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}[\![X_{1},\dots,X_{n}]\!]
, where we are using that
\nabla(X_{j})=\mathrm{d}x_{j}
for the connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
.

Working modulo
t^{\ell}
from now on, our assumption implies that each
f_{k}
is contained in the ideal
(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})
. However, this means that the right-hand side of (
7.1.10
) has vanishing constant term, which in turn means that each
f_{k}
must be contained in
(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})^{2}
. Inductively, we see that, modulo
t^{\ell}
, each
f_{k}
is contained in all powers of the ideal
(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})
, which implies that
f_{k}
vanishes modulo
t^{\ell}
. In other words, each
f_{k}
is divisible by
t^{\ell}
and this is what we wanted to show.
∎

Finally, we can prove
7.1
.

Proof of
7.1
.

Note that
7.4
already establishes the claim in the situation where
X
admits an étale map
X\rightarrow\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle
whose postcomposition with the canonical map to
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
is Berkovich étale. As this can always be arranged locally on
X
, our task is to check that the equivalence from loc. cit. glues across different charts.

For this, take two étale charts
X\rightarrow\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle
and
X\rightarrow\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle\widetilde{x}_{1}^{\pm 1},\dots,\widetilde{x}_{1}^{\pm 1}\rangle
and define
X_{\infty,0}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
and
X_{0,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
by

X_{\infty,0}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\coloneqq X\times_{\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle}\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle\;,

and similarly for
X_{0,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
and the coordinates
\widetilde{x}_{i}
; moreover, put
X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\coloneqq X_{\infty,0}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\times_{X}X_{0,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
. Analogously, we define

X_{\infty,0}^{\mathrm{la}}\coloneqq X\times_{\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}}\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{x_{i}\mapsto x_{i}^{p}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}\;,

where the map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
is induced by the coordinates
x_{i}
, and similarly we define
X_{0,\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
in terms of the coordinates
\widetilde{x}_{i}
; again, we put
X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\coloneqq X_{\infty,0}^{\mathrm{la}}\times_{X}X_{0,\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
.

On the localised site
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}/X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
, the proof of
7.4
supplies isomorphisms

(7.1.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{0}(W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}})^{\nabla=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\sum_{i}\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X},x_{i}\mapsto[x_{i}^{\flat}]}t^{-i}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i}\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X},\widetilde{x}_{i}\mapsto[\widetilde{x}_{i}^{\flat}]}t^{-i}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where we emphasise that the base change from
\mathcal{O}_{X}
to
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
is along two different maps, one of them induced by
x_{i}\mapsto[x_{i}^{\flat}]
and the other induced by
\widetilde{x}_{i}\mapsto[\widetilde{x}_{i}^{\flat}]
, as the notation suggests. Our task is to check that the isomorphism

(7.1.12)

\sum_{i}\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X},x_{i}\mapsto[x_{i}^{\flat}]}t^{-i}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\xrightarrow{\cong}\sum\limits_{i}\Fil^{i}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X},\widetilde{x}_{i}\mapsto[\widetilde{x}_{i}^{\flat}]}t^{-i}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}

induced by changing charts makes the diagram (
7.1.11
) commute. To see where this last isomorphism comes from, note that, as in the proof of
7.4
, there is a unique map
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}
induced by
x_{i}\mapsto[x_{i}^{\flat}]
and then (
7.1.12
) is induced by the unique
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}})
-linear automorphism of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}
making the diagram

(7.1.13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{i}\mapsto[x_{i}^{\flat}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{x}_{i}\mapsto[\widetilde{x}_{i}^{\flat}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

commute. Indeed, such an automorphism exists uniquely: We have

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle)\cong\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}})\langle[x_{1}^{\flat}]^{1/p^{\infty}},\dots,[x_{n}^{\flat}]^{1/p^{\infty}}\rangle^{\wedge}_{\xi}

and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}
is a
(p,\xi)
-completed colimit of finite étale algebras over this ring as
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(-)
preserves finite étale maps, so there is a unique solution to the lifting problem

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Recalling from the proof of
7.4
that the left map in (
7.1.11
) is obtained by setting
X_{1}=x_{1}-[x_{1}^{\flat}],\dots,X_{n}=x_{n}-[x_{n}^{\flat}]
to zero while the right map sends
\widetilde{X}_{1}=\widetilde{x}_{1}-[\widetilde{x}_{1}^{\flat}],\dots,\widetilde{X}_{n}=\widetilde{x}_{n}-[\widetilde{x}_{n}^{\flat}]
to zero, we are reduced to checking that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty,\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{i}\mapsto[x_{i}^{\flat}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{x}_{i}\mapsto[\widetilde{x}_{i}^{\flat}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

commutes, where the horizontal map on the bottom is given by (
7.1.13
). However, this is immediate from the diagram (
7.1.13
) and this concludes the proof.
∎

7.2.
The de Rham comparison theorem

Having proved
7.1
, we know that the following definition is compatible with the terminology from
[
43
, §7]
in the case of vector bundles.

Definition 7.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For any perfect complex
E
on
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
, its
associated perfect complex of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-modules
is defined to be the image of
E
under the functor

(7.2.1)

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})\rightarrow\Perf(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})

defined analogously to (
7.1.4
).

Remark 7.7
.

Imitating the proof of
7.1
, one can show that, if
E
is a perfect complex on
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
corresponding to a filtered perfect complex with connection
(\Fil^{\bullet}W,\nabla)
on
X
, its associated perfect complex of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-modules
\mathbb{M}
satisfies

\Fil^{\bullet}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\cong\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\;.\qed

Remark 7.8
.

We warn the reader that, contrary to the functor

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}

on the abelian level, the functor (
7.2.1
) is
not
fully faithful.
∎

With this terminology in place, we can prove the following version of the relative de Rham comparison theorem, which the reader should compare with
[
43
, Thm. 8.8]
.

Theorem 7.9
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow S
be a smooth proper morphism of smooth partially proper rigid spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then the pushforward
f^{\mathrm{dR},+}_{*}\mathcal{O}\langle-i\rangle\in\mathcal{D}(S^{\mathrm{dR},+})
is perfect and its associated perfect complex of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-modules on
S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is given by
f_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
. In particular, for any smooth proper rigid space
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, there is an isomorphism

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}\cong R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}

compatible with the
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-actions and the filtrations.

Proof.

By
6.21
, the induced map
f^{\mathrm{Syn}}:X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is cohomologically smooth and weakly cohomologically proper and the pushforward
E\coloneqq f^{\mathrm{Syn}}_{*}\mathcal{O}\{i\}
is a perfect complex on
S^{\mathrm{Syn}}
.

By proper base change, we know that
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}^{*}E
identifies with
f^{\mathrm{dR},+}_{*}\mathcal{O}\langle-i\rangle
. Moreover, by
[
2
]
and using proper base change again, we also know that the pullback of
E|_{S{\Delta}}
along
Y_{S^{\diamondsuit}}\rightarrow S{\Delta}
identifies with the perfect complex on
Y_{S^{\diamondsuit}}
obtained from
f_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i)
by tensoring with
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{S^{\diamondsuit}}}
. In particular, we conclude that the functor
\Perf(S^{\mathrm{dR},+})\rightarrow\Perf(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})
sends
f^{\mathrm{dR},+}_{*}\mathcal{O}\langle-i\rangle
to
f_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
and this implies the claim. Finally, the last assertion follows by specialising to
S=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
using
2.40
.
∎

Remark 7.10
.

In
9.16
below, we will see that vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
identify with vector bundles on
\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
which are de Rham in a certain sense, see
9.14
. In particular, one concludes that there is a fully faithful embedding

\Loc^{\mathrm{dR}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\hookrightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})

of the category of de Rham local systems in the sense of
[
43
, Def. 8.3]
into vector bundle analytic
F
-gauges on
X
. Then the same proof as above shows that, as in
[
43
, Thm. 8.8]
, the conclusion of
7.9
stays valid if we replace the Tate twist
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i)
by any de Rham local system
\mathbb{L}
on
X
.
∎

8.
The comparison with filtered Hyodo–Kato cohomology

Recall that the main utility of syntomic cohomology classically is that it relates to both “de Rham” and “étale data” via comparison theorems such as the ones proved in
[
16
]
or
[
17
]
. In this spirit, we want to use the present section to show how perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and their cohomology can be understood through a “de Rham lens” for Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich spaces
X
; in particular, this includes smooth partially proper rigid spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and thus also relates to more classical settings of
p
-adic analytic geometry.

More specifically, we are going to show that perfect complexes on the syntomification
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
of a Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich space and their cohomology can be completely understood via perfect complexes on the Hyodo–Kato stack
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
and on the filtered de Rham stack
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
of
X
. Namely, we are going to construct a commutative diagram

(8.0.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{dR},+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{HK}}}

where the reader may mentally replace
X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}
by the usual Hyodo–Kato stack
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
for the moment, and show that it induces an equivalence of categories

(8.0.2)

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HK}})\times_{\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR}})}\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})

via pullback. We note that this result should be compared to the so-called Beilinson fibre square for syntomic cohomology of
p
-adic formal schemes in its stacky formulation from
[
29
, §3]
, where the occurrence of the Hyodo–Kato stack above is replaced by the algebraic syntomification of the special fibre.

In particular, the equivalence (
8.0.2
) includes the statement that the syntomic cohomology of
X
with coefficients in a perfect analytic
F
-gauge may be computed as a pullback of the Hyodo–Kato cohomology and the Hodge-filtered de Rham cohomology over the full de Rham cohomology of
X
, each with suitable coefficients obtained from the perfect analytic
F
-gauge we started with through various realisation functors, which we shall introduce. Taking coefficients in a Breuil–Kisin twist and comparing with
[
16
, Thm. 1.1]
, this in particular implies that our definition of
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))
for smooth partially proper qcqs rigid spaces
X
recovers the one of Colmez–Nizioł.

Finally, applying the above discussion to the case
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we will be able to identify vector bundles on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
with
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-equivariant vector bundles on
\mathrm{FF}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
which are de Rham in the sense of
[
22
, Def. 15.12]
. Moreover, in the case where the de Rham bundle arises from a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-representation
V
of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
by extending scalars, we will see that

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}},V)\cong H^{1}_{g}(\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},V)\;,

where the right-hand side was defined by Bloch–Kato in
[
9
]
as the subspace of
H^{1}(\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},V)
spanned by classes corresponding to extensions of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by
V
which are de Rham.

One should compare this to similar of result of Bhatt–Lurie about the algebraic syntomification of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
: In
[
8
, §§6.6, 6.7]
, they prove that
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattices in crystalline
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representations are equivalent to the category of so-called reflexive coherent sheaves on the algebraic syntomification of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and that this equivalence identifies
H^{1}
on the syntomification with the Bloch–Kato–Selmer group
H^{1}_{f}
after rationalisation, see
[
8
, Prop. 6.7.3]
.

8.1.
Accessing
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
via
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
and
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}

We begin by defining the stack
X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}
and the map
i_{\mathrm{HK}}:X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
occurring in the desired commutative diagram (
8.0.1
). This works for an arbitrary Gelfand stack
X
and we start by introducing the following auxiliary object: For any Gelfand stack
X
, the Gelfand stack
X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}
is defined as the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times[0,1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can}\times\{0\}}

We now obtain
X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}
from
X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}
by gluing. For this, note that the map

Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}\xrightarrow{\id\times\{0\}}Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times[0,1]

and the identity on
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}
glue to a map

(8.1.1)

\id\times\{0\}:Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}\coprod_{Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}}Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}\;,

the source of which is isomorphic to
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}
by
2.8
.

Definition 8.1
.

Let
X
be any Gelfand stack. The
mock Hyodo–Kato stack

X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}
of
X
is defined by the coequaliser diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\times\{0\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi\times\{1\}}

To obtain the map
i_{\mathrm{HK}}:X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, recall that

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{3/2},\infty)}\cong Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times[0,1]

via
\pi
by
4.9
and
3.19
. Moreover, recalling that
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=1}\cong X{\Delta}
via
\pi
, another application of
3.19
yields

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|t|=1,[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}\cong Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}

via
\pi
. Gluing these two isomorphisms along
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}
, we obtain a map

X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}

which is an isomorphism onto its image, see
Fig.

5
. Finally, this will descend to the desired map

i_{\mathrm{HK}}:X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\;,

which we will call the
Hyodo–Kato map
.

To get the desired diagram (
8.0.1
), recall that the map
\varphi\circ\iota:\widehat{X}\rightarrow Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
factors over the preimage of
\{p\}\subseteq(0,\infty)
under the radius map and hence induces a map
X^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}
, which in turn yields a composite map

(8.1.2)

X^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}\;.

Observing that the postcomposition of this map with
i_{\mathrm{HK}}
identifies with the precomposition of
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}
with the canonical map
X^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
, we obtain the desired commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{dR},+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{HK}}}

For this to be of any use to us in the sequel, we of course need to relate
X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}
and, in particular, the category of perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}
, to the actual Hyodo–Kato stack
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
and perfect complexes thereon.

Figure 5.
A schematic picture of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
with the image of
X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}

outlined in bold (interior shaded in grey)

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
X
be any Gelfand stack. Then there is an equivalence of categories

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HK}})

induced by the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HK}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\times\{0\}}

Proof.

By
2.7
, we have

\Perf(Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times[0,1])\cong\Perf(Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}})

and hence the definition of
X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}
shows that pullback along the map

\id\times\{0\}:Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}\coprod_{Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}}Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}

from (
8.1.1
) induces an equivalence on perfect complexes. As the pushout on the left is isomorphic to
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}
by
2.8
, we conclude that the categories of perfect complexes of
X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}
and

to
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
along
\kappa
using universality of colimits in
\infty
-topoi.
∎

Using
8.2
, the diagram (
8.0.1
) now induces various realisation functors for perfect analytic
F
-gauges. Firstly, pullback along the map
i_{\mathrm{HK}}
induces a realisation functor

T_{\mathrm{HK}}:\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HK}})

from perfect analytic
F
-gauges to perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
, which should be thought of as coefficients for Hyodo–Kato cohomology of
X
following
[
1
, Rem. 6.1.3]
. Secondly, via pullback along the filtered de Rham map
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}
, we obtain a realisation functor

T_{\mathrm{dR},+}:\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})

from analytic
F
-gauges to quasicoherent sheaves on
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
, i.e. a category of filtered analytic
D
-modules on
X
, and, lastly, postcomposing
T_{\mathrm{dR},+}
with pullback along the canonical map
X^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
yields a realisation functor

T_{\mathrm{dR}}:\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{dR}})\;.

Note that pullback along the map
X^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}
from (
8.1.2
) also induces a functor
\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HK}})\rightarrow\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR}})
whose precomposition with
T_{\mathrm{HK}}
identifies with
T_{\mathrm{dR}}
by commutativity of the diagram (
8.0.1
). Using this terminology, we can formulate the main result of this section as follows:

Theorem 8.3
.

Let
X
be a Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then there is an equivalence of categories

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HK}})\times_{\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR}})}\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})

induced by the realisation functors
T_{\mathrm{HK}}
and
T_{\mathrm{dR},+}
. In particular, for any
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
, there is a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HK}},T_{\mathrm{HK}}(E))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+},T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR}},T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E))\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Using the expected relation between the stack
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
and Hyodo–Kato cohomology of
X
, comparing the result above with
[
16
, Thm. 1.1]
in particular shows that our definition of syntomic cohomology recovers the more classical definition of syntomic cohomology of Colmez–Nizioł for smooth partially proper qcqs rigid spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, see e.g.
[
16
, §4.4]
. Indeed, applying the above result to
E=\mathcal{O}\{i\}
and using
2.40
, we obtain:

Corollary 8.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper qcqs rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is a cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(X)^{\varphi=p^{i},N=0,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{\mathrm{Hod}}^{i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X)\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

where we note that the
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-invariants in the top right corner should be interpreted as smooth group cohomology and are hence
underived
.

Let us now move on to the proof of
8.3
. For this, we first record the following lemma, which says that, if we “chop up”
X^{\mathrm{N}}
along the radius map, gluing the pieces back together recovers
X^{\mathrm{N}}
.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
X
be any Gelfand stack. The canonical inclusion maps induce an isomorphism between the iterated pushout

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}}X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{-1/2}]}}X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-3/2},p^{-1/2}]}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-3/2},p^{-3/2}]}}\dots

and the stack
X^{\mathrm{N}}
.

Proof.

This follows by repeatedly applying
2.8
.
∎

X^{\mathrm{dR}}
\varphi(X^{\mathrm{dR}})
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
\varphi(X^{\mathrm{dR}})
\varphi^{2}(X^{\mathrm{dR}})

A schematic picture of
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}
with

the two copies of
\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}
in bold

To move on, we introduce the following notation: For any Gelfand stack
X
, let
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}
be defined as the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times[0,\infty)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\times\{0\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{HT}}}

see
Fig.

6
. Then
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}
receives two maps from the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times[0,\infty)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\times\{0\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can}}

Namely, one of them is induced by
j_{\mathrm{HT}}:X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}
and the canonical inclusion
X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\rightarrow X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}
and we denote this map by

j_{\mathrm{HT}}:\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}

as well. The other one is induced by
j_{\mathrm{dR}}:X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}
and the isomorphism

(8.1.3)

X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times[0,\infty)\xrightarrow{\cong}(X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times[0,1])\coprod_{X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{-1/2}]}}(X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{-1/2}]}\times[0,\infty))\;,

where the maps defining the pushout on the right-hand side are
\varphi\times\{1\}
“to the left” and
\id\times\{0\}
“to the right”, respectively, and we use the isomorphism

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\cong X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times[0,1]

from
4.9
to view the target of (
8.1.3
) as a closed substack of
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}
. We denote this second map by

j_{\mathrm{dR}}:\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}\;.

In this notation, our next lemma reads as follows:

\varphi^{-2}(X^{\mathrm{HT}})
\varphi^{-1}(X^{\mathrm{HT}})
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
\varphi(X^{\mathrm{dR}})
\varphi^{-1}(X^{\mathrm{HT}})
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
\varphi(X^{\mathrm{dR}})
\varphi^{2}(X^{\mathrm{dR}})

Figure 7.
Proof of
8.6
: chop up
X^{\mathrm{N}}
, then glue along

the yellow, orange and red pieces to get
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
X
be any Gelfand stack. Then
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is isomorphic to the coequaliser of the two maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{HT}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}}

Proof.

See also
Fig.

7
. By definition,
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is obtained as the coequaliser of the maps
j_{\mathrm{HT}},j_{\mathrm{dR}}:X{\Delta}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}
. However, by
8.5
, we can view
X^{\mathrm{N}}
and
X{\Delta}
as glued together from the smaller pieces

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)},X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]},\dots\;,\hskip 8.5359pt\text{and}\hskip 8.5359ptX{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)},X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]},\dots\;,

respectively. To obtain
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
from
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)},X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]},\dots
, we have to perform the following gluings:

(1)

For each
k\geq-1
odd, glue the two copies of
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-k/2},p^{-k/2}]}
inside
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-k/2-1},p^{-k/2}]}
and
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-k/2},p^{-k/2+1}]}
(or
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}
if
k=-1
), respectively;

(2)

for each
k\geq 1
odd, glue the two copies of
X{\Delta}_{[p^{-k/2-1},p^{-k/2}]}
embedded into
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-k/2},p^{-k/2+1}]}
and
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-k/2-1},p^{-k/2}]}
via
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
and
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
, respectively;

(3)

glue the two copies of
X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}
which are embedded into
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}
and
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}
via
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
and
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
, respectively;

(4)

glue the two copies of
X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}
which are embedded into
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}
via
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
and
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
, respectively.

We now change the order of these gluings: For this, first note that

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-k/2},p^{-k/2+1}]}\cong X{\Delta}_{[p^{-k/2},p^{-k/2+1}]}\times[0,1]\cong X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times[0,1]

for each
k\geq 1
odd, where the first isomorphism is due to
4.9
and the second one is via a
(k-1)/2
-fold application of
\varphi
, which we recall defines an isomorphism
X{\Delta}_{(0,1)}\cong X{\Delta}_{(0,p)}
by
3.28
. Thus, first performing the gluings (2) and (3), we obtain
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}
as defined above.

Observing that

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{-k/2},p^{-k/2}]}\cong X{\Delta}_{[p^{-k/2},p^{-k/2}]}\times[0,1]\cong X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times[0,1]

by another application of
4.9
and
3.28
, we now see that subsequently performing the gluings (1) and (4) equivalently amounts to gluing the two copies of
\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}
embedded into
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}
via
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
and
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
. This proves the claim.
∎

.

For any Gelfand stack
X
, pullback along the morphism

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.23877pt}{-9.3833pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope

{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hskip-4.26773pt)\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}

induces an equivalence on perfect complexes.

Proof.

This immediately follows from the previous lemma once we know that

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)})\hskip 8.5359pt\text{and}\hskip 8.5359pt\Perf(\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}})\cong\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)})

via pullback along the natural maps
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}
and
X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}\rightarrow\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}
. However, by definition of
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{HK}}
, the first equivalence follows from the fact that pullback along
\id\times\{0\}
induces an equivalence

\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times[0,\infty))\cong\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]})\;,

which is a consequence of
2.7
. Similarly, the second one follows from the fact that pullback along
\id\times\{0\}
induces an equivalence

\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times[0,\infty))\cong\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]})\;,

which is proved by the same argument.
∎

We now come to the key lemma. This uses the spreading out results for perfect complexes we have proved in §
2.3
as well as the nice coverability results from §§
4.5
,
5.3
. Note that these were already key to the proof of
5.18
, which we will make use of as well.

Lemma 8.8
.

Let
X
be a Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then pullback along the map

X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{dR}}}X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}\;,

which is induced by
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}
, induces an equivalence on perfect complexes.

Proof.

First note that we may replace the interval
[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]
by an interval
[r,s]\subseteq(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})
with
r-p^{1/2}
and
p^{3/2}-s
sufficiently small. Indeed, by
2.8
, we have

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},r]}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[r,r]}}X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[r,s]}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[s,s]}}X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[s,p^{3/2}]}

and the same is true for
(-){\Delta}
in place of
(-)^{\mathrm{N}}
. Then recalling from
4.9
that

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[r^{\prime},s^{\prime}]}\cong X{\Delta}_{[r^{\prime},s^{\prime}]}\times[0,1]

for
[r^{\prime},s^{\prime}]
being any of the intervals
[p^{1/2},r],[r,r],[s,s]
or
[s,p^{3/2}]
, we obtain

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[r^{\prime},s^{\prime}]})\cong\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[r^{\prime},s^{\prime}]})

via pullback along
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
from
2.6
, which yields the desired reduction.

Next, note that, by compatibility of
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{N}}
with rational localisations, see
4.22
, we may assume that
X
admits a finite étale map
X\rightarrow Z
to some rational localisation
Z
of
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
. Then
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[r,s]}
is nicely coverable by
5.11
and our task is to show that the diagram

(8.1.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf(X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[r,s]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[r,s]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{dR},+}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{dR}}^{*}}

is cartesian.

To see this, consider the overconvergent normed divisor
Z\coloneqq\{|ut|=0\}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[r,s]}
and note that, for each
\varepsilon>0
small enough, we have
Z_{\varepsilon}\setminus Z\cong(X{\Delta}_{|\widetilde{\mu}|\leq\varepsilon}\setminus X^{\mathrm{dR}})\times[0,1]
by
4.9
, whence
2.6
yields

\Perf(Z_{\varepsilon}\setminus Z)\cong\Perf(X{\Delta}_{|\widetilde{\mu}|\leq\varepsilon}\setminus X^{\mathrm{dR}})\;.

Similarly, we obtain

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[r,s]}\setminus Z)\cong\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[r,s]}\setminus X^{\mathrm{dR}})\;.

Finally, from
5.18
, we know that

\Perf(Z)\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\;.

Applying
2.19
to the overconvergent normed divisor
Z
, the results of the previous paragraph yield a cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf(X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[r,s]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[r,s]}\setminus X^{\mathrm{dR}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\varepsilon>0}\Perf(X{\Delta}_{|\widetilde{\mu}|\leq\varepsilon}\setminus X^{\mathrm{dR}})\;.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{dR},+}^{*}}

Putting this together with the cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[r,s]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[r,s]}\setminus X^{\mathrm{dR}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\varepsilon>0}\Perf(X{\Delta}_{|\widetilde{\mu}|\leq\varepsilon}\setminus X^{\mathrm{dR}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{dR}}^{*}}

obtained by applying
2.19
to the overconvergent normed divisor
X^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong(\{|\widetilde{\mu}|=0\}\subseteq X{\Delta}_{[r,s]})
, where the isomorphism is via
i_{\mathrm{dR}}
, then finally yields the desired cartesian diagram (
8.1.4
). We point out that
X{\Delta}_{[r,s]}
is indeed nicely coverable: This is deduced from nice coverability of
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[r,s]}
by base change using
2.12
and
2.14
.
∎

Finally, we can put everything together and prove
8.3
.

Proof of
8.3
.

First recall from
4.9
that
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}\cong X{\Delta}_{[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}\times[0,1]
, which yields

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]})\cong\Perf(X{\Delta}_{[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]})

via pullback along
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
by
2.6
. Using
2.8
, we hence deduce from
8.8
that pullback along the map

(8.1.5)

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{3/2},\infty)}\coprod_{X{\Delta}_{[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}}X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{dR}}}X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}

induces an equivalence on perfect complexes, where the map “to the left” in the leftmost pushout is induced by
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
. Recalling that
X{\Delta}_{[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}\cong Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}^{\mathrm{dR}}
and

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{3/2},\infty)}\cong Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{3/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}\times[0,1]\;,

which follow from
3.23
and
4.9
, we recognise the source of (
8.1.5
) as the pushout of
X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}
along
X^{\mathrm{dR}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
. Furthermore observing that
X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},\infty)}\cong Y_{X^{\diamondsuit},[p^{1/2},\infty)}^{\mathrm{dR}}
, which is due to
3.23
as well, we deduce from (
8.1.5
) and
8.7
that pullback along

\operatorname{coeq}(\hskip-4.26773pt\hbox to171.02pt{\vbox to35.47pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 85.50827pt\lower-16.94302pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
