induces an equivalence on perfect complexes as well, where we note that we have switched the order of the colimits on the left-hand side. However, by definition of
X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}
, the source of this last map is exactly

X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{dR}}}X^{\mathrm{dR},+}

and this concludes the proof.
∎

8.2.
Bloch–Kato’s
H^{1}_{g}
via the syntomification

We now specialise to the case
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and use
8.3
to explicitly describe vector bundles on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and their cohomology in terms of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-equivariant vector bundles on the Fargues–Fontaine curve.

Theorem 8.9
.

There is an equivalence of categories

\Vect(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}})\cong\Vect^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathrm{FF}_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}})

between vector bundles on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and vector bundles on the Fargues–Fontaine curve of
\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}
which are de Rham in the sense of
[
22
, Def. 15.12]
. Moreover, if
V
and
W
are de Rham representations of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, then

\RHom_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}}(V,W)\cong\RHom_{\Rep^{\mathrm{dR}}(\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})}(V,W)\;.

In particular, we have

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}},V)\cong H^{1}_{g}(\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},V)\;.

Proof.

By
[
1
, Thm. 7.1.1]
, the category of vector bundles of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
is equivalent to the category of
(\varphi,N,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-modules over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}
. Using that vector bundles on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
are equivalent to finitely filtered
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector spaces by
2.23
, we conclude from
8.3
that vector bundles on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
are equivalent to filtered
(\varphi,N,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-modules over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}
; indeed, note that the proof of loc. cit. works just as well if we replace
\Perf(-)
by
\Perf^{[a,b]}(-)
for all
a\leq b
. Recalling that filtered
(\varphi,N,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-modules over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}
are equivalent to potentially semistable vector bundles on
\mathrm{FF}_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
by
[
22
, Prop. 10.6.7]
, the first part of the theorem now follows by the theorem that “de Rham
=
potentially semistable”, see
[
22
, Thm. 10.6.10]
.

For the second part about Ext groups, we can reduce to the case
V=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by replacing
W
by
W\otimes V^{\vee}
and then
8.3
tells us that we have to check whether the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}},W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}},T_{\mathrm{HK}}(W))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+},T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(W))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR}},T_{\mathrm{dR}}(W))}

computes the Exts between
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
W
in de Rham representations. Recalling that cohomology on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
computes cohomology of
(\varphi,N,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-modules by
[
1
, Thm. 7.1.1]
and using the theorem “de Rham
=
potentially semistable”, the claim follows by Galois descent from a computation of Emerton–Kisin of the Ext groups of semistable representations in terms of filtered
(\varphi,N)
-modules, see
[
20
, Cor. 2.4.4]
.
∎

9.
The comparison with proétale cohomology

We now play the game from the previous section in the other direction: Instead of approaching the syntomification of Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich spaces from the de Rham side, we will now exploit the relation between
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and “étale data” of
X
. In fact, this will work using almost the same arguments as in the previous section, but “in the other direction”, i.e. we have to swap the roles of
u
and
t
and reverse the direction on the
\kappa
-axis.

Namely, we are going to prove that, for a Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich space
X
, perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and their cohomology can be understood via perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and on the stack
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
that we have introduced previously. This will again take the form of a commutative diagram

(9.0.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}}

where
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}
should again be thought of as
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
for all intents and purposes, for which we are going to show that it induces an equivalence of categories

(9.0.2)

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}})\times_{\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})}\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\;.

Let us point out that, given the fact that the corresponding result (
8.0.2
) in the “de Rham case” has an analogue for
p
-adic formal schemes in terms of the algebraic syntomification of Drinfeld and Bhatt–Lurie, it seems to be an interesting question to investigate to which extent the naive analogue of (
9.0.2
) holds for the algebraic syntomification of smooth
p
-adic formal schemes.

As the equivalence (
9.0.2
) again entails a fibre product formula expressing syntomic cohomology of
X
with coefficients in a perfect analytic
F
-gauge in terms of cohomology on
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}},X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
and
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
, we will be able to recover the comparison between syntomic cohomology and proétale cohomology of a smooth partially proper rigid space
X
in a suitable range from
[
16
, Thm. 1.1]
. Indeed, as cohomology on
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
agrees with proétale cohomology by
[
2
]
, this comes down to controlling the difference between cohomology on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
and
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
, which is precisely responsible for the necessary truncation in the comparison theorem.

Finally, we will make use of the equivalence (
9.0.2
) above to explicitly describe vector bundles on the syntomification of any smooth partially proper rigid space
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Namely, we are going to prove that there is an equivalence

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\cong\Vect^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})

between vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and vector bundles on
\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
which are de Rham in a certain sense to be defined below, see
9.14
. For this, we are going to show that the pullback functor
\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})
is fully faithful. To determine its essential image and make the de Rham condition appear, we are going to use the main result of §
7
: Namely, the functor

\begin{split}\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})&\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})\\
&\rightarrow\Vect((\widehat{X}\subseteq Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}})^{\wedge})\cong\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on }X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\}\end{split}

obtained from
5.18
agrees with Scholze’s functor

\{\text{filtered vector bundles on $X$ with connection}\}\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on }X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\}

from
[
43
, §7]
and hence the functor
\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})
automatically factors through a certain full subcategory of “generically flat” vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
to be defined below, see
9.21
. The hardest part will then be to show that this factorisation is already essentially surjective and this will involve an explicit computation using differential operators, which we carry out in
9.23
.

9.1.
Accessing
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
via
X{\Delta}
and
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}

We start by defining the stack
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}
and constructing the commutative diagram (
9.0.1
). To this end, we introduce the following object: For any Gelfand stack
X
, the Gelfand stack
X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}
is defined as the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{-1/2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{(0,p^{1/2}]}\times[0,1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi\times\{1\}}

From this, we again obtain
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}
via gluing. Namely, note that the map

X{\Delta}_{(0,p^{-1/2}]}\xrightarrow{\varphi\times\{1\}}X{\Delta}_{(0,p^{1/2}]}\times[0,1]

and the identity on
X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}
glue to a map

\varphi\times\{1\}:X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}\coprod_{X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{-1/2}]}}X{\Delta}_{(0,p^{-1/2}]}\rightarrow X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}\;,

the source of which is isomorphic to
X{\Delta}_{(0,p^{1/2}]}
by
2.8
.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
X
be any Gelfand stack. The Gelfand stack
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}
is defined by the coequaliser diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{(0,p^{1/2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\times\{0\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi\times\{1\}}

To obtain the map
i_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}:X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, recall that

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{(0,p^{1/2}]}\cong X{\Delta}_{(0,p^{1/2}]}\times[0,1]

via
\pi
by
4.9
. Moreover, recalling that
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=1}\cong X{\Delta}
from
4.8
, we also obtain an isomorphism

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=1,[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}\cong X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}\;.

As the isomorphism
X^{\mathrm{N}}_{|u|=1}\cong X{\Delta}
intertwines
\pi
with the Frobenius on
X{\Delta}
, the two displayed isomorphisms above glue to give a map

X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}

which is an isomorphism onto its image, see
Fig.

8
. Finally, this descends to the desired map

i_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}:X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\;.

In order to get to the desired diagram (
9.0.1
) from here, note that
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
factors through
X{\Delta}_{[1,1]}
and hence we obtain a composite map

(9.1.1)

X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\rightarrow X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}\rightarrow X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}\;.

Observing that the postcomposition of this map with
i_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
identifies with the precomposition of the natural map

i_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}:X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}

with the canonical map
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
, we obtain the desired commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}}

\varphi^{-2}(X^{\mathrm{HT}})
\varphi^{-1}(X^{\mathrm{HT}})
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
\varphi^{-1}(X^{\mathrm{HT}})
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
\varphi(X^{\mathrm{dR}})
0
1/p
1
p
p^{2}
\kappa
\varphi

Figure 8.
A schematic picture of
X^{\mathrm{N}}
with the image of
X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}},\mathrm{pre}}

outlined in bold (interior shaded in grey)

As before with the stack
X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}}
, for the introduction of
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}
to have any point at all, we need to relate its category of perfect complexes to perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
.

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
X
be any Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. There is an equivalence of categories

\Perf(X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}})

induced by the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{(0,p^{1/2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi\times\{1\}}

Proof.

As in
8.2
.
∎

Using
9.2
, the diagram (
9.0.1
) induces various realisation functors for perfect analytic
F
-gauges. Firstly, pullback along the map
i_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
induces a realisation functor

T_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}:\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}})

from perfect analytic
F
-gauges to perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
, which should be thought of as coefficients for proétale cohomology of
X
following
[
2
]
. Secondly, via pullback along the map
i_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
from above, we obtain a realisation functor

T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}:\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})

from analytic
F
-gauges to quasicoherent sheaves on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
and, lastly, postcomposing
T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
with pullback along the canonical map
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
yields a realisation functor

T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}:\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})\;.

Note that pullback along the map
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}
from (
9.1.1
) induces a functor
\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}})\rightarrow\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})
whose precomposition with
T_{\mathrm{HK}}
identifies with
T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
by commutativity of the diagram (
9.0.1
). This enables us to formulate the “étale” analogue of
8.3
:

Theorem 9.3
.

Let
X
be a Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then there is an equivalence of categories

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}})\times_{\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})}\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})

induced by the realisation functors
T_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and
T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
. In particular, for any
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
, there is a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}},T_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}(E))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+},T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}(E))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger},T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}(E))\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Using that cohomology on
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
coincides with proétale cohomology for smooth partially proper rigid spaces
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by
[
2
]
, the result above lets us recover the classical comparison theorem between syntomic cohomology and proétale cohomology in a certain range, see e.g.
[
16
, Thm. 1.1.(4)]
.

Theorem 9.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. If
E\in\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
is a vector bundle analytic
F
-gauge with Hodge–Tate weights all at most
-i
for some
i\geq 0
, then the natural morphism

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},E)\rightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}},T_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}(E))

is an isomorphism on
\tau^{\leq i}
and induces an injection on
H^{i+1}
. In particular, for
E=\mathcal{O}\{i\}
, we obtain

\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))\cong\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))\;.

Proof.

By
9.3
, our task is to prove that the cofibre of the morphism

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+},T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}(E))\rightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger},T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}(E))

is concentrated in degrees at least
i+1
. By compatibility of the functors
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
and
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
with open localisation, which one deduces from
4.22
, we are reduced to the case where
X
admits a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
. However, in this case,
5.17
gives precise double complexes computing the cohomologies in question. Indeed, inspecting these double complexes, we see that our claim follows once we show that the ascending filtration
\Fil_{\bullet}V
associated to the restriction of
E
to
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
via
5.15
stabilises from
\Fil_{-i}V
onwards, i.e.

\Fil_{-i}V=\Fil_{-i+1}V=\dots\;.

To see this, recall from
5.15
that the (descendingly) filtered vector bundle with connection
(\Fil^{\bullet}W,\nabla)
on
X
corresponding to the restriction of
E
to
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is obtained by Tate twisting and then descending the associated graded pieces of
\Fil_{\bullet}V
to
X
, i.e.
\gr_{k}V
descends to
\Fil^{k}W
up to a Tate twist, and hence we equivalently have to show that

\dots=\Fil^{-i+2}W=\Fil^{-i+1}W=0\;.

However, our assumption on the Hodge–Tate weights precisely means that
\gr^{k}W=0
for
k\geq-i+1
and now we are done by noting that the decreasing filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}W
is separated, which follows e.g. from
5.15
.
∎

Remark 9.5
.

Note that
9.3
in some sense improves upon the “classical” comparison from
9.4
by also describing the failure of the map
R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}},\mathcal{O}\{i\})\rightarrow R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))
to be an isomorphism in higher degrees: Namely, this is exactly measured by the failure of
R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+},\mathcal{O}\{i\})\rightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger},\mathcal{O}\{i\})
to be an isomorphism.
∎

Of course, combining
9.4
with
8.3
, we immediately obtain the following extension of Colmez–Nizioł’s “basic comparison theorem” from
[
16
, Thm. 1.3]
to vector bundle
F
-gauge coefficients:

Corollary 9.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper qcqs rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. If
E\in\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})
is a vector bundle analytic
F
-gauge with Hodge–Tate weights all at most
-i
for some
i\geq 0
, then there is a natural map

\fib(R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HK}},T_{\mathrm{HK}}(E))\rightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR}},T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E))/R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+},T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E)))\rightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}},T_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}(E))

which induces an isomorphism on
\tau^{\leq i}
and an injection on
H^{i+1}
. In particular, for
E=\mathcal{O}\{i\}
, we obtain

\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))\cong\tau^{\leq i}\fib(R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(X)^{\varphi=p^{i},N=0,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\rightarrow R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X)/\Fil^{i}_{\mathrm{Hod}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X))\;.

Let us now say something about the proof of
9.3
. As everything is entirely analogous to what happens in the proof of
8.3
, we just explain the steps of the proof in order to avoid too much redundancy. We first note the following analogue of
8.5
:

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
X
be any Gelfand stack. The canonical inclusion maps induce an isomorphism between the iterated pushout

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{(0,p^{3/2}]}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}}X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{3/2},p^{5/2}]}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{5/2},p^{5/2}]}}X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{5/2},p^{7/2}]}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{7/2},p^{7/2}]}}\dots

and the stack
X^{\mathrm{N}}
.

Proof.

Repeatedly apply
2.8
.
∎

X^{\mathrm{HT}}
\varphi^{-1}(X^{\mathrm{HT}})
\varphi^{-2}(X^{\mathrm{HT}})
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
\varphi^{-1}(X^{\mathrm{HT}})

Figure 9.
A schematic picture of
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
with

the two copies of
\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
in bold

For any Gelfand stack
X
, we now define the stack
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
as the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}\times(-\infty,0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}_{(0,p^{3/2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\times\{0\}}

see
Fig.

9
. Then
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
receives two maps from the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times(-\infty,0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}_{(0,p^{1/2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\times\{0\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can}}

Namely, one of them is induced by
j_{\mathrm{dR}}:X{\Delta}_{(0,p^{1/2}]}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{(0,p^{3/2}]}
and the canonical inclusion
X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\rightarrow X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}
and we denote this map by

j_{\mathrm{dR}}:\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}

as well. The other one is induced by
j_{\mathrm{HT}}:X{\Delta}_{(0,p^{1/2}]}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{(0,p^{3/2}]}
and the isomorphism

(9.1.2)

X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times(-\infty,0]\xrightarrow{\cong}(X{\Delta}_{[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}\times[0,1])\coprod_{X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}}(X{\Delta}_{[p^{1/2},p^{1/2}]}\times(-\infty,0])\;,

where the maps defining the pushout on the right-hand side are
\varphi\times\{0\}
“to the left” and
\id\times\{0\}
“to the right”, respectively, and we use the isomorphism

X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}\cong X{\Delta}_{[p^{3/2},p^{3/2}]}\times[0,1]

from
4.9
to view the target of (
9.1.2
) as a closed substack of
X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
. We denote this second map by

j_{\mathrm{HT}}:\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\;.

Now the next lemma reads as follows:

Lemma 9.8
.

Let
X
be any Gelfand stack. Then
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is isomorphic to the coequaliser of the two maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{pre},\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{HT}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}}

Proof.

Analogous to
8.6
replacing the use of
8.5
by
9.7
.
∎

.
∎

The following lemma is again the key to proving
9.3
. The proof is analogous to the one of
8.8
, but uses the part

\Perf((X^{\mathrm{N}})_{|ut|=0})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})

of the statement of
5.18
instead of the equivalence between perfect complexes on
(X^{\mathrm{N}})_{|ut|=0}
and
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
.

Lemma 9.10
.

Let
X
be a Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then pullback along the map

X{\Delta}_{[p^{-1/2},p^{1/2}]}\coprod_{X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}}X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}_{[p^{1/2},p^{3/2}]}\;,

which is induced by
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
and
i_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
, induces an equivalence on perfect complexes.

Proof.

Analogous to
8.8
with the modification mentioned above.
∎

Finally, one puts together
9.9
and
9.10
to obtain
9.3
, analogously to what happens in the proof of
8.3
.

9.2.
De Rham bundles on
\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
via the syntomification

We now want to use
9.3
to explicitly describe vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
for a smooth partially proper rigid space
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For this, recall the functor

\begin{split}\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})&\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})\\
&\rightarrow\Vect((\widehat{X}\subseteq Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}})^{\wedge})\cong\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on }X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\}\end{split}

obtained from
5.18
, which we have shown to agree with Scholze’s functor

(9.2.1)

\{\text{filtered vector bundles with connection on $X$}\}\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}

from
[
43
, §7]
in
7.1
. Also recall from
[
43
, Thm. 7.7]
that (
9.2.1
) is fully faithful and that the image of a given filtered vector bundle with connection on
X
under this functor is called its
associated
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system
. We can thus make the following definition:

Definition 9.11
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. A
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is called
generically flat
if it lies in the essential image of the functor (
9.2.1
), i.e. if it is associated to a filtered vector bundle with connection on
X
.

Remark 9.12
.

Note that the above definition recovers the notion of a generically flat
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-representation of
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
of
[
22
, Def. 10.4.1]
in the case
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
∎

Remark 9.13
.

Generic flatness of a
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system may be checked locally on
X
. Indeed, this is because (
9.2.1
) is fully faithful and filtered vector bundles with connection satisfy descent for open covers.
∎

Now recall from (
7.1.2
) that there is an equivalence

\Vect((\widehat{X}\subseteq\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})^{\wedge})\cong\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}\;.

Thus, pullback along the map
(\widehat{X}\subseteq\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})^{\wedge}\rightarrow\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
yields a
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system associated to any vector bundle on
\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
.

Definition 9.14
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. A vector bundle on
\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
is called
de Rham
if its associated
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system is generically flat. We let
\Vect^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})
be the full subcategory of vector bundles on
\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}
which are de Rham.

Remark 9.15
.

Using
9.12
, we see that the definition above recovers the notion of being de Rham for a
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-equivariant vector bundle on the Fargues–Fontaine curve from
[
22
, Def. 10.4.5]
in the case
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
∎

With the above notation in place, we can now give the desired explicit description of the category of vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
.

Theorem 9.16
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then there is an equivalence of categories

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}})\cong\Vect^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})\;.

Before we move on to the proof of the theorem above, let us make some remarks about the relation between
9.16
and the results from §
8
.

Remark 9.17
.

Contrary to the situation for
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
from
8.9
, cohomology on
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
does
not
compute the
\RHom
in the category of de Rham local systems on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
in general. This can already be seen in the case
X=\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Then
8.4
yields a cartesian diagram

(9.2.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{\varphi=p,N=0,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{1}_{\mathrm{Hod}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{P}^{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{P}^{1})}

and we know that
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{P}^{1})\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{p}[-2]
while
\Fil^{1}_{\mathrm{Hod}}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{P}^{1})\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}[-2]
. Meanwhile, by
[
1
, Lem. 6.2.9]
, we have
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(\mathbb{P}^{1})\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}[-2]
with the monodromy being trivial,
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
acting in the canonical way and Frobenius fixing the basis element of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}
while multiplying the basis element of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}[-2]
by
p
. Thus, we have

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{\varphi=p,N=0,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{p}[-1]\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{p}[-2]^{\oplus 2}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{p}[-3]

and therefore (
9.2.2
) yields

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(1))\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}[-1]^{\oplus 2}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{p}[-2]^{\oplus 2}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{p}[-3]\;.

However, note that proétale de Rham local systems on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
are just equivalent to de Rham
\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representations and hence the
\RHom
s in this category have vanishing
H^{i}
for
i>2
by the proof of
8.9
. In fact, using loc. cit., one easily calculates

\RHom_{\Rep^{\mathrm{dR}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(1))\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}[-1]^{\oplus 2}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{p}[-2]\;.\qed

Remark 9.18
.

Note that the case
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
of
9.16
is already covered by
8.9
, but the proof we will give for
9.16
is diametrically opposite to the one we have given for
8.9
: Indeed, here we will make use of the connection between
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
, i.e. the “étale aspect” of
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, while the proof of loc. cit. exploits the connection between
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and
X^{\mathrm{HK}}
, i.e. the “de Rham aspect” of
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
.
∎

Remark 9.19
.

Another view on the two proofs we have given for the above result in the case
X=\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is the following: While the proof via
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
we will give below directly shows that vector bundles on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
are de Rham bundles on
\mathrm{FF}_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, the proof of
8.9
rather shows that vector bundles on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
are the same as filtered
(\varphi,N,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-modules, i.e. potentially semistable vector bundles on
\mathrm{FF}_{\Spd\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. In other words, putting these two proofs together gives a new proof of the theorem that “de Rham
=
potentially semistable” of Berger. Note, however, that we need the equivalence between vector bundles on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
and
(\varphi,N,\Gal_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
-modules from
[
1
, Thm. 7.1.1]
as an input for the proof of
8.9
; this should be seen as analogous to Berger’s reduction of the
p
-adic monodromy theorem to Crew’s conjecture about
p
-adic differential equations.
∎

Developing the previous remarks further, we might want to compare the results of
9.16
and
8.3
more generally for smooth partially proper rigid spaces
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. This immediately yields the following result, which was already sketched in
[
1
, Rem. 7.5.7]
and, as explained in loc. cit., in particular also implies Shimizu’s theorem about de Rham local systems from
[
49
, Thm. 1.1]
:

Corollary 9.20
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then there is an equivalence of categories

\Vect^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HK}})\times_{\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR}})}\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\;.

Let us now come back to proving
9.16
. For this, recall from
3.19
that there is a natural map
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
for any Gelfand stack
X
and, similarly, one proves that there is a natural map
\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
. In our current situation, i.e. for
X
a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, furthermore recall from
[
2
]
that pullback along this map induces an equivalence

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}})\cong\Vect(\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})\;.

Thus, in view of
9.3
, the main part of the proof of
9.16
is to understand the pullback functor
\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})
.

More precisely, we see that our task is to prove that this functor is fully faithful and that its essential image is exactly cut out by the de Rham condition in
9.3
. More to the point, recall from (
7.1.3
) that there is a natural pullback functor

(9.2.3)

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})\rightarrow\Vect((\widehat{X}\subseteq\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}})^{\wedge})\cong\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}\;,

which associates a
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
to any vector bundle on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
. This leads to the following definition:

Definition 9.21
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. A vector bundle on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
is called
generically flat
if its associated
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is generically flat.

With this terminology in place, the above discussion shows that the proof of
9.16
reduces to the following statement:

Proposition 9.22
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then the pullback functor

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})

is fully faithful and its essential image is given by the full subcategory of vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
which are generically flat.

From
7.1
, we can already conclude that the pullback functor
\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})
factors through the full subcategory of vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
which are generically flat. As Scholze’s functor (
9.2.1
) is fully faithful and we already know that
\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})
from
5.18
, both the full faithfulness and essential surjectivity in
9.22
will follow if we can prove that the functor

\{\text{generically flat vector bundles on $X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}$}\}\rightarrow\{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}

obtained by restricting (
9.2.3
) to generically flat vector bundles is fully faithful. By dualisability, this reduces to a cohomology computation, which we in turn would like to reduce to a question about vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{HT}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-local systems on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
using the
t
-adic filtration on the double complex from
5.17
. However, note that this filtration is a priori not complete! This is remedied by the following key technical lemma:

Lemma 9.23
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
equipped with an étale map
X\rightarrow\Spa\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle
such that the induced map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
is Berkovich étale. Let
E
be a generically flat vector bundle on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
, which by
5.15
corresponds to a vector bundle
V
on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{t\}^{\dagger}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
equipped with a semilinear locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action. If
\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}:V\rightarrow V
denotes the “arithmetic Sen operator” on
V
obtained from the Lie algebra action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, then

\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}:t^{i}V\rightarrow t^{i}V

is an isomorphism for
i\gg 0
.

Proof.

By generic flatness of
E
, there is a filtered vector bundle with connection
(\Fil^{\bullet}W,\nabla)
on
X
whose associated
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system agrees with the
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system associated to
E
. We will show that
\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}
is an isomorphism on
t^{i}V
for any
i
such that
\Fil^{i}W=0
, which implies the claim.

To prove this, we may work locally on
X
and, in particular, assume that all
\Fil^{\bullet}W
are free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules and that the transition maps of the filtration are given by the standard embeddings via the first couple of coordinates. Moreover, by
[
43
, Lem. 7.3]
, we may assume that the pullback of
V
to
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
is free after possibly further localising on
X
and then we may even assume that
V
itself is free since freeness of a finite projective module may be checked after
\dagger
-reduction (as the
\dagger
-nilradical is contained in the Jacobson radical). In the following, let us introduce the notation
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}}
for the pushforward of the structure sheaf along

X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{t\}^{\dagger}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow X

and note that this may be viewed as a sheaf on the underlying topological space
|X|
of
X
in the sense of
[
1
, Def. 4.3.1]
. We point out that the above map is affine in the sense that the source becomes affine after pulling back to any affine
\GSpec A\rightarrow X
.

Now note that the semilinearity of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action implies that
\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}
will be a
t
-connection in the following sense: for all local sections
f
of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}}
and
v
of
V
, we have

\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}(fv)=f\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}(v)+t\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}v\;.

Thus, after choosing a basis of
V
, we have

\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}=A+t\frac{\partial}{\partial t}

for some
m\times m
-matrix
A
of global sections of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}}
, where
m
is the rank of
V
, and our claim is that the operator

(9.2.4)

(A+i)+t\frac{\partial}{\partial t}:(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}})^{\oplus m}\rightarrow(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}})^{\oplus m}

is invertible for all
i
as in the first paragraph.

To this end, writing

A=A_{0}+A_{1}t+A_{2}t^{2}+\dots

for
m\times m
-matrices
A_{\ell}
of global functions on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
, our first claim is the following:

Claim 1.
For all
i
as in the first paragraph, the matrix
A_{0}+i
is invertible.

Proof of the claim.
Recall from the proof of
7.4
that the
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system associated to
(\Fil^{\bullet}W,\nabla)
coincides with the base change of the
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})[t]
-module

(9.2.5)

\bigoplus_{\bullet}t^{-\bullet}(\Fil^{\bullet}W\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}))\;,

where
t
acts via the transition maps
t^{-\bullet}\Fil^{\bullet}W\rightarrow t^{-\bullet+1}\Fil^{\bullet-1}W
, along the map
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})[t]\rightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+}
induced by the given toric chart. Here, (
9.2.5
) is equipped with an action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
by letting
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
act canonically on
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}
while
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
acts on
t
by multiplication and on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
via the usual Galois action. As we have assumed that all
\Fil^{\bullet}W
are free and that the transition maps are the standard inclusions, this means that the image of
(\Fil^{\bullet}W,\nabla)
is isomorphic to
(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}}^{+})^{\oplus m}
on the localised site
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}/X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
and that, under this isomorphism, the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
is given by
\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
acting on the standard basis via the diagonal matrix
\mathrm{diag}(\gamma^{-a_{1}},\dots,\gamma^{-a_{m}})
, where the
a_{k}
are the indices of the jumps in the filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}W
counted with multiplicity. In particular, note that
a_{k}<i
.

By assumption, there is an invertible
m\times m
-matrix
S
of sections of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
over
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
such that the map

V\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}}}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR},X^{\mathrm{cycl}}_{\infty}}\cong(\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR},X^{\mathrm{cycl}}_{\infty}})^{\oplus m}\overset{S}{\longrightarrow}(\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR},X^{\mathrm{cycl}}_{\infty}})^{\oplus m}

is
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant, where the right-hand side denotes the restriction of the image of
(\Fil^{\bullet}W,\nabla)
under Scholze’s functor to
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}/X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
with the induced
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action while the isomorphism on the left-hand side uses our chosen basis of
V
. Writing
S=(s_{ij})_{i,j}
and
S^{-1}=(s^{\prime}_{ij})_{i,j}
, this means that the action of
\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
on the
k
-th basis vector
e_{k}
of
V
is given by

(9.2.6)

\begin{split}\gamma.e_{j}&=S^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma^{-a_{1}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\gamma^{-a_{m}}\end{pmatrix}Se_{j}=S^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma^{-a_{1}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\gamma^{-a_{m}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}s_{1j}\\
\vdots\\
s_{mj}\end{pmatrix}\\
&=S^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma^{-a_{1}}\cdot(\gamma.s_{1j})\\
\vdots\\
\gamma^{-a_{m}}\cdot(\gamma.s_{mj})\end{pmatrix}=\left(\sum_{k}s^{\prime}_{ik}\gamma^{-a_{k}}(\gamma.s_{kj})\right)_{i}\;.\end{split}

Since the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
on
V
is locally analytic, all entries of the last vector must be locally analytic functions in
\gamma
and, in particular, the functions
\gamma\mapsto\gamma.s_{kj}
must be locally analytic: Indeed, multiplying the last vector by the matrix of constants
S
from the left yields the vector
(\gamma^{-a_{k}}\cdot(\gamma.s_{kj}))_{k}
, all of whose entries must thus be locally analytic functions in
\gamma
. As taking the derivative of the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times,\mathrm{la}}
at
\gamma=1
yields the action of
\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}
, we obtain

(9.2.7)

\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}(e_{j})=\left(\sum_{k}(-a_{k})s^{\prime}_{ik}s_{kj}+s^{\prime}_{ik}\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}(\gamma.s_{kj})}{\mathrm{d}\gamma}\right|_{\gamma=1}\right)_{i}

from (
9.2.6
), i.e. the right-hand side is the
j
-th column of the matrix
A
.

Now note that
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}})/t\cong\mathcal{O}(X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}})
and recall that there is a canonical map
\mathcal{O}(X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}})\rightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cycl}})
. Thus, after passing to a rational localisation of
X
so that
X
becomes affine, we may approximate
s_{kj}
as

s_{kj}=r_{kj,N}+p^{N}(\,\dots)+t(\,\dots)

for any
N\geq 0
and some
r_{kj,N}\in\mathcal{O}(X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}})
. As the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
is smooth and
\gamma.t=\gamma\cdot t
, this yields

\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}(\gamma.s_{kj})}{\mathrm{d}\gamma}\right|_{\gamma=1}=p^{N}(\,\dots)+t(\,\dots)\;,

where we note that the implicit terms in brackets might now be different ones than in the previous equation. In other words, we may conclude from (
9.2.7
) that the
j
-th column of
A_{0}
coincides with
(-\sum_{k}a_{k}s^{\prime}_{ik}s_{kj})_{i}
up to a multiple of
p^{N}
. Since the ring
\mathcal{O}(X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}))
is
p
-adically separated, this yields

A_{0}=\left(-\sum_{k}a_{k}s^{\prime}_{ik}s_{kj}\right)_{i,j}=S^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}-a_{1}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&-a_{m}\end{pmatrix}S\;,

hence

A_{0}+i=S^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}-a_{1}+i&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&-a_{m}+i\end{pmatrix}S\;.

Now we are done: Indeed, the above yields

\det(A_{0}+i)=\prod_{k=1}^{m}(i-a_{k})

and, by assumption, we have
a_{k}<i
for all
k
. We conclude that
\det(A_{0}+i)
is a unit in the
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
\mathcal{O}(X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}))
and thus the claim follows. ∎

Recall from (
9.2.4
) that we need to show that the operator

\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}+i=(A+i)+t\frac{\partial}{\partial t}:(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}})^{\oplus m}\rightarrow(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}})^{\oplus m}

is invertible. We start with injectivity and, to this end, take some

x=\sum_{\ell}x_{\ell}t^{\ell}\in(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}})^{\oplus m}\;,

where each
x_{\ell}
is an
m
-tuple of local sections of the pushforward of the structure sheaf along
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})\rightarrow X
viewed as a sheaf on
|X|
. Let
\ell
be minimal such that
x_{\ell}\neq 0
. Then

(\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}+i)(x)=(A_{0}+i)x_{\ell}t^{\ell}+\ell x_{\ell}t^{\ell}+t^{\ell+1}(\,\dots)=(A_{0}+(i+\ell))x_{\ell}t^{\ell}+t^{\ell+1}(\,\dots)

and by applying Claim 1 to
i+\ell
in place of
i
, we see that
(A_{0}+(i+\ell))x_{\ell}
and consequently
(\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}+i)(x)
is nonzero.

For surjectivity, let
y=\sum_{\ell}y_{\ell}t^{\ell}\in(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}})^{\oplus m}
. By Claim 1, we can then recursively solve the equations

(9.2.8)

(A_{0}+i+\ell)x_{\ell}+\sum_{k<\ell}A_{\ell-k}x_{k}=y_{\ell}

for
\ell=0,1,2,\dots
and we claim that then

x\coloneqq\sum_{\ell}x_{\ell}t^{\ell}

is in
(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}})^{\oplus m}
and satisfies
(\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}+i)(x)=y
. Indeed, once we know the former, the latter is immediate since the coefficient of
t^{\ell}
in the expansion of
(\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}+i)(x)
is exactly given by the left-hand side of (
9.2.8
). To establish that
x\in(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}})^{\oplus m}
, we need to understand the growth of the
x_{\ell}
, for which we begin by proving the following explicit formula:

Claim 2.
For all
\ell\geq 0
, we have

x_{\ell}=\sum_{k\leq\ell}\left(\left(\sum_{\lambda\vdash(\ell-k)}(-1)^{\ell(\lambda)}\prod_{j}A_{\lambda_{j}}(A_{0}+i+k+|\lambda|_{\leq j})^{-1}\right)\cdot(A_{0}+i+k)^{-1}y_{k}\right)\;,

where the inner sum runs over all ordered (!) partitions
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{s})
of
\ell-k
into positive parts,
\ell(\lambda)\coloneqq s
denotes the length of such a partition and we set

|\lambda|_{\leq j}\coloneqq\lambda_{1}+\dots+\lambda_{j}

for any
1\leq j\leq s
.

Proof of the claim.
We use induction on
\ell
, in the base case
\ell=0
the claimed formula becomes
x_{0}=(A_{0}+i)^{-1}y_{0}
, which is true (note that
0
admits the empty partition!). For the induction step, note that (
9.2.8
) implies that

x_{\ell}=(A_{0}+i+\ell)^{-1}\left(y_{\ell}-\sum_{k<\ell}A_{\ell-k}x_{k}\right)\;.

Plugging in the formulas for the
x_{k}
with
k<\ell
, we see that the right-hand side is a linear combination of the
y_{k}
with
k\leq\ell
. For
k=\ell
, the coefficient of
y_{\ell}
is given by
(A_{0}+i+\ell)^{-1}
, which is in keeping with the claimed formula (again because the empty partition of
0
is allowed), while the coefficient of
y_{k}
for
k<\ell
is given by

-(A_{0}+i+\ell)^{-1}\sum_{k\leq k^{\prime}<\ell}A_{\ell-k^{\prime}}\left(\left(\sum_{\lambda\vdash(k^{\prime}-k)}(-1)^{\ell(\lambda)}\prod_{j}A_{\lambda_{j}}(A_{0}+i+k+|\lambda|_{\leq j})^{-1}\right)\cdot(A_{0}+i+k)^{-1}\right)\;.

Noting that there is a bijection

\begin{split}\{\text{pairs $(k^{\prime},\lambda)$ with $k\leq k^{\prime}<\ell$ and $\lambda\vdash(k^{\prime}-k)$}\}&\xleftrightarrow{1:1}\{\mu\vdash(\ell-k)\}\\
(k^{\prime},(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{s}))&\;\,\mapsto\;\,\mu\coloneqq(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{s},\ell-k^{\prime})\;,\end{split}

we see that the above expression simplifies to

\left(\sum_{\mu\vdash(\ell-k)}(-1)^{\ell(\mu)}\prod_{j}A_{\mu_{j}}(A_{0}+i+k+|\mu|_{\leq j})^{-1}\right)(A_{0}+i+k)^{-1},

which is exactly the coefficient of
y_{k}
in the claimed formula. ∎

In order to show
x\in(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}})^{\oplus m}
, we have to argue that there is some
N>0
such that the local sections
p^{\ell N}x_{\ell}
forms a nullsequence. Since
A
is a matrix over
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}}
and
y\in(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger,\mathrm{la}})^{\oplus m}
, we know that there is some
M>0
with
p^{\ell M}A_{\ell}\rightarrow 0
and
p^{\ell M}y_{\ell}\rightarrow 0
; as the rings we are working in are bounded, after possibly enlarging
M
, we may even assume that, for
\ell\geq 1
, the sequence
(p^{\ell M}A_{\ell})_{\ell}
is a nullsequence in the subring of powerbounded elements. Observing that

p^{\ell M}x_{\ell}=\sum_{k\leq\ell}\left(\left(\sum_{\lambda\vdash(\ell-k)}(-1)^{\ell(\lambda)}\prod_{j}p^{\lambda_{j}M}A_{\lambda_{j}}(A_{0}+i+k+|\lambda|_{\leq j})^{-1}\right)\cdot(A_{0}+i+k)^{-1}p^{kM}y_{k}\right)

by Claim 2, it thus only remains to control the inverses occurring in the formula.

However, note that
\det(A_{0}+i+k+|\lambda|_{\leq j})(A_{0}+i+k+|\lambda|_{\leq j})^{-1}
is a matrix whose entries are degree
m
integer polynomials in the entries of
A_{0}
by Cramer’s rule. Further observing that each summand in the formula above only contains at most
\ell+1
such inverses as factors, we are thus reduced to controlling the determinants
\det(A_{0}+i+k+|\lambda|_{\leq j})
. Recalling that these determinants are integers by the proof of Claim 1, we have to show that the
p
-adic valuation of the integers

\det(A_{0}+i+k)\prod_{j}\det(A_{0}+i+k+|\lambda|_{\leq j})

is uniformly linearly bounded in
\ell
, where
k\leq\ell
and
\lambda\vdash(\ell-k)
.

Recall, though, that the proof of Claim 1 gives an explicit expression for these determinants; namely, the above product is equal to

\prod_{r=1}^{m}\left((i+k-a_{r})\prod_{j}(i+k+|\lambda|_{\leq j}-a_{r})\right)\;.

Observing that
1\leq|\lambda|_{\leq 1}<|\lambda|_{\leq 2}<\dots\leq\ell-k
, we see that the above is a divisor of

\prod_{r=1}^{m}(i+\ell-a_{r})!

upon recalling that
i>a_{r}
for all
r
. As the
p
-adic valuation of this latter product is bounded by
m\cdot(i+\ell-a)/(p-1)
, where
a
denotes the minimum of the
a_{r}
, we win.
∎

Finally, we can finish the proof of
9.22
. In particular, recall that this also immediately proves
9.16
by the discussion preceding
9.22
.

Proof of
9.22
.

By compatibility of
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
with open localisations, see
4.22
, and since generic flatness may be checked locally on
X
, we may assume that
X
admits a rigid étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
. In this case, recall that we already know that the functor
\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})
factors through the full subcategory spanned by generically flat vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
by
7.1
.

If we can show that the functor

(9.2.9)

\{\text{generically flat vector bundles on $X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}$}\}\rightarrow\{\text{$\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}$-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}

obtained by restricting (
9.2.3
) to generically flat vector bundles is fully faithful, we are done: Full faithfulness then follows from the fact that the composite functor

(9.2.10)

\begin{split}\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})&\rightarrow\{\text{generically flat vector bundles on $X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}$}\}\\
&\rightarrow\{\text{$\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}$-local systems on $X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}$}\}\end{split}

agrees with Scholze’s functor (
9.2.1
) by
7.1
, which is known to be fully faithful, see
[
43
, Thm. 7.6]
. We also get essential surjectivity: If
E
is a generically flat vector bundle on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
, its associated
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system
\mathbb{M}
is associated to a filtered vector bundle with connection
(\Fil^{\bullet}W,\nabla)
on
X
. Then
(\Fil^{\bullet}W,\nabla)
corresponds to a vector bundle
E^{\prime}
on
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
, whose image under the composite functor (
9.2.10
) is known to be isomorphic to
\mathbb{M}
by
7.1
. As the last functor in (
9.2.10
) is fully faithful, this means that the image of
E^{\prime}
under

\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})\rightarrow\{\text{generically flat vector bundles on $X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}$}\}

is already isomorphic to
E
and then the desired preimage of
E
is the vector bundle on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
corresponding to
E^{\prime}
via the equivalence
\Vect(X^{\mathrm{dR},+})\cong\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+})
.

To prove that (
9.2.9
) is fully faithful, first note that it is symmetric monoidal and compatible with taking duals. Thus, checking that
\Hom(F,E)\cong\Hom(\mathbb{N},\mathbb{M})
for
E,F\in\Vect(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger})
generically flat and
\mathbb{M},\mathbb{N}
their associated
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local systems reduces to the case
F=\mathcal{O}
by replacing
E
by
E\otimes F^{\vee}
. In other words, it suffices to show that, for any generically flat vector bundle
E
on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
, we have

(9.2.11)

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger},E)\cong R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{M})\;,

where
\mathbb{M}
is the
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-local system associated to
E
. Using the notation from
5.17
, the left-hand side is given by the (underived)
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-invariants of the cohomology on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
of the total complex of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{2}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-2)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{2}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-2)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}

where
D
is just given by
\Theta^{\mathrm{arithm}}
, and then
9.23
shows that the
t
-adic filtration on this total complex is complete. Moreover, since
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
and hence
\mathbb{M}
is
t
-adically complete, also the filtration

\dots\rightarrow R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},t^{2}\mathbb{M})\rightarrow R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},t\mathbb{M})\rightarrow R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{M})

is complete.

Thus, we may check the isomorphism (
9.2.11
) on associated graded pieces. Each graded piece of
t^{\bullet}V
will be a vector bundle
V^{\prime}
on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
equipped with a semilinear locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action and the corresponding graded piece of
t^{\bullet}\mathbb{M}
will be the pullback of
V^{\prime}
to a locally free
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-module
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
. Our claim then amounts to showing that the (underived)
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-invariants of the cohomology on
X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
of the total complex of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{2}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-2)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega^{2}_{X_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}(-2)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}

where
\nabla
and
D
arise from the Lie algebra actions of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, respectively, compute
R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{M}^{\prime})
. However, this is now an instance of geometric Sen theory, see
[
40
]
: taking locally analytic vectors for the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
-action on
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
recovers
V^{\prime}
by
[
2
]
and
\nabla
is the corresponding geometric Sen operator while
D
is the arithmetic Sen operator of the Galois representation
R\Gamma(X_{\mathbb{C}_{p},\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{M}^{\prime})
.
∎

Remark 9.24
.

One can also define a notion of generic flatness for
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
-local systems on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
using the functor from filtered vector bundles with connection on
X
to
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
-local systems from
7.2
. It is then not a priori clear that generic flatness of a
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
-local system may be checked after extending scalars to
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
. However, using that vector bundles on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
are equivalent via pullback by
[
2
]
, one should be able to use the isomorphism (
9.2.11
) from the proof above to check that this is indeed the case.
∎

10.
Geometric syntomic cohomology

Finally, we want to comment on how to apply the methods we have developed to the geometric case, i.e. to the cohomology of rigid spaces over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. For this, first recall the situation for the de Rham stack: By
[
1
, Ex. 4.7.6.(3)]
, we have
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR}}\cong\GSpec\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and hence just taking the de Rham stack cannot compute the usual de Rham cohomology of smooth partially proper rigid spaces over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. Indeed, the problem is that the stack
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
always computes “absolute de Rham cohomology”, i.e. de Rham cohomology with respect to the base
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, while the usual de Rham cohomology of rigid spaces over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
is rather relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. In other words, if
X
is a Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
, we should define an appropriate version
X^{\mathrm{dR}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
of
X^{\mathrm{dR}}
“relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
”. Meditating on the situation, one is eventually led to defining
X^{\mathrm{dR}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
via the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

where
\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR}}
is the canonical map, see
[
1
, Def. 4.5.11.(2)]
. One then proves as before that the cohomology of this stack calculates de Rham cohomology if
X
is a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
, see
[
1
, Prop. 5.2.1]
.

Thus, the key issue in defining syntomic cohomology in the geometric situation, i.e. for Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich spaces over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
, is to identify the “correct” base change of
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
or
X^{\mathrm{N}}
. Below, we will explain that there is a canonical map

\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\coloneqq(\{ut=\varphi^{-1}(\xi)\}\subseteq Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-})\,/\,\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\;,

where
\xi=p-[p^{\flat}]
, and that base changing
X^{\mathrm{N}}
along this map yields an appropriate definition for the filtered prismatisation
X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
of
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. We will justify this claim by showing that basically everything we have done in the “arithmetic case”, i.e. for smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, has an analogue in the geometric case with the definition of
X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
proposed above.

In particular, this includes the basic description of the geometry of the filtered prismatisation that we have discussed in §
4.2
and the local presentations of the filtered prismatisation obtained in §
5
. This will enable us to transfer the results from §§
8
,
9
and their proofs almost verbatim to the geometric setting. In particular, we recover versions of the comparison results between syntomic cohomology over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
and filtered Hyodo–Kato cohomology and between syntomic cohomology over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
and proétale cohomology from
[
17
, Thm. 1.3]
. However, we warn the reader that, in the geometric case, syntomic cohomology as we define it will
not
agree with the more classical definition as it appears e.g. in the work of Colmez–Nizioł, see
[
17
]
, but will rather recover a slight variant that has been introduced by Bosco in
[
15
]
, where it is called
syntomic Fargues–Fontaine cohomology
, see also
10.17
.

On the way, we will obtain a stacky interpretation of the
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology for smooth partially proper rigid spaces over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
as e.g. defined in
[
17
, §3]
,
[
28
]
or
[
12
, §13]
through a suitable base change of the analytic de Rham stack, and this will enable us to give a simple proof for the comparison between
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology and de Rham cohomology. Moreover, similarly to what happened in §
7
, we will be able to recover the geometric de Rham comparison theorem from
[
12
, Thm. 13.1]
, see
10.12
.

10.1.
Stacks attached to
p
-adic cohomology theories relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}

We start by recalling some definitions of stacks computing
p
-adic cohomology theories relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. As already discussed above, the main question throughout is along which map one should base change. To this end, recall from
3.19
that there is a canonical map
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
for any Gelfand stack
X
and that, similarly, we have canonical maps
\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and
\mathrm{FF}_{X^{\diamondsuit}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{HK}}
.

Definition 10.1
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. The
relative analytic prismatisation

X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
of
X
over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
is defined by the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}{\Delta}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Moreover, the
relative Hyodo–Kato stack

X^{\mathrm{HK}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
of
X
over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
and the stack
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
are defined by the pullback diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HK}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FF}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HK}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FF}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

We remark that
X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
is still equipped with a radius map
\kappa:X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
obtained by postcomposing the canonical map
X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
with the usual radius map on
X{\Delta}
. Moreover, base changing
3.23
shows that the natural map

X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow(X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})^{\mathrm{dR}/Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}\cong Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}^{\mathrm{dR}/Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}

is an isomorphism over the locus
(1,\infty)
, where
(X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})^{\mathrm{dR}/Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
is defined as the pullback of
(X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
along the canonical map
Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}
; note that Frobenius is an isomorphism on
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}^{\mathrm{dR}/Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
and that
Y_{X^{\diamondsuit}}^{\mathrm{dR}/Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}/\varphi{Z}\cong X^{\mathrm{HK}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
. Furthermore, from
3.28
, we deduce that Frobenius induces an isomorphism
X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{(0,1)}\cong X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{(0,p)}
and that

\operatorname{coeq}(\hskip-4.26773pt\hbox to122.32pt{\vbox to26.79pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 61.1606pt\lower-14.8408pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
