Let us also note that, as in
2.46
, one proves that pushforward along
X^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
preserves perfect complexes for any smooth partially proper qcqs rigid space
X
over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. In particular, the pushforward of any perfect complex along this map identifies with a
(\varphi,N)
-module in
\Perf(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}})
by
[
1
, Thm. 7.1.1]
and, as in the arithmetic case, this pushforward is expected to compute Hyodo–Kato cohomology of
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
, see
[
1
, Rem. 6.1.3]
.

Definition 10.2
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper qcqs rigid space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. We define the
Hyodo–Kato cohomology

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(X)
of
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
as the
(\varphi,N)
-module in
\Perf(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}})
corresponding to the pushforward of the structure sheaf along
X^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
.

We now turn to the filtered prismatisation. As already announced above, our aim will be to base change
X^{\mathrm{N}}
for
X
a Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
from
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
to the Gelfand stack

\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\coloneqq(\{ut=\varphi^{-1}(\xi)\}\subseteq Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-})\,/\,\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;,

where
t
denotes the coordinate on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}
,
u
is the coordinate on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-}
and
\xi=p-[p^{\flat}]
. Intuitively, one should think of this stack as the Rees stack associated to the “
\varphi^{-1}(\xi)
-adic filtration on
Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
” and we urge the reader to compare this definition to the presentation of the algebraic Nygaardification of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
in the sense of Drinfeld and Bhatt–Lurie from
[
8
, Ex. 5.5.6]
.

Our task will be to construct a map
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
along which to base change. For this, first observe that
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
admits a canonical map to
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}
via
t
and
u
. Moreover, there is an obvious projection map
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
and postcomposing with the canonical map
Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}{\Delta}
thus yields a map
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}{\Delta}
. Finally, unwinding definitions, we see that the equality
ut=\varphi^{-1}(\xi)
on
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
ensures that the composition

\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\mu}}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}

identifies with the composition

\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\times(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{mult}}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})^{\mathrm{dR}}

and hence we overall obtain a map

\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}{\Delta}}\mathbb{C}_{p}{\Delta}\;.

To upgrade this to a map to
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
, note that
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
also maps to
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\Cone}
over
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
. Indeed, using the isomorphism

(\{\varphi^{-1}(\xi)=0\}\subseteq Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong(\{\xi=0\}\subseteq Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}\;,

which is induced by Frobenius, we have

(\{t=0\}\subseteq\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-}\,/\,\overline{\mathbb{T}}

and the stack on the right-hand side admits a canonical map to
\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}
, which overall induces a map
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\Cone}
. Recalling from
4.7
that
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}
may be expressed as the pullback of
\mathbb{C}_{p}{\Delta}
along
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\Cone}\rightarrow(\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\Cone})^{\mathrm{dR}}
, the preceding discussion yields an induced map

\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}

upon realising that the maps
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}{\Delta}
and
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\Cone}
we have given are compatible upon mapping to
(\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\Cone})^{\mathrm{dR}}
. Using this, we make the following definition:

Definition 10.3
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. The
relative analytic Nygaardification

X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
of
X
over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
is defined by the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{N}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

As in §
4.2
, let us discuss the restriction of
X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
to various interesting loci. As we have already done this for
X^{\mathrm{N}}
in loc. cit., this reduces to investigating various restrictions of
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
. Namely, first note that there are isomorphisms

(\{|u|=1\}\subseteq\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\cong(\{|t|=1\}\subseteq\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})

and thus, using
4.8
, we conclude that

X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{|u|=1}\cong X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{|t|=1}

for any Gelfand stack
X
over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. As in the arithmetic case, we denote the corresponding closed embeddings of
X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
into
X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
by

j_{\mathrm{dR}}:X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{|t|=1}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\;,\hskip 14.22636ptj_{\mathrm{HT}}:X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{|u|=1}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\;.

Moreover, observe that
2.22
yields an isomorphism

\begin{split}(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}^{\times}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-}^{\times})\,/\,\overline{\mathbb{T}}&\cong(\{|x|\leq|u|\}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-}^{\times}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})\cong(\{|x|\leq|u|\}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}\times(0,1])\\
(t,u)&\mapsto(tu,u)\;,\end{split}

where
x=tu
is the coordinate on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{\times}
in the target and hence we obtain

(\{|ut|\neq 0\}\subseteq\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong(\{0<|\varphi^{-1}(\xi)|\leq|u|\}\subseteq Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\times(0,1])\;.

Finally, we note that

(Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\setminus\{\varphi^{-1}(\xi)=0\})\times[0,1]\xrightarrow{\cong}\{0<|\varphi^{-1}(\xi)|\leq|u|\}\subseteq Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\times(0,1])

via the map sending
y\in[0,1]
to the real number
|u|\in[|\varphi^{-1}(\xi)|,1]
which is the second coordinate of the unique intersection point
(|t|,|u|)
of the hyperbola
\{|u|\cdot|t|=|\varphi^{-1}(\xi)|\}
and the line through
(1,1)
and
(1-y,y)
. Together with
4.9
, this induces an isomorphism

X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{|ut|\neq 0}\cong X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{|\widetilde{\mu}|\neq 0}\times[0,1]

with the same properties as in loc. cit., where we note that the structure map
\pi:X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
is induced by the projection
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
and the structure map
\pi:X^{\mathrm{N}}\rightarrow X{\Delta}
in the arithmetic case.

What is the analogue of the map
i_{\mathrm{dR},+}:X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}}
? Here we actually encounter a new “relative stack”, which we shall promptly define. Before, however, we recall the ring
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
defined by

B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\coloneqq A_{\mathrm{inf}}\{\xi\}^{\dagger}[\tfrac{1}{p}]=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{n}A_{\mathrm{inf}}\langle p^{-n}\xi\rangle[\tfrac{1}{p}]

and note that it is a (separable qfd) Gelfand ring: Indeed, we have
\xi\in\Nil^{\dagger}(B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger})
and hence the uniform completion of
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
is given by
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}/\xi\cong\mathbb{C}_{p}
. Now observe that the quotient

\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;\big/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;,

where
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
acts by multiplication on
t
and by division on
u
, parametrises
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
-algebras
A
together with a pair
(t:L\rightarrow A,u:A\rightarrow L)
of normed (dual) generalised Cartier divisors with
|t|\leq 1
and
|u|=0
and an identification between the composition

A\xrightarrow{u}L\xrightarrow{t}A

and multiplication by
\xi
. Noting that this data in particular yields a map

\mathbb{C}_{p}\cong B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}/\xi\rightarrow A/\xi\cong A/tu\rightarrow\Cone(L\otimes_{A}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\xrightarrow{t}A)\;,

we obtain a canonical map

\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;\big/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}\;.

Warning 10.4
.

We immediately warn the reader that the coordinate
t
is
not
to be confused with the element
\log[\varepsilon]\in B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
, where
\varepsilon=(1,\zeta_{p},\zeta_{p^{2}},\dots)
, which usually goes by the same name. However, this element will
not
play a role in the sequel and the letter
t
will always refer to the coordinate on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}
.

Definition 10.5
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. The
filtered
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
-stack

X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
of
X
is defined by the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;\big/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Moreover, the
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
-stack

X^{\mathrm{dR}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
of
X
is defined as the base change of
X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
along the canonical map
\overline{\mathbb{T}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
while the stack
X^{\mathrm{Hod}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
is defined as the base change of
X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
along
*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
.

From the definition of
\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
, we deduce that there is an isomorphism

(\{|u|=0\}\subseteq\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;\big/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;,

which we remark involves a Frobenius on
Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
and thus we conclude from
4.11
that there is an isomorphism

X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{|u|=0}

for any Gelfand stack
X
over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. In particular, as

(\{u=0\}\subseteq\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;\big/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}})\cong\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\;,

we obtain

X^{\mathrm{dR},+/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\cong X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}_{u=0}\cong X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{u=0}\;,

where the source is defined as the base change of
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
along the canonical map
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}
, see
[
39
, Def. 5.2.2.(2)]
.

Let us also define

X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\coloneqq X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{|t|=0}\;,\hskip 14.22636ptX^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\coloneqq X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{|t|=0,|u|=1}

and note that

(\{|t|=0,|u|\neq 0\}\subseteq\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\times(0,1]

by
2.22
, which together with
4.13
yields an isomorphism

X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{|u|\neq 0}\cong X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\times(0,1]

with the same properties as in loc. cit.

Finally, we define the syntomification relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. This is done in the usual way using the closed embeddings
j_{\mathrm{dR}},j_{\mathrm{HT}}:X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
from above.

Definition 10.6
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. The
relative analytic syntomification

X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
of
X
over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
is defined as the coequaliser

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathbbl{\Delta}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\;.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{HT}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{dR}}}

Moreover, for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
relative analytic syntomic cohomology
of weight
i
of
X
over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
is defined as

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X/\mathbb{C}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))\coloneqq R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathcal{O}\{i\})\;,

where
\mathcal{O}\{i\}
is pulled back along the natural map
X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
.

To define Hodge–Tate weights of perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
, note that the above discussion shows that the locus
\{u=t=0\}
inside
X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
identifies with the stack
X^{\mathrm{Hod}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
, which sits in a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Hod}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Hod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{Hod}}}

and hence receives a natural map

X\times*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Hod}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\;.

Definition 10.7
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
and
E
a perfect complex on
X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
. Identifying the pullback of
E
along the map

X\times*/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{Hod}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}

with a graded perfect complex
M^{\bullet}
on
X
, the
Hodge–Tate weights
of
E
are defined to be those integers
i\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
M^{i}
is nonzero. The Hodge–Tate weights of a perfect analytic
F
-gauge
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})
on
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
are defined to be the Hodge–Tate weights of its pullback to the stack
X^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
.

10.2.
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology via stacks

Before we can move on to discussing the analogues of the comparison theorems from §§
8
,
9
in the geometric setting, we first have to take a closer look at the filtered
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
-stack
X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
for a smooth partially proper rigid space
X
over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. Namely, we will prove that cohomology on this stack actually computes the usual
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology
R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})
of
X
equipped with its filtration after base changing along
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\rightarrow B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
:

Theorem 10.8
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. Then there is a natural isomorphism

\Fil^{\bullet}R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},\mathcal{O})\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\cong\Fil^{\bullet}R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})\;.

Before we begin the proof, let us prepare ourselves by studying the cohomology of
X^{\mathrm{Hod}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
, which is defined as the locus
\{t=0\}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
and hence sits inside a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Hod}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}\{u\}^{\dagger}/\,\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Hod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{Hod}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Lemma 10.9
.

Let
X
be a Berkovich smooth
\dagger
-rigid space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
in the sense of
[
1
, Def. 4.3.6]
. Then

X^{\mathrm{Hod}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\cong\left(X\times\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}\{u\}^{\dagger}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\right)\big/\,\mathcal{T}_{X/\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle\;,

where the action of
\mathcal{T}_{X/\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle
is trivial and
\mathcal{T}_{X/\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\dagger}
denotes the overconvergent neighbourhood of the zero section inside the restriction of
\AnSpec_{X}\Sym_{X}^{\bullet}\Omega^{1}_{X/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
to the test category of Gelfand rings. In particular, we have

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Hod}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},\mathcal{O}\langle i\rangle)\cong\bigoplus_{j\leq i}R\Gamma(X,\Omega^{j}_{X/\mathbb{C}_{p}})[-j]

for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Recall that
X^{\mathrm{Hod}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
is the sheafification of the assignment sending any triple
(A,u:A\rightarrow L)
of a Gelfand
\mathbb{C}_{p}
-algebra
A
and a dual normed generalised Cartier divisor of norm zero to the anima of maps
\GSpec(A\oplus L\otimes_{A}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)[1])\rightarrow X
. By derived deformation theory, the anima of such maps which lift a fixed map
\eta:\GSpec A\rightarrow X
is a torsor for

\map_{A}(\eta^{*}\Omega^{1}_{X/\mathbb{C}_{p}},\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\otimes_{A}L[1])\cong\eta^{*}(\Omega^{1}_{X/\mathbb{C}_{p}})^{\vee}\otimes_{A}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\otimes_{A}L[1]

and hence we conclude that the natural map
X^{\mathrm{Hod}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\rightarrow X\times\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}\{u\}^{\dagger}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is a gerbe banded by
\mathcal{T}_{X/\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle
. Noting that the gerbe has a splitting induced by the canonical map
A\rightarrow A\oplus L\otimes_{A}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)[1]
proves the first assertion. Now the second assertion follows by Cartier duality, see
[
39
, Thm. 4.3.13]
and
2.26
, together with
2.20
.
∎

We can now move on to the proof of
10.8
.

Proof of
10.8
.

We first note that the map
\Cone(L\otimes_{A}\Nil^{\dagger}(A)\rightarrow A)\rightarrow\overline{A}
for any Gelfand
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
-algebra
A
equipped with a normed generalised Cartier divisor
L\rightarrow A
induces a natural map

f:X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{dR}}\times\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\rightarrow|X|\times\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\;.

Note that, by compatibility of the assignment
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
with open localisation, which one deduces from
4.22
by base change, the preimage of any open subset of
|X|
under this map is given by the filtered
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
-stack of the corresponding open subspace of
X
. By
[
15
, Prop. 2.42]
, our task is to check that there is a natural isomorphism

\Fil^{\bullet}f_{*}\mathcal{O}\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\cong\tau^{\leq 0}_{\mathrm{Beil}}\nu_{*}\Fil^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\;,

where the filtration on
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
is the
\xi
-adic filtration,
\nu
is the morphism of sites
\nu:X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow|X|
and the Beilinson
t
-structure on filtered objects is taken with respect to the standard
t
-structure on sheaves on the topological space
|X|
; moreover, we write
\Fil^{\bullet}f_{*}\mathcal{O}
for the filtered object

\dots\xrightarrow{t}f_{*}\mathcal{O}\langle-1\rangle\xrightarrow{t}f_{*}\mathcal{O}\xrightarrow{t}f_{*}\mathcal{O}\langle 1\rangle\xrightarrow{t}\dots\;.

We first construct a natural filtered morphism

(10.2.1)

\Fil^{\bullet}f_{*}\mathcal{O}\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\rightarrow\nu_{*}\Fil^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\;,

which in turn comes down to constructing natural filtered morphisms

\Fil^{\bullet}R\Gamma(U^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},\mathcal{O})\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\rightarrow R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U,\Fil^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})\;,

for any open subspace
U\subseteq X
. This works as follows: For any affinoid perfectoid
C
over
U
, we have a map

\GSpec\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}(C)\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{dR},+}

defined in the same way as for
C=\mathbb{C}_{p}
above, and together with the natural map
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}(C)
, this induces a map

(10.2.2)

\GSpec\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}(C)\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\;.

Now observe that the filtered object calculated by the cohomology of the source of (
10.2.2
) is given by
\Fil^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}(C)
and thus, by functoriality of the filtered
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
-stack, we obtain a filtered morphism

\begin{split}\Fil^{\bullet}R\Gamma(U^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},\mathcal{O})\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}&\rightarrow\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{\GSpec C\rightarrow U}\Fil^{\bullet}R\Gamma(C^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},\mathcal{O})\\
&\rightarrow\operatornamewithlimits{lim}_{\GSpec C\rightarrow U}\Fil^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(C)\cong R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U,\Fil^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})\;,\end{split}

where the second arrow is given by pullback of sections along (
10.2.2
).

Next, observe that
\Fil^{\bullet}f_{*}\mathcal{O}\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
is connective for the Beilinson
t
-structure since

(10.2.3)

\gr^{i}f_{*}\mathcal{O}\cong\bigoplus_{j\leq i}\Omega_{X/\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{j}[-j]

by applying
10.9
to open subspaces
U\subseteq X
and thus (
10.2.1
) naturally factors through a map

(10.2.4)

\Fil^{\bullet}f_{*}\mathcal{O}\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\rightarrow\tau^{\leq 0}_{\mathrm{Beil}}\nu_{*}\Fil^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\;.

Putting this together with

\nu_{*}t^{i}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}/t^{i+1}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\cong\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\Omega^{n}_{X}[-n]\;,

see
[
44
, Prop. 3.23]
, we conclude that (
10.2.4
) is an isomorphism on graded pieces. As all filtration steps of
\nu_{*}\Fil^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
are cohomologically bounded above by
[
15
, Prop. 2.40]
, the natural map

\tau_{\mathrm{Beil}}^{\leq 0}\nu_{*}\Fil^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\rightarrow\nu_{*}\Fil^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}

is an isomorphism in high enough filtration degrees. Since the filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
is complete, we conclude that the target of (
10.2.4
) is complete for the filtration as well. As we have just seen that (
10.2.4
) induces isomorphisms on graded pieces, we are done once we know that the source is complete for the filtration as well.

For this we may localise on
|X|
and then assume that
X
admits a smooth lift
\widetilde{X}
to
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
which comes equipped with a Berkovich étale map
\widetilde{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n}\times\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
for some
n\geq 0
. Since Berkovich étale maps are
\dagger
-formally étale, this yields a cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{X}\times_{\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n}\times\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Now the bottom map exhibits the target as the quotient of the source by the action of
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle^{n}
by addition and thus we conclude

X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\cong\left(\widetilde{X}\times_{\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}\right)\big/\,\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle^{n}\;.

By Cartier duality, see
2.26
, and using that the addition action of
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
corresponds to the endomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{\mathbb{D}}}
given by the derivative, we conclude from the above presentation that

\Fil^{\bullet}f_{*}\mathcal{O}\cong\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}^{1}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}^{2}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}}\dots)\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\Fil^{\bullet}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}

and tensoring with
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
yields

\Fil^{\bullet}f_{*}\mathcal{O}\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\cong\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Hod}}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}^{1}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}^{2}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}}\dots)\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\Fil^{\bullet}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\;.

Finally, as the Hodge filtration vanishes in high enough degrees, completeness of the filtration on the left-hand side now follows from completeness of the
\xi
-adic filtration on
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

The fact that the stack
X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
computes filtered
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology of smooth partially proper rigid spaces over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
by
10.8
now lets us easily deduce some well-known properties of
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology.

Corollary 10.10
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. Then there is a natural isomorphism

R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}}\mathbb{C}_{p}\cong R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/\mathbb{C}_{p})

compatible with the filtrations. Moreover, if
X
is qcqs and admits a model
\overline{X}
over some finite extension
K
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, then

R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})\cong R\Gamma_{\mathrm{dR}}(\overline{X})\otimes_{K}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\;,

again compatibly with the filtrations.

Proof.

For the first isomorphism, observe that the big rectangle and the lower square in the diagram

(10.2.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}}

are cartesian, hence the upper square is cartesian as well. Moreover, the map

\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}\rightarrow\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}

is cohomologically smooth since this property is local on the target and the map

\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\rightarrow\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)

is a complete intersection with kernel generated by
u
. Thus, the top square in the diagram (
10.2.5
) satisfies base change by
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.13.(i)]
and this implies the claim upon recalling that
X^{\mathrm{dR},+/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
calculates Hodge-filtered de Rham cohomology of
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
, see
[
1
, Rem. 5.2.2]
.

For the second claim, we argue similarly: Both the bottom and the top square in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{X}^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\mathrm{dR},+}}

are cartesian and hence the same is true for the large rectangle. By
10.11
below, the map
\overline{X}^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow K^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong\GSpec K\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is prim and thus the large rectangle satisfies base change by
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.13.(ii)]
, which implies the claim.
∎

The following lemma was used in the proof:

Lemma 10.11
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper qcqs rigid space over a finite extension
K
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then the map
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow K^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong\GSpec K\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is prim.

Proof.

As in the proof of
2.45
, we may assume that
X
admits a Berkovich étale map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{K}^{n}
after passing to a finite strict closed cover of
X
using compatibility of
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{dR},+}
with rational localisations, which one deduces from
4.22
. After possibly passing to a further finite strict closed cover, we can assume that the map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{K}^{n}
factors as
X\rightarrow Z\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{K}^{n}
, where
X\rightarrow Z
is finite étale and
Z\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{K}^{n}
is a rational localisation. By
4.22
, the map
Z^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow(\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n}_{K})^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is a rational localisation as well and hence prim. Moreover, as
X\rightarrow Z
is finite étale and hence in particular
\dagger
-formally étale, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\mathrm{dR},+}}

is cartesian and since
Z
is
\dagger
-formally smooth (as it is a rational subspace of
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{K}^{n}
), the bottom horizontal map is a
!
-cover. Hence,
X^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow Z^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is prim if the same is true for
X\rightarrow Z
, but the latter is a finite étale map, thus in particular affine, whence prim.

Thus, we are done once we show that
(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{K}^{n})^{\mathrm{dR},+}\rightarrow K^{\mathrm{dR},+}
is prim. However, we have

(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{K}^{n})^{\mathrm{dR},+}\cong(\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{K}^{n}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}/\overline{\mathbb{T}})\;/\;\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle^{n}\;,

where
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle^{n}
acts on
\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{K}^{n}
by translation and thus the claim follows from the fact that both
\overline{\mathbb{D}}^{n}_{K}\rightarrow\GSpec K
and
*/\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}\langle-1\rangle^{n}\rightarrow*
are prim: Indeed, the first map is affine and for the second one this is
[
39
, Thm. 4.3.13]
.
∎

10.3.
Comparison theorems over
\mathbb{C}_{p}

Having introduced all the relevant stacks in the geometric situation, i.e. over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
, and clarified the relation between the filtered
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
-stack and classical
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology, we are now in a position to state and prove the analogues of the main comparison theorems from §§
7
,
8
and
9
. We start with the geometric form of the de Rham comparison theorem from
[
12
, Thm. 13.1]
.

Theorem 10.12
.

Let
X
be a smooth proper rigid space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. Then there is a natural isomorphism

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}\cong R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}

compatible with the filtrations.

Proof.

By
6.21
, the induced map
f^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}:X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
is cohomologically smooth and weakly cohomologically proper and thus the pushforward
E\coloneqq f^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}_{*}\mathcal{O}
is a perfect complex on
\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
by
[
31
, Lem. 4.5.16]
.

By proper base change and
10.8
, we know that, under the Rees equivalence from
2.23
, the pullback of
E
to

\mathbb{C}_{p,|u|=0}^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}=(\{|u|=0\}\subseteq\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle t\rangle_{\leq 1}\{u\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;\big/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}

identifies with a descendingly filtered perfect complex
\Fil^{\bullet}V
over
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
equipped with backwards maps
u:\Fil^{i}V\rightarrow\Fil^{i+1}V
such that
ut=\xi
whose base change to
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
is given by
\Fil^{\bullet}R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})
. Moreover, by
[
2
]
and using proper base change again, we also know that
E|_{Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
identifies with the perfect complex on
Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
obtained from
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by tensoring with
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
. In particular, we see that the pullback of
E
to
\{|u|=1,|t|=0\}
is given by
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
. As the pullback of
E
to

(\{|ut|=0\}\subseteq\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle u,t\rangle_{\leq 1}/(ut-\xi)\;\big/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}

is perfect, we conclude that

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\cong\sum_{i}\Fil^{i}R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})\otimes_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}}\xi^{-i}B_{\mathrm{dR}}\;,

from which the claim follows by inverting
\xi
.
∎

Let us now move on to adapting the results from §
8
. For this, we make the following definition:

Definition 10.13
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. The
relative mock Hyodo–Kato stack

X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
of
X
over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
is defined as the base change

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{HK}}}

As in
8.2
, one proves that there is an equivalence of categories

\Perf(X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HK}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})

for any Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. Moreover, we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{{\mathrm{HK},\mathrm{mock}}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\;,}

which hence induces realisation functors

T_{\mathrm{HK}}:\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\rightarrow\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HK}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})

from perfect analytic
F
-gauges on
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
to coefficients for Hyodo–Kato cohomology of
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
and

T_{\mathrm{dR},+}:\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}})

from analytic
F
-gauges on
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
to coefficients for filtered
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-cohomology of
X
. Postcomposing the latter functor with pullback to
X^{\mathrm{dR}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
, we obtain a functor

T_{\mathrm{dR}}:\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{dR}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}})\;.

Then the analogue of
8.3
in the geometric setting, i.e. for Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich spaces over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
, is the following:

Theorem 10.14
.

Let
X
be a Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. Then there is an equivalence of categories

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HK}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\times_{\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}})}\Perf(X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}})

induced by the realisation functors
T_{\mathrm{HK}}
and
T_{\mathrm{dR},+}
. In particular, for any
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})
, there is a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}},E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HK}/\mathbb{C}_{p}},T_{\mathrm{HK}}(E))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E))\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Proof.

Carries over almost verbatim from
8.3
with the use of
5.11
replaced by
10.15
below.
∎

The following lemma was used in the proof:

Lemma 10.15
.

Let
X
be a derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
admitting a map
X\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{n}
which is the composition of a finite étale map
X\rightarrow Z
and a rational localisation
Z\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}^{n}
. Then the Gelfand stack
X_{[r,s]}^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
is nicely coverable for any
[r,s]\subseteq(p^{1/2},p^{3/2})
.

Proof.

It suffices to do the case
X=\GSpec\mathbb{C}_{p}
, then the general case follows by the same argument as in
5.11
. However, we have

\mathbb{C}_{p,[r,s]}^{\mathrm{N}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}=(\Fil Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})_{[r,s]}=(\{ut=\varphi^{-1}(\xi)\}\subseteq Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p},[r,s]}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-})/\overline{\mathbb{T}}

and this is covered by the affine truncated Gelfand stack
(\{ut=\varphi^{-1}(\xi)\}\subseteq Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p},[r,s]}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-})
with the Čech nerve being

(\{ut=\varphi^{-1}(\xi)\}\subseteq Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p},[r,s]}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{+}\times\overline{\mathbb{D}}_{-})\times\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{\bullet}\;.

This proves the claim as
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is affine, static and flat over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by
2.15
.
∎

As in the arithmetic case, we want to make the conclusion of
10.14
explicit in the case
E=\mathcal{O}\{i\}
and expect that this compares to a more classical comparison theorem in the
p
-adic Hodge theory of rigid spaces over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. While the first guess one might have for what we obtain might be
[
17
, Thm. 1.3.(3)]
, this is actually not precisely what we get: This is due to the appearance of the period ring
B_{\mathrm{st}}^{+}
in loc. cit. while we will rather see
B_{\log}
appear. Hence, our result should more appropriately be compared with
[
15
, Thm. 7.1]
.

Corollary 10.16
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper qcqs rigid space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. For any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is a cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X/\mathbb{C}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}}B_{\log})^{\varphi=p^{i},N=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{i}R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

where
B_{\log}
is the period ring from
[
22
, Def. 10.3.1]
.

Proof.

Let us first say something about the bottom row. For this, note that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},\mathcal{O}\langle-i\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},\mathcal{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{i}R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})}

obtained from
10.8
is cartesian: Indeed, this amounts to checking that the induced map between the cofibres of the horizontal maps is an isomorphism, which in turn reduces to proving that

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Hod}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},\mathcal{O}\langle-i\rangle)\cong\gr^{i}R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})

by induction. However, this follows from
10.9
and
[
17
, Prop. 3.13]
.

Thus, we are done once we show that

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HK}/\mathbb{C}_{p}},\mathcal{O}\{i\})\cong(R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}}B_{\log})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{i}}\;.

For this, recall that
X^{\mathrm{HK}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
by definition sits in a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HK}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FF}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HK}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

and note that
X^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}
is prim by the same proof as in
2.45
. Thus, the above diagram satisfies base change and we obtain

f^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}\{i\}=f^{\prime}_{*}{g^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{O}\{i\}\cong g^{*}f_{*}\mathcal{O}\{i\}\;.

As
f_{*}\mathcal{O}\{i\}
identifies with a twist of the
(\varphi,N)
-module
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(X)
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}
via the equivalence on perfect complexes induced by pullback along the map

\Psi:\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\mathrm{HK}}/\,\mathbb{G}_{a}\{-1\}\cong\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}/\,\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\varphi{Z}

from
[
1
, Thm. 7.1.1]
, understanding
g^{*}f_{*}\mathcal{O}(i)
, amounts to understanding pullback along the map

\Psi\circ g:\mathrm{FF}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\GSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}/\,\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\varphi{Z}\;.

However, by the construction in loc. cit., the
\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\varphi{Z}
-bundle over
\mathrm{FF}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
this map classifies is just the pullback of the Fargues–Fontaine surface from
[
22
, Def. 10.3.7]
along
Y_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathrm{FF}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
and hence the claim follows from the fact that the global sections of this bundle are given by
B_{\log}
.
∎

Remark 10.17
.

The above result shows that
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X/\mathbb{C}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))
is
not
the same as the geometric syntomic cohomology of Colmez–Nizioł from
[
17
]
. Indeed, this is because, for their geometric syntomic cohomology
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{CN}}(X/\mathbb{C}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))
, we have a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn},\mathrm{CN}}(X/\mathbb{C}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}}B_{\mathrm{st}}^{+})^{\varphi=p^{i},N=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{i}R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}

see
[
17
, Thm. 1.3]
, and replacing
B_{\mathrm{st}}^{+}
by
B_{\log}
here
does
in general change the
(\varphi=p^{i},N=0)
-eigenspace. Indeed, the result above rather shows that our geometric syntomic cohomology
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X/\mathbb{C}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))
recovers Bosco’s
syntomic Fargues–Fontaine cohomology
from
[
15
, §6]
by
[
15
, Thm.s 4.1, 6.3]
and, by
[
15
, Ex. 6.36]
, this already differs from Colmez–Nizioł geometric syntomic cohomology in the case of the projective line.
∎

Finally, let us discuss the analogue of the comparison with proétale cohomology from §
9
.

Definition 10.18
.

Let
X
be a Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. The Gelfand stack
X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
is defined as the base change

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}}

Again, there is an equivalence of categories

\Perf(X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Div}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})

for any Gelfand stack over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
and we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{{\mathrm{Div}^{1},\mathrm{mock}}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\;,}

which hence induces realisation functors

T_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}:\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\rightarrow\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})

from perfect analytic
F
-gauges on
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
to perfect complexes on
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
, which according to
[
2
]
should be thought of as coefficients for proétale cohomology of
X
, and

T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}:\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}})

from analytic
F
-gauges on
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
to quasicoherent sheaves on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
. Postcomposing the latter functor with pullback to
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
, we also obtain a functor

T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}:\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\;.

This lets us formulate the analogue of
9.3
in the geometric setting.

Theorem 10.19
.

Let
X
be a Berkovich smooth derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. Then there is an equivalence of categories

\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\cong\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})\times_{\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger/\mathbb{C}_{p}})}\Perf(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}})

induced by the realisation functors
T_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}
and
T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}
. In particular, for any
E\in\Perf(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})
, there is a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}},E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}/\mathbb{C}_{p}},T_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}(E))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}},T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}(E))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger/\mathbb{C}_{p}},T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}(E))\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;.}}

Proof.

As in
9.3
, replacing the use of
5.11
by
10.15
.
∎

To control the cofibre of the map

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}},T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+}(E))\rightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger/\mathbb{C}_{p}},T_{\mathrm{HT},\dagger}(E))

and hence deduce an honest comparison theorem between our geometric syntomic cohomology and proétale cohomology in a suitable range, we again have to understand
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
more explicitly, at least locally on
X
. Namely, we need the following analogue of
5.14
,
5.15
and
5.17
, which is proved in the same way:

Proposition 10.20
.

Let
X
be a derived Berkovich space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
and assume that
X
admits a lift
\widetilde{X}
to
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}
equipped with a Berkovich étale map
\widetilde{X}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}_{B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}
for some
n\geq 0
.

(1)

There is an isomorphism

X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}}\cong\left(\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}\times_{\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}\GSpec B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}\langle u\rangle_{\leq 1}\{t\}^{\dagger}/(ut-\xi)\;/\;\overline{\mathbb{T}}\right)\,\big/\,(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}})^{n}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}\;,

where the pushout is taken along the map
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}\rightarrow(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}
given by multiplication by
u
and
\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
denotes the base change of
\widetilde{X}
along
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n,\mathrm{la}}_{\infty}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n}
. Here, the action of
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{la}})^{n}\coprod^{\mathrm{AbGrp}}_{(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger})^{n}
on
\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
is obtained by pulling back the action on
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n,\mathrm{la}}_{\infty}
given by

(\underline{\theta},\underline{v}).(\underline{x}^{1/p^{m}})_{m}\coloneqq\left([\varepsilon]^{\underline{\theta}/p^{m}}\exp\left(\frac{t^{\prime}\underline{v}}{p^{m}}\right)\underline{x}^{1/p^{m}}\right)_{m}\;,

where
(\underline{x}^{1/p^{m}})_{m}
are the coordinates on
\overline{\mathbb{T}}^{n,\mathrm{la}}_{\infty}
and
t^{\prime}\coloneqq t\cdot\log[\varepsilon]/\xi
.

(2)

A perfect complex on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
amounts to the following data:

(i)

A diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{i-1}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{i}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{i+1}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

of perfect complexes over
\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
such that
ut=\xi
with the property that
t:\Fil_{\bullet}V\rightarrow\Fil_{\bullet-1}V
becomes an isomorphism for
\bullet\ll 0
and
u:\Fil_{\bullet}V\rightarrow\Fil_{\bullet+1}V
becomes an isomorphism for
\bullet\gg 0
;

(ii)

a
t
-connection
\nabla:V\rightarrow V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}^{1}
on
V
, i.e. a map satisfying the
t
-deformed Leibniz rule
\nabla(fm)=f\nabla(m)+t\cdot\mathrm{d}f
, whose restriction to
\Fil_{i}V
is equipped with a factorisation through
\Fil_{i-1}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}^{1}
;

(iii)

a semilinear locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
-action on
\Fil_{\bullet}V
commuting with all the previous data;

(iv)

an identification between the “geometric Sen operator”
\Theta^{\mathrm{geom}}:\Fil_{i}V\rightarrow\Fil_{i}V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}^{1}
coming from the Lie algebra action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
and the
t
-connection
u\nabla
for each
i
.

Under this identification, restriction to
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
corresponds to forgetting the
u
-filtration.

(3)

Under the above identifications, the cohomology of a perfect complex on
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger,+/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
is computed by the (underived)
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
-invariants of the cohomology on
\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
of the total complex of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{0}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{-1}V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil_{-2}V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}

while the cohomology of its restriction to
X^{\mathrm{HT},\dagger/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
is given by the (underived)
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{n}
-invariants of the cohomology on
\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}
of the total complex of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{la}}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\;,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}

where
V
denotes the underlying unfiltered object of
\Fil_{\bullet}V
.

Proof.

As in
5.14
,
5.15
and
5.17
.
∎

Using
10.20
and that cohomology on
X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}/\mathbb{C}_{p}}
computes proétale cohomology of
X
by
[
2
]
if
X
is a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
, we deduce the following explicit comparison theorem from
10.19
:

Theorem 10.21
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. If
E\in\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})
is a vector bundle analytic
F
-gauge on
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
with Hodge–Tate weights all at most
-i
for some
i\geq 0
, then the natural morphism

R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}},E)\rightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}/\mathbb{C}_{p}},T_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}(E))

is an isomorphism on
\tau^{\leq i}
and induces an injection on
H^{i+1}
. In particular, for
E=\mathcal{O}\{i\}
, we obtain

\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{Syn}}(X/\mathbb{C}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))\cong\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))\;.

Proof.

As in
9.4
.
∎

Finally, we can again combine the results of
10.14
and
10.21
to obtain a version of Colmez–Nizioł’s “basic comparison theorem” in the geometric case, see
[
17
, Thm. 1.1]
, with vector bundle
F
-gauge coefficients. However, as already pointed out above, our version differs from the one in loc. cit. due to the appearance of the period ring
B_{\log}
in place of
B_{\mathrm{st}}^{+}
and should thus rather be compared to the result of Bosco from
[
15
, Thm. 7.2]
.

Corollary 10.22
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper rigid space over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. If
E\in\Vect(X^{\mathrm{Syn}/\mathbb{C}_{p}})
is a vector bundle analytic
F
-gauge on
X
relative to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
with Hodge–Tate weights all at most
-i
for some
i\geq 0
, then there is a natural map

\begin{split}\fib(R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{HK}/\mathbb{C}_{p}},T_{\mathrm{HK}}(E))\rightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR}/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},T_{\mathrm{dR}}(E))/R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{dR},+/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+,\dagger}},&T_{\mathrm{dR},+}(E)))\\
&\rightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\mathrm{Div}^{1}},T_{\mathrm{Div}^{1}}(E))\end{split}

which induces an isomorphism on
\tau^{\leq i}
and an injection on
H^{i+1}
. In particular, if
X
is qcqs and
E=\mathcal{O}\{i\}
, we obtain

\tau^{\leq i}R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{p}(i))\cong\tau^{\leq i}((R\Gamma_{\mathrm{HK}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{un}}}B_{\log})^{\varphi=p^{i},N=0}\rightarrow R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})/\Fil^{i}R\Gamma(X/B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}))\;.

Proof.

The first part follows by combining
10.14
and
10.21
while the second part then follows using
10.16
.
∎
